“Just so—forgery,” assented Harker. “And the signature that he forged was—the Duke of Saxonsteade’s! As a matter of fact, he was the Duke’s London estate agent. He got wrong, somehow, and he forged the Duke’s name to a cheque. Now, then, considering who Glassdale is, and that he was certainly a fellow-convict of Brake’s, and that I myself saw him here in Wrychester on the day of Brake’s death—what’s the conclusion to be drawn? That Brake wanted to see the Duke on some business of Glassdale’s! Without a doubt! It may have been that he and Glassdale wanted to visit the Duke, together.”

===Chinese===
“你方才说的——是伪造罪？”布赖斯应道。

“正是——伪造罪，”哈克附和道，“而他伪造的那个签名，正是——萨克森斯蒂德公爵的！说起来，他本是公爵在伦敦的地产代理人。不知怎的他走了歪路，假冒公爵的名字签了一张支票。那么，考虑到格拉斯代尔这号人物，又考虑到他肯定是布雷克的狱友，再加上布雷克横死那天我亲眼在赖彻斯特、在这里见到了他——能得出什么结论呢？那就是：布雷克想见公爵，为的是格拉斯代尔的某桩事！毫无疑问！说不定他和格拉斯代尔本打算一道去拜访公爵呢。”

===Original===
Bryce silently considered this suggestion for awhile.

“You said, just now, that Glassdale could be traced?” he remarked at last.

“Traced—yes,” replied Harker. “So long as he’s in England.”

“Why not set about it?” suggested Bryce.

“Not yet,” said Harker. “There’s things to do before that. And the first thing is—let’s get to know what the mystery of that scrap of paper is. You say you’ve found Richard Jenkins’s tomb? Very well—then the thing to do is to find out if anything is hidden there. Try it tomorrow night. Better go by yourself—after dark. If you find anything, let me know. And then—then we can decide on a next step. But between now and then, there’ll be the inquest on this man Collishaw. And, about that—a word in your ear! Say as little as ever you can!—after all, you know nothing beyond what you saw. And—we mustn’t meet and talk in public—after you’ve done that bit of exploring in Paradise tomorrow night, come round here and we’ll consider matters.”

===Chinese===
布赖斯默默地琢磨了这个推测一阵子。

“你刚才说，格拉斯代尔是可以追查得到下落的？”他终于开口道。

“追查——是的，”哈克答道，“只要他人还在英格兰。”

“那何不着手去查？”布赖斯提议。

“还不行，”哈克说，“在那之前还有别的事要做。头一件就是——咱们得先弄清楚那张小纸片儿到底藏着什么秘密。你说你找到了理查德·詹金斯的那座坟？很好——那么接下来要做的，就是查清楚那儿是不是藏着什么东西。明儿夜里去试试。最好你一个人去——等天黑以后。要是发现了什么，就告诉我。到那时候——到那时候咱们再定下一步。不过从现在到那时候，还要开那个科利肖的验尸聆讯。关于这件事——我跟你说句私话！你能少说就尽量少说！——毕竟除了你亲眼所见的，你什么都不知道。还有——咱们不能在公开场合会面、交谈——明儿夜里你在「乐园」探查完了之后，就到我这儿来，咱们再合计。”

===Original===
There was little that Bryce could say or could be asked to say at the inquest on the mason’s labourer next morning. Public interest and excitement was as keen about Collishaw’s mysterious death as about Braden’s, for it was already rumoured through the town that if Braden had not met with his death when he came to Wrychester, Collishaw would still be alive. The Coroner’s court was once more packed; once more there was the same atmosphere of mystery. But the proceedings were of a very different nature to those which had attended the inquest on Braden. The foreman under whose orders Collishaw had been working gave particulars of the dead man’s work on the morning of his death. He had been instructed to clear away an accumulation of rubbish which had gathered at the foot of the south wall of the nave in consequence of some recent repairs to the masonry—there was a full day’s work before him. All day he would be in and out of Paradise with his barrow, wheeling away the rubbish he gathered up. The foreman had looked in on him once or twice; he had seen him just before noon, when he appeared to be in his usual health—he had made no complaint, at any rate. Asked if he had happened to notice where Collishaw had set down his dinner basket and his tin bottle while he worked, he replied that it so happened that he had—he remembered seeing both bottle and basket and the man’s jacket deposited on one of the box-tombs under a certain yew-tree—which he could point out, if necessary.

===Chinese===
第二天上午，在那位石匠帮工的验尸聆讯上，布赖斯没有什么可说的，也没有什么会被要求说的。公众对科利肖神秘之死的关切与亢奋，一如对布雷登之死那般强烈；因为城里早已传开了一种说法：倘若布雷登来到赖彻斯特时没有横遭不测，科利肖原本还会活着。验尸官的法庭又一次挤得水泄不通；又一次弥漫着同样神秘的气氛。然而这一回的程序，与审理布雷登案时大不相同。科利肖生前在其手下干活的那个工头，交代了死者遇害那天上午的活计情况。他奉命清理中殿南墙根一带因近日修缮石作而堆积起来的一堆废料——这够他干上一整天的。他一整天都要推着独轮车在「乐园」里进进出出，把收拾起来的废料一车车运走。工头中途去看过他一两回；将近正午时还见过他，当时他看上去身体如常——至少没听他有一句怨言。被问及是否碰巧留意到科利肖干活时把饭篮和锡瓶搁在了哪儿，他回答说，巧得很，他留意到了——他记得清清楚楚，看见那只瓶子和饭篮，还有那人的外套，都搁在某棵紫杉树底下的一座箱形石墓上——必要的话，他可以指出来是哪一座。

===Original===
Bryce’s account of his finding of Collishaw amounted to no more than a bare recital of facts. Nor was much time spent in questioning the two doctors who had conducted the postmortem examination. Their evidence, terse and particular, referred solely to the cause of death. The man had been poisoned by a dose of hydrocyanic acid, which, in their opinion, had been taken only a few minutes before his body was discovered by Dr. Bryce. It had probably been a dose which would cause instantaneous death. There were no traces of the poison in the remains of his dinner, nor in the liquid in his tin bottle, which was old tea. But of the cause of his sudden death there was no more doubt than of the effects. Ransford had been in the court from the outset of the proceedings, and when the medical evidence had been given he was called. Bryce, watching him narrowly, saw that he was suffering from repressed excitement—and that that excitement was as much due to anger as to anything else. His face was set and stern, and he looked at the Coroner with an expression which portended something not precisely clear at that moment. Bryce, trying to analyse it, said to himself that he shouldn’t be surprised if a scene followed—Ransford looked like a man who is bursting to say something in no unmistakable fashion. But at first he answered the questions put to him calmly and decisively.

===Chinese===
布赖斯对发现科利肖经过的陈述，无非是把事实干巴巴地复述了一遍。对那两位主持尸检的医生，也没花多少时间去盘问。他们的证词简明而具体，只涉及死因。死者是因一剂氢氰酸（hydrocyanic acid）中毒身亡的；据他们判断，这剂毒药是在布赖斯医生发现尸体之前几分钟才服下的。那很可能是服下后立时致命的一剂。在他吃剩的饭菜里、在他那只锡瓶里所装的陈茶中，都找不到毒药的痕迹。但他猝然毙命的原因，却与其效果一样不容置疑。兰斯福德从一开始就坐在法庭里；待医学证词供毕，他便被传唤。布赖斯紧紧盯着他，看出他正按捺着一股激动——而那激动之中，怒气至少不比别的成分少。他面色凝重而冷峻，望着验尸官的神情预示着某种此时还不甚分明的事态。布赖斯暗自揣度，心说要是接下来不出一场风波那才怪呢——兰斯福德那副模样，活脱脱是一个憋着一肚子话、非要用最不含糊的方式一吐为快的人。不过起初，他回答那些向他提出的问题时，倒还算镇定果断。

===Original===
“When this man’s clothing was searched,” observed the Coroner, “a box of pills was found, Dr. Ransford, on which your writing appears. Had you been attending him—professionally?”

“Yes,” replied Ransford. “Both Collishaw and his wife. Or, rather, to be exact, I had been in attendance on the wife, for some weeks. A day or two before his death, Collishaw complained to me of indigestion, following on his meals. I gave him some digestive pills—the pills you speak of, no doubt.”

“These?” asked the Coroner, passing over the box which Mitchington had found.

“Precisely!” agreed Ransford. “That, at any rate, is the box, and I suppose those to be the pills.”

“You made them up yourself?” inquired the Coroner.

===Chinese===
“搜查这个人的衣物时，”验尸官说道，“发现了一盒药丸，兰斯福德医生，上头有您的笔迹。您此前可曾——以医生的身份——为他诊治过？”

“是的，”兰斯福德答道，“科利肖和他妻子我都看过。或者更确切地说，我照看过他妻子，已有几个星期了。他死前一两日，科利肖向我诉说饭后肠胃不消化。我给了他一些助消化的药丸——想必就是您说的那些药丸。”

“就是这些？”验尸官问着，把米钦顿找到的那只药盒递了过去。

“正是！”兰斯福德应道，“无论如何，盒子就是这只，我想那些也就是那些药丸了。”

“是您亲手配制的？”验尸官问。

===Original===
“I did—I dispense all my own medicines.”

“Is it possible that the poison we have heard of, just now, could get into one of those pills—by accident?”

“Utterly impossible!—under my hands, at any rate,” answered Ransford.

“Still, I suppose, it could have been administered in a pill?” suggested the Coroner.

“It might,” agreed Ransford. “But,” he added, with a significant glance at the medical men who had just given evidence. “It was not so administered in this case, as the previous witnesses very well know!”

===Chinese===
“是我——我所有的药都自行调配。”

“我们方才听说的那种毒药，有没有可能——出于意外——混进其中一颗药丸里去？”

“绝无可能！——无论如何，经我手的药绝无可能，”兰斯福德答道。

“可我想，毒药总还是可以掺在一颗药丸里下的吧？”验尸官提示道。

“倒也可以，”兰斯福德承认道，“可是，”他向方才作证的那两位医生意味深长地瞥了一眼，接着说，“在这一案里，毒药并不是这么下的，方才那几位证人再清楚不过！”

===Original===
The Coroner looked round him, and waited a moment.

“You are at liberty to explain—that last remark,” he said at last. “That is—if you wish to do so.”

“Certainly!” answered Ransford, with alacrity. “Those pills are, as you will observe, coated, and the man would swallow them whole—immediately after his food. Now, it would take some little time for a pill to dissolve, to disintegrate, to be digested. If Collishaw took one of my pills as soon as he had eaten his dinner, according to instructions, and if poison had been in that pill, he would not have died at once—as he evidently did. Death would probably have been delayed some little time until the pill had dissolved. But, according to the evidence you have had before you, he died quite suddenly while eating his dinner—or immediately after it. I am not legally represented here—I don’t consider it at all necessary—but I ask you to recall Dr. Coates and to put this question to him: Did he find one of those digestive pills in this man’s stomach?”

===Chinese===
验尸官环顾四周，等了片刻。

“您尽可以解释——最后那句话，”他终于说道，“前提是——您愿意的话。”

“当然愿意！”兰斯福德爽快地答道，“各位都看到了，那些药丸是包着糖衣的，那人会把整颗吞下去——一吃完饭就吞。而一颗药丸要溶开、崩解、被消化掉，总得花上一阵子工夫。假如科利肖照吩咐，一吃完午饭便服下我的一颗药丸，又假如那颗药丸里藏着毒药，他绝不会当场毙命——可他显然是当场毙命的。死亡多半会推迟一些时候，直到药丸溶开为止。然而，根据你们已经听到的证词，他是在吃饭当中——或刚吃完之后——猝然倒毙的。我在这里并没有律师代表——我也认为完全没有必要——但我请求你们把科茨医生（Dr. Coates）重新传来，问他这样一个问题：他在这人的胃里，有没有找到过一颗那样的助消化药丸？”

===Original===
The Coroner turned, somewhat dubiously, to the two doctors who had performed the autopsy. But before he could speak, the superintendent of police rose and began to whisper to him, and after a conversation between them, he looked round at the jury, every member of which had evidently been much struck by Ransford’s suggestion.

“At this stage,” he said, “it will be necessary to adjourn. I shall adjourn the inquiry for a week, gentlemen. You will—” Ransford, still standing in the witness-box, suddenly lost control of himself. He uttered a sharp exclamation and smote the ledge before him smartly with his open hand.

“I protest against that!” he said vehemently. “Emphatically, I protest! You first of all make a suggestion which tells against me—then, when I demand that a question shall be put which is of immense importance to my interests, you close down the inquiry—even if only for the moment. That is grossly unfair and unjust!”

===Chinese===
验尸官将信将疑地转向那两位主持解剖的医生。可他还没来得及开口，警察局长便站起身来，附耳对他低语起来；两人交谈了一番之后，他才环视陪审团——每一个陪审团员显然都被兰斯福德的提议打动了。

“在这个阶段，”他说，“必须休庭了。我要把聆讯延后一星期，各位先生。诸位将——”兰斯福德仍站在证人席上，忽然按捺不住了。他短促地喝了一声，抬手在面前的栏杆上狠狠拍了一掌。

“我对此提出抗议！”他激烈地说，“我郑重提出抗议！你们先是抛出一个对我不利的暗示——接着，当我要求提出一个对我的利害至关重大的问题时，你们却又把聆讯收起来——哪怕只是暂时收起。这太不公平、太不公道了！”

===Original===
“You are mistaken,” said the Coroner. “At the adjourned inquiry, the two medical men can be recalled, and you will have the opportunity—or your solicitor will have—of asking any questions you like for the present—”

“For the present you have me under suspicion!” interrupted Ransford hotly. “You know it—I say this with due respect to your office—as well as I do. Suspicion is rife in the city against me. Rumour is being spread—secretly—and, I am certain—from the police, who ought to know better. And—I will not be silenced, Mr. Coroner!—I take this public opportunity, as I am on oath, of saying that I know nothing whatever of the causes of the deaths of either Collishaw or of Braden—upon my solemn oath!”

“The inquest is adjourned to this day week,” said the Coroner quietly.

===Chinese===
“您误会了，”验尸官说，“在续审的时候，那两位医生可以重新被传唤，您将有机会——或者您的律师将有机会——提出您想问的任何问题，眼下——”

“眼下你们已经把我当作嫌疑人了！”兰斯福德怒不可遏地打断他，“这一点你们心知肚明——我对您说这话，是对您的职位怀着应有敬意的——跟我一样清楚。城里对我猜疑四起。流言正在暗暗传播——而且我敢肯定——是从警察那边传出来的，他们本不该这么糊涂。而且——我绝不被噤声，验尸官先生！——我借着现在这个当众开口、又是在宣誓之下的机会，声明如下：无论科利肖之死，还是布雷登之死，其缘由我一概不知——我以庄严的誓言作保！”

“验尸聆讯延期至下周今日，”验尸官平静地说。

===Original===
Ransford suddenly stepped down from the witness-box and without word or glance at anyone there, walked with set face and determined look out of the court, and the excited spectators, gathering into groups, immediately began to discuss his vigorous outburst and to take sides for and against him.

Bryce, judging it advisable to keep away from Mitchington just then, and, for similar reasons, keeping away from Harker also, went out of the crowded building alone—to be joined in the street outside by Sackville Bonham, whom he had noticed in court, in company with his stepfather, Mr. Folliot.

Folliot, Bryce had observed, had stopped behind, exchanging some conversation with the Coroner. Sackville came up to Bryce with a knowing shake of the hand. He was one of those very young men who have a habit of suggesting that their fund of knowledge is extensive and peculiar, and Bryce waited for a manifestation.

===Chinese===
兰斯福德蓦地从证人席上走下来，对在场的任何人不发一言、也不看一眼，板着脸、神情决然地走出了法庭。激动的旁听者们随即三三两两聚成一团，立刻议论起他这番激烈的爆发，各执一词，有替他说话的，也有反对他的。

布赖斯这时觉得最好先避开米钦顿，出于同样的缘故，也暂且躲着哈克，便独自走出了那栋挤满人的房子——到了外面街上，萨克维尔·博纳姆却凑了上来；他在法庭里早就注意到此人，当时他正陪着他的继父福利奥特先生。

布赖斯留意到，福利奥特留在了后头，正和验尸官攀谈。萨克维尔走上前来，煞有介事地同布赖斯握了握手。他是那种年纪轻轻、却惯于给人一种“自己见多识广、所知甚奇”印象的小伙子，布赖斯便等着他露那一手。

===Original===
“Queer business, all that, Bryce!” observed Sackville confidentially. “Of course, Ransford is a perfect ass!”

“Think so?” remarked Bryce, with an inflection which suggested that Sackville’s opinion on anything was as valuable as the Attorney-General’s. “That’s how it strikes you, is it?”

“Impossible that it could strike one in any other way, you know,” answered Sackville with fine and lofty superiority. “Ransford should have taken immediate steps to clear himself of any suspicion. It’s ridiculous, considering his position—guardian to—to Miss Bewery, for instance—that he should allow such rumours to circulate. By God, sir, if it had been me, I’d have stopped ’em!—before they left the parish pump!”

“Ah?” said Bryce. “And—how?”

===Chinese===
“这一摊子事，真够古怪的，布赖斯！”萨克维尔一副交心的口吻说道，“当然了，兰斯福德是个十足的蠢驴！”

“你这么看？”布赖斯说道，语气里带着一种暗示——仿佛萨克维尔对任何事的见解，都和总检察长（Attorney-General）的见解一样金贵。“你就是这样觉得的，是吗？”

“不可能有别的看法啊，你明白，”萨克维尔答道，一副漂亮而高人一等的派头。“兰斯福德本该立刻采取措施，把自己从一切嫌疑里摘出来。想想他的身份——比如说，是——是比尤里小姐的监护人——竟任凭这种流言四处传播，简直荒唐。老天作证，先生，要是换了我，我早就堵住它们了！——早在它们还在教区水井边嚼舌头的时候！”

“哦？”布赖斯说，“那——怎么个堵法？”

===Original===
“Made an example of somebody,” replied Sackville, with emphasis. “I believe there’s law in this country, isn’t there?—law against libel and slander, and that sort of thing, eh? Oh, yes!”

“Not been much time for that—yet,” remarked Bryce.

“Piles of time,” retorted Sackville, swinging his stick vigorously. “No, sir, Ransford is an ass! However, if a man won’t do things for himself, well, his friends must do something for him. Ransford, of course, must be pulled—dragged!—out of this infernal hole. Of course he’s suspected! But my stepfather—he’s going to take a hand. And my stepfather, Bryce, is a devilish cute old hand at a game of this sort!”

===Chinese===
“杀一儆百，拿某个人开刀，”萨克维尔加重语气说道，“我相信咱们这个国家是有法律的，不是吗？——有反诽谤、反污蔑那一类的法，嗯？哦，没错！”

“到现在为止——还没多少工夫顾得上这个呢，”布赖斯说道。

“工夫多的是，”萨克维尔反驳道，把手杖甩得虎虎生风。“不，先生，兰斯福德就是头蠢驴！不过嘛，一个人要是不肯为自己出力，那他的朋友们总得替他做点什么。兰斯福德嘛，当然得被人拉——硬拽！——从这个该死的窟窿里拽出来。他当然被人怀疑啦！可我的继父——他要插手管这件事了。我跟你说，布赖斯，我继父办起这一类事来，可是个精得要命的老手！”

===Original===
“Nobody doubts Mr. Folliot’s abilities, I’m sure,” said Bryce. “But—you don’t mind saying—how is he going to take a hand?”

“Stir things towards a clearing-up,” announced Sackville promptly. “Have the whole thing gone into—thoroughly. There are matters that haven’t been touched on, yet. You’ll see, my boy!”

“Glad to hear it,” said Bryce. “But—why should Mr. Folliot be so particular about clearing Ransford?”

===Chinese===
“我相信，没有人会怀疑福利奥特先生的能耐，”布赖斯说，“不过——您不介意说说吧——他打算怎么个插手法？”

“搅动搅动，好叫事情水落石出，”萨克维尔立刻宣布道，“要把整桩事查个底朝天——彻彻底底地查。有些事至今还没人提到过呢。你等着瞧吧，老兄！”

“听你这么说我很高兴，”布赖斯说，“可是——福利奥特先生为什么偏偏这么上心，非得替兰斯福德洗清嫌疑不可呢？”

===Original===
Sackville swung his stick, and pulled up his collar, and jerked his nose a trifle higher.

“Oh, well,” he said. “Of course, it’s—it’s a pretty well understood thing, don’t you know—between myself and Miss Bewery, you know—and of course, we couldn’t have any suspicions attaching to her guardian, could we, now? Family interest, don’t you know—Caesar’s wife, and all that sort of thing, eh?”

“I see,” answered Bryce, quietly—“sort of family arrangement. With Ransford’s consent and knowledge, of course?”

===Chinese===
萨克维尔甩了甩手杖，把衣领竖了起来，又把鼻子微微朝上翘了翘。

“哦，这个嘛，”他说，“当然了，这——这是一件大家心照不宣的事，你明白的——就是我和比尤里小姐之间，你知道——那么当然喽，总不能让她监护人身上沾着什么嫌疑，对不对？一家子的利害，你明白的——凯撒之妻，那一类的讲究，嗯？”

“我明白了，”布赖斯不动声色地答道，“算是某种家事上的安排。当然是经过兰斯福德同意和知情的吧？”

===Original===
“Ransford won’t even be consulted,” said Sackville, airily. “My stepfather—sharp man, that, Bryce!—he’ll do things in his own fashion. You look out for sudden revelations!”

“I will,” replied Bryce. “Bye-bye!”

===Chinese===
“连问都不会问兰斯福德一声，”萨克维尔轻描淡写地说，“我继父——那可是个厉害角色，布赖斯！——他凡事自有他自己的章法。你等着突如其来的真相大白吧！”

“我会留意的，”布赖斯答道，“再会！”

===Original===
He turned off to his rooms, wondering how much of truth there was in the fatuous Sackville’s remarks. And—was there some mystery still undreamt of by himself and Harker? There might be—he was still under the influence of Ransford’s indignant and dramatic assertion of his innocence. Would Ransford have allowed himself an outburst of that sort if he had not been, as he said, utterly ignorant of the immediate cause of Braden’s death? Now Bryce, all through, was calculating, for his own purposes, on Ransford’s share, full or partial, in that death—if Ransford really knew nothing whatever about it, where did his, Bryce’s theory, come in—and how would his present machinations result? And, more—if Ransford’s assertion were true, and if Varner’s story of the hand, seen for an instant in the archway, were also true—and Varner was persisting in it—then, who was the man who flung Braden to his death that morning? He realized that, instead of straightening out, things were becoming more and more complicated.

But he realized something else. On the surface, there was a strong case of suspicion against Ransford. It had been suggested that very morning before a coroner and his jury; it would grow; the police were already permeated with suspicion and distrust. Would it not pay him, Bryce, to encourage, to help it? He had his own score to pay off against Ransford; he had his own schemes as regards Mary Bewery. Anyway, he was not going to share in any attempts to clear the man who had bundled him out of his house unceremoniously—he would bide his time. And in the meantime there were other things to be done—one of them that very night.

===Chinese===
他转身朝自己的住处走去，一路揣摩着这番出自那个蠢不可及的萨克维尔的话里，究竟有几分是真。还有——是不是还有一层他自己和哈克都做梦也没想到的隐情？也许有的——他仍处在兰斯福德那番义愤填膺、颇具戏剧性地为自己申辩无辜的余波之下。倘若兰斯福德果真如他所言，对布雷登之死的直接缘由一无所知，他还会任由自己发作那样一通吗？须知布赖斯从头到尾都在为着自家的盘算，把兰斯福德在那桩命案里——无论全部还是部分——的干系算计在内；如果兰斯福德当真对此一无所知，那他布赖斯的那套理论又该往哪儿摆——而他眼下这一连串的暗中布局，又将落得什么下场？再者——如果兰斯福德的申辩属实，如果瓦纳所说那只一闪而过、在拱道里出现过的手的故事也属实——而瓦纳仍一口咬定不松口——那么，那天早晨把布雷登摔下去害死的人，究竟是谁？他意识到，事情非但没有理顺，反而越发纠缠复杂了。

可他也意识到了另一件事。表面上看，对兰斯福德的嫌疑颇为有力。这一点，就在那天上午、在验尸官和他的陪审团面前，已被点了出来；它还会滋长；警察那边早已被猜疑和不信任浸透了。那么，他布赖斯若是去推波助澜、甚至助它一臂之力，岂不是正中下怀？他自有一笔要跟兰斯福德算的账；他自有一套关于玛丽·比尤里的图谋。无论如何，他是绝不会参与任何替那人洗刷嫌疑的举动的——正是那人毫不客气地把他从自家屋子里撵了出去——他要静待时机。而在此期间，还有别的事要做——其中一件就在今夜。

===Original===
But before Bryce could engage in his secret task of excavating a small portion of Paradise in the rear of Richard Jenkins’s tomb, another strange development came. As the dark fell over the old city that night and he was thinking of setting out on his mission, Mitchington came in, carrying two sheets of paper, obviously damp from the press, in his hand. He looked at Bryce with an expression of wonder.

“Here’s a queer go!” he said. “I can’t make this out at all! Look at these big handbills—but perhaps you’ve seen ’em? They’re being posted all over the city—we’ve had a bundle of ’em thrown in on us.”

“I haven’t been out since lunch,” remarked Bryce. “What are they?”

===Chinese===
然而，还没等布赖斯着手执行他那个秘密任务——在理查德·詹金斯墓后头的那一块「乐园」里刨开一小片土地——又冒出了一桩离奇的变故。那天夜里，当暮色笼罩住这座古城、他正打算动身去办差的时候，米钦顿走了进来，手里拿着两张纸，显然刚从印刷机上取下来，还透着湿气。他带着一脸惊奇望着布赖斯。

“这事儿真怪！”他说，“我怎么也琢磨不透！您瞧瞧这些大张的招贴——不过也许您已经见过了？它们正被张贴得满城都是——有人朝我们这儿也扔进了一捆。”

“午饭以后我就没出过门，”布赖斯说，“是什么东西？”

===Original===
Mitchington spread out the two papers on the table, pointing from one to the other.

“You see?” he said. “Five Hundred Pounds Reward!—One Thousand Pounds Reward! And—both out at the same time, from different sources!”

“What sources?” asked Bryce, bending over the bills. “Ah—I see. One signed by Phipps & Maynard, the other by Beachcroft. Odd, certainly!”

“Odd?” exclaimed Mitchington. “I should think so! But, do you see, doctor? that one—five hundred reward—is offered for information of any nature relative to the deaths of John Braden and James Collishaw, both or either. That amount will be paid for satisfactory information by Phipps & Maynard. And Phipps & Maynard are Ransford’s solicitors! That bill, sir, comes from him! And now the other, the thousand pound one, that offers the reward to anyone who can give definite information as to the circumstances attending the death of John Braden—to be paid by Mr. Beachcroft. And he’s Mr. Folliot’s solicitor! So—that comes from Mr. Folliot. What has he to do with it? And are these two putting their heads together—or are these bills quite independent of each other? Hang me if I understand it!”

===Chinese===
米钦顿把那两张纸摊在桌上，从这一张指到那一张。

“您瞧见了吧？”他说，“悬赏五百镑！——悬赏一千镑！而且——两份同时贴出来，还来自不同的出处！”

“什么出处？”布赖斯俯身看着那两张招贴问道，“哦——我看见了。一份由菲普斯与梅纳德（Phipps & Maynard）联名签署，另一份署名比奇克罗夫特（Beachcroft）。确实古怪！”

“古怪？”米钦顿失声道，“我看岂止古怪！可是，医生，您看出来了吗？那一份——五百镑悬赏——是为任何有关约翰·布雷登与詹姆斯·科利肖之死（无论两者或其一）的线索而设的。凡能提供令人满意的线索者，可由菲普斯与梅纳德支付该笔赏金。而菲普斯与梅纳德正是兰斯福德的律师！先生，那张招贴就是从他那儿来的！再看另一份，一千镑的那一份，悬赏给任何能就约翰·布雷登之死的相关情形提供确凿消息的人——赏金由比奇克罗夫特先生支付。而他是福利奥特先生的律师！这么说来——那一张是从福利奥特先生那边来的。他跟这事有什么相干？这两个人是不是在碰头合计——还是说这两张招贴彼此全不相干？真见鬼，我可搞不懂！”

===Original===
Bryce read and reread the contents of the two bills. And then he thought for awhile before speaking.

“Well,” he said at last, “there’s probably this in it—the Folliots are very wealthy people. Mrs. Folliot, it’s pretty well known, wants her son to marry Miss Bewery—Dr. Ransford’s ward. Probably she doesn’t wish any suspicion to hang over the family. That’s all I can suggest. In the other case, Ransford wants to clear himself. For don’t forget this, Mitchington!—somewhere, somebody may know something! Only something. But that something might clear Ransford of the suspicion that’s undoubtedly been cast upon him. If you’re thinking to get a strong case against Ransford, you’ve got your work set. He gave your theory a nasty knock this morning by his few words about that pill. Did Coates and Everest find a pill, now?”

===Chinese===
布赖斯把两张招贴的内容读了一遍又一遍。然后他思索了好一阵才开口。

“嗯，”他终于说道，“这里头大概有这么一层——福利奥特家是有钱人。众所周知，福利奥特太太想让她儿子娶比尤里小姐——也就是兰斯福德医生的被监护人。她多半不愿意这家人头上悬着什么嫌疑。我能想到的就这些。至于另一份嘛，兰斯福德是想替自己洗刷。你别忘了这一点，米钦顿！——在什么地方，也许有人知道些什么！哪怕只是一点点。可那一点点，也许就能把毫无疑问已经落到兰斯福德头上的嫌疑洗清。你要是指望攒出一桩对付兰斯福德的铁案，那你可有得忙了。今儿早上，他就凭关于那颗药丸的寥寥几句话，把你们那套理论狠狠将了一军。那么，科茨和埃弗里斯特（Everest）到底有没有找出药丸来呢？”

===Original===
“Not at liberty to say, sir,” answered Mitchington. “At present, anyway. Um! I dislike these private offers of reward—it means that those who make ’em get hold of information which is kept back from us, d’you see! They’re inconvenient.”

Then he went away, and Bryce, after waiting awhile, until night had settled down, slipped quietly out of the house and set off for the gloom of Paradise.

===Chinese===
“这个不便透露，先生，”米钦顿答道，“至少眼下不便。嗯！我不喜欢这种私人悬赏——这意味着悬赏的人会弄到一些对我们隐瞒的消息，您明白吗！这叫人不便行事。”

随后他便走了。布赖斯又等了一阵，等到夜色完全落定，便悄悄溜出屋子，朝「乐园」那一片幽暗走了过去。

## XVI — Beforehand / 第十六章　抢先一步

===Original===
In accordance with his undeniable capacity for contriving and scheming, Bryce had made due and careful preparations for his visit to the tomb of Richard Jenkins. Even in the momentary confusion following upon his discovery of Collishaw’s dead body, he had been sufficiently alive to his own immediate purposes to notice that the tomb﻿—a very ancient and dilapidated structure﻿—stood in the midst of a small expanse of stone pavement between the yew-trees and the wall of the nave; he had noticed also that the pavement consisted of small squares of stone, some of which bore initials and dates. A sharp glance at the presumed whereabouts of the particular spot which he wanted, as indicated in the scrap of paper taken from Braden’s purse, showed him that he would have to raise one of those small squares﻿—possibly two or three of them. And so he had furnished himself with a short crowbar of tempered steel, specially purchased at the ironmonger’s, and with a small bull’s-eye lantern. Had he been arrested and searched as he made his way towards the cathedral precincts he might reasonably have been suspected of a design to break into the treasury and appropriate the various ornaments for which Wrychester was famous. But Bryce feared neither arrest nor observation. During his residence in Wrychester he had done a good deal of prowling about the old city at night, and he knew that Paradise, at any time after dark, was a deserted place. Folk might cross from the close archway to the wicket-gate by the outer path, but no one would penetrate within the thick screen of yew and cypress when night had fallen. And now, in early summer, the screen of trees and bushes was so thick in leaf, that once within it, foliage on one side, the great walls of the nave on the other, there was little likelihood of any person overlooking his doings while he made his investigation. He anticipated a swift and quiet job, to be done in a few minutes.

===Chinese===
凭着那不容否认的筹谋算计的本事，布赖斯为这一趟前往理查德·詹金斯墓地的查探，作了周密而细致的准备。即便在发现科利肖尸体后那一阵短暂的慌乱里，他也仍惦记着自己眼下的目的，留意到那座墓——一座极为古老、破败不堪的建筑——坐落于紫杉树与中殿高墙之间一片不大的石板铺地上；他还留意到，那片铺地是由一方一方的小石板拼成的，其中几块上面刻着姓名首字母和年月。他从布雷登钱袋里拿走的那张纸片上标明了所要找的那个具体方位；他对着那大概的位置利落一瞥，便看出自己得撬起其中一块小石板——也许要撬两三块。于是他给自己备好了一根淬火钢的短撬棍——是特地在五金店里买来的——还有一盏小小的牛眼灯。倘若他在前往大教堂辖地的路上被捕遭搜身，人家满有理由怀疑他意图闯入宝库，把赖彻斯特素负盛名的各色教堂珍宝据为己有。可布赖斯既不怕被捕，也不怕被人撞见。在赖彻斯特居住的这段日子，他夜里常在这座古城里四处逡巡，深知「乐园」一入夜便是个无人问津的荒寂去处。行人也许会沿着外头那条小径，从教堂区的拱门穿到便门那一头去，可天一黑，便再无一人肯钻进那道紫杉与丝柏织成的厚厚屏障里去。而眼下正值初夏，那一带树木灌丛枝叶正密，一旦钻进去，一侧是层层叠叠的树叶，另一侧是中殿高大的石墙，他尽可放手查探，几乎不必担心有人窥见。他预计这活儿干起来又快又静，几分钟便可了事。

===Original===
But there was another individual in Wrychester who knew just as much of the geography of Paradise as Pemberton Bryce knew. Dick Bewery and Betty Campany had of late progressed out of the schoolboy and schoolgirl hail-fellow-well-met stage to the first dawnings of love, and in spite of their frequent meetings had begun a romantic correspondence between each other, the joy and mystery of which was increased a hundredfold by a secret method of exchange of these missives. Just within the wicket-gate entrance of Paradise there was an old monument wherein was a convenient cavity﻿—Dick Bewery’s ready wits transformed this into love’s post-office. In it he regularly placed letters for Betty: Betty stuffed into it letters for him. And on this particular evening Dick had gone to Paradise to collect a possible mail, and as Bryce walked leisurely up the narrow path, enclosed by trees and old masonry which led from Friary Lane to the ancient enclosure, Dick turned a corner and ran full into him. In the light of the single lamp which illumined the path, the two recovered themselves and looked at each other.

===Chinese===
可在赖彻斯特，还有另一个人，对「乐园」的地形了如指掌，丝毫不亚于彭伯顿·布赖斯。迪克·比尤里和贝蒂·坎帕尼（Betty Campany）近来已从少男少女那种嘻嘻哈哈、不分彼此的阶段，走到了情窦初开的边上；两人虽然时时见面，却彼此开始了一种罗曼蒂克的通信，而借由一套秘密传递信件的法子，这通信的甜蜜与神秘更增了百倍。「乐园」便门入口的内侧立着一座古老的纪念碑，碑上有一个现成的小洞——迪克·比尤里脑子一转，便把它改造成了爱情的邮局。他按时把写给贝蒂的信放进去：贝蒂也把写给他的信塞进去。就在这个特别的傍晚，迪克到「乐园」来看看有没有信可取；布赖斯正慢悠悠地沿着一条窄窄的小径走着——那条小径两侧夹着树木与古老的砖石建筑，从弗里亚里巷一路通向那片古老的围地——这时迪克拐过一个弯，一头撞进了他怀里。在照亮小径的唯一一盏路灯的光下，两人各自站稳，对视了一眼。

===Original===
“Hullo!” said Bryce. “What’s your hurry, young Bewery?”

Dick, who was panting for breath, more from excitement than haste, drew back and looked at Bryce. Up to then he knew nothing much against Bryce, whom he had rather liked in the fashion in which boys sometimes like their seniors, and he was not indisposed to confide in him.

“Hullo!” he replied. “I say! Where are you off to?”

“Nowhere!﻿—strolling round,” answered Bryce. “No particular purpose, why?”

“You weren’t going in﻿—there?” asked Dick, jerking a thumb towards Paradise.

“In﻿—there!” exclaimed Bryce. “Good Lord, no!﻿—dreary enough in the daytime! What should I be going in there for?”

===Chinese===
“喂！”布赖斯说，“你这么急着赶路，小比尤里？”

迪克正喘着粗气——倒多半是激动，而非赶路所致——他退后一步，望着布赖斯。在此之前，他并不觉得布赖斯有什么不好，反而挺喜欢他，正像男孩子有时会喜欢自己的年长一辈那样；他倒并不排斥对他推心置腹。

“喂！”他应道，“我说！你这是上哪儿去？”

“哪儿也不去！——随便逛逛，”布赖斯答道，“没特别的事，怎么啦？”

“你该不是要进去——到那里面去？”迪克问，朝「乐园」那边竖了竖大拇指。

“到——那里面去！”布赖斯失声叫道，“老天爷，才不呢！——那地方大白天就够阴森了！我进去做什么？”

===Original===
Dick seized Bryce’s coat-sleeve and dragged him aside.

“I say!” he whispered. “There’s something up in there﻿—a search of some sort!”

Bryce started in spite of an effort to keep unconcerned.

“A search? In there?” he said. “What do you mean?”

Dick pointed amongst the trees, and Bryce saw the faint glimmer of a light.

===Chinese===
迪克一把抓住布赖斯的衣袖，把他拽到一边。

“我说！”他压低声音道，“那里面出事了——像是在搜查什么！”

布赖斯尽管竭力装作若无其事，还是吃了一惊。

“搜查？在那里面？”他说，“你这话什么意思？”

迪克朝树丛里一指，布赖斯便看见一点微弱的光。

===Original===
“I was in there﻿—just now,” said Dick. “And some men﻿—three or four﻿—came along. They’re in there, close up by the nave, just where you found that chap Collishaw. They’re﻿—digging﻿—or something of that sort!”

“Digging!” muttered Bryce. “Digging?”

“Something like it, anyhow,” replied Dick. “Listen.”

Bryce heard the ring of metal on stone. And an unpleasant conviction stole over him that he was being forestalled, that somebody was beforehand with him, and he cursed himself for not having done the previous night what he had left undone till this night.

“Who are they?” he asked. “Did you see them﻿—their faces?”

===Chinese===
“我刚才——就在那里面，”迪克说，“有几条汉子——三四个——走了进来。他们就在里面，紧挨着中殿那一带，正是你发现科利肖那家伙的地方。他们在——挖——或者是那一类的事！”

“挖！”布赖斯喃喃道，“挖？”

“反正差不多是那样，”迪克答道，“你听。”

布赖斯听见了金属撞击石板的声响。一种不快的预感悄悄爬上心头：他被人抢了先，有人赶在他头里下手了；他不禁暗骂自己，头一晚该做的事偏要拖到今晚才做。

“他们是谁？”他问，“你看见他们了吗——看见脸没有？”

===Original===
“Not their faces,” answered Dick. “Only their figures in the gloom. But I heard Mitchington’s voice.”

“Police, then!” said Bryce. “What on earth are they after?”

“Look here!” whispered Dick, pulling at Bryce’s arm again. “Come on! I know how to get in there without their seeing us. You follow me.”

Bryce followed readily, and Dick stepping through the wicket-gate, seized his companion’s wrist and led him amongst the bushes in the direction of the spot from whence came the metallic sounds. He walked with the step of a cat, and Bryce took pains to follow his example. And presently from behind a screen of cypresses they looked out on the expanse of flagging in the midst of which stood the tomb of Richard Jenkins.

===Chinese===
“脸没看见，”迪克答道，“只瞧见黑影里的身形。可我听见了米钦顿的声音。”

“那便是警察了！”布赖斯说，“他们到底要找什么？”

“听我说！”迪克悄声道，又扯了扯布赖斯的胳膊，“来！我知道怎么进去才不被他们发觉。你跟着我。”

布赖斯欣然跟上。迪克从便门穿进去，一把抓住同伴的手腕，领着他钻进灌木丛，朝那金属声响传来的方向走去。他脚下走着猫一般的步子，布赖斯也费心学着他的样子。不一会儿，两人便从一道丝柏屏障后头探望出去，望见那片开阔的石板铺地，理查德·詹金斯的墓就立在铺地正中。

===Original===
Round about that tomb were five men whose faces were visible enough in the light thrown by a couple of strong lamps, one of which stood on the tomb itself, while the other was set on the ground. Four out of the five the two watchers recognized at once. One, kneeling on the flags, and busy with a small crowbar similar to that which Bryce carried inside his overcoat, was the master-mason of the cathedral. Another, standing near him, was Mitchington. A third was a clergyman﻿—one of the lesser dignitaries of the Chapter. A fourth﻿—whose presence made Bryce start for the second time that evening﻿—was the Duke of Saxonsteade. But the fifth was a stranger﻿—a tall man who stood between Mitchington and the Duke, evidently paying anxious attention to the master-mason’s proceedings. He was no Wrychester man﻿—Bryce was convinced of that.

===Chinese===
那座墓四周围着五个人，在两盏明亮的灯投下的光里，他们的脸看得清清楚楚——一盏就搁在墓上，另一盏搁在地上。五个里面，藏身观望的两人一下子认出了四个。其中一个跪在石板上，正忙着摆弄一根小撬棍，跟布赖斯大衣里头揣着的那根差不多——那是大教堂的首席石匠。另一个站在他身旁，是米钦顿。第三个是个牧师——教士会中一位职位较低的要人。第四个——他的在场让布赖斯当晚第二次大吃一惊——竟是萨克森斯蒂德公爵。可第五个却是个生人——一个高个子男人，站在米钦顿和公爵之间，分明正紧张地关注着首席石匠的一举一动。他绝非赖彻斯特本地人——这一点布赖斯深信不疑。

===Original===
And a moment later he was convinced of another equally certain fact. Whatever these five men were searching for, they had no clear or accurate idea of its exact whereabouts. The master-mason was taking up the small squares of flagstone with his crowbar one by one, from the outer edge of the foot of the old box-tomb; as he removed each, he probed the earth beneath it. And Bryce, who had instinctively realized what was happening, and knew that somebody else than himself was in possession of the secret of the scrap of paper, saw that it would be some time before they arrived at the precise spot indicated in the Latin directions. He quietly drew back and tugged at Dick Bewery.

“Stop here, and keep quiet!” he whispered when they had retreated out of all danger of being overheard. “Watch ’em! I want to fetch somebody﻿—want to know who that stranger is. You don’t know him?”

“Never seen him before,” replied Dick. “I say!﻿—come quietly back﻿—don’t give it away. I want to know what it’s all about.”

===Chinese===
片刻之后，他又确信了另一桩同样笃定的事。这五个人要找的东西，他们自己也并不清楚确切的方位。首席石匠正用撬棍，从那座古老匣形墓底部的外沿起，一方一方地把小石板撬起来；每撬起一块，他便探查底下的泥土。布赖斯凭着本能便明白了眼前是怎么回事，也知道除他之外另有人掌握了那张纸片上的秘密；他看得出来，照拉丁文指引的那个精确位置，他们还得花上一阵子才能摸到。他悄悄退后，扯了扯迪克·比尤里。

“你待在这儿，别出声！”两人退到绝无被听见的危险之处，他才悄声说，“盯着他们！我得去找个人——想知道那个生人是谁。你认得他吗？”

“从来没见过，”迪克答道，“我说！——你悄悄回来——可别走漏了风声。我想知道这到底是怎么回事。”

===Original===
Bryce squeezed the lad’s arm by way of assurance and made his way back through the bushes. He wanted to get hold of Harker, and at once, and he hurried round to the old man’s house and without ceremony walked into his parlour. Harker, evidently expecting him, and meanwhile amusing himself with his pipe and book, rose from his chair as the younger man entered.

“Found anything?” he asked.

“We’re done!” answered Bryce. “I was a fool not to go last night! We’re forestalled, my friend!﻿—that’s about it!”

“By﻿—whom?” inquired Harker.

“There are five of them at it, now,” replied Bryce. “Mitchington, a mason, one of the cathedral clergy, a stranger, and the Duke of Saxonsteade! What do you think of that?”

===Chinese===
布赖斯捏了捏少年的胳膊以示安抚，便穿过灌木丛往回走。他要立刻找到哈克，于是急匆匆绕到老人家里，也不讲究客套，径直走进了他的起居室。哈克显然正在等他，这会儿正含着烟斗、翻着书打发工夫，见年轻人进来，便从椅子上站起身。

“找到什么了吗？”他问。

“全完啦！”布赖斯答道，“我昨夜没去，真是个蠢货！我们被人抢了先，我的朋友！——就是这么回事！”

“被——谁？”哈克问。

“他们一伙五个人，眼下正干着呢，”布赖斯答道，“米钦顿，一个石匠，一位大教堂的牧师，一个生人，还有萨克森斯蒂德公爵！你看这事怎么样？”

===Original===
Harker suddenly started as if a new light had dawned on him.

“The Duke!” he exclaimed. “You don’t say so! My conscience!﻿—now, I wonder if that can really be? Upon my word, I’d never thought of it!”

“Thought of what?” demanded Bryce.

“Never mind! tell you later,” said Harker. “At present, is there any chance of getting a look at them?”

“That’s what I came for,” retorted Bryce. “I’ve been watching them, with young Bewery. He put me up to it. Come on! I want to see if you know the man who’s a stranger.”

===Chinese===
哈克猛地一怔，仿佛心里忽然亮堂起来。

“公爵！”他失声叫道，“你不是说着玩的吧！我的老天！——这下我倒要想想，莫非真是那样？说真的，我可从来没往这上头想过！”

“想什么？”布赖斯追问。

“先别管！回头再告诉你，”哈克说，“眼下嘛，还有没有机会瞧他们一眼？”

“我正是为这个来的，”布赖斯反唇相讥，“我一直跟小比尤里在一旁盯着呢。是他提醒我的。走吧！我要看看那个生人你认不认得。”

===Original===
Harker crossed the room to a chest of drawers, and after some rummaging pulled something out.

“Here!” he said, handing some articles to Bryce. “Put those on over your boots. Thick felt overshoes﻿—you could walk round your own mother’s bedroom in those and she’d never hear you. I’ll do the same. A stranger, you say? Well, this is a proof that somebody knows the secret of that scrap of paper besides us, doctor!”

“They don’t know the exact spot,” growled Bryce, who was chafing at having been done out of his discovery. “But, they’ll find it, whatever may be there.”

===Chinese===
哈克穿过屋子走到五斗橱前，翻找了一阵，掏出了什么东西。

“给！”他说着，把几样东西递给布赖斯，“套在你靴子外面穿上。厚毡面的套鞋——穿着它在你亲娘的卧室里走动，她也听不见声响。我也照样来一双。你说是个生人？嗳，这可证明了一件事，医生：除了咱们俩，还有别人也知道那张纸片上的秘密！”

“他们不知道确切的位置，”布赖斯闷声道，他正为到手的一桩发现被人截了胡而气恼不已，“可不管那底下埋着什么，他们总会找出来的。”

===Original===
He led Harker back to Paradise and to the place where he had left Dick Bewery, whom they approached so quietly that Bryce was by the lad’s side before Dick knew he was there. And Harker, after one glance at the ring of faces, drew Bryce back and put his lips close to his ear and breathed a name in an almost imperceptible yet clear whisper.

“Glassdale!”

Bryce started for the third time. Glassdale!﻿—the man whom Harker had seen in Wrychester within an hour or so of Braden’s death: the ex-convict, the forger, who had forged the Duke of Saxonsteade’s name! And there! standing, apparently quite at his ease, by the Duke’s side. What did it all mean?

===Chinese===
他领着哈克回到「乐园」，回到他留下迪克·比尤里的那个地方。两人走得极轻，布赖斯已到了少年身边，迪克才察觉他回来了。哈克只朝围成一圈的那几张脸瞥了一眼，便把布赖斯拉到一边，嘴唇凑近他的耳朵，用一个几不可闻却清晰分明的气声，吐出了一个名字。

“格拉斯代尔！”

布赖斯已是当夜第三次大吃一惊。格拉斯代尔！——布雷登死后个把钟头之内，哈克在赖彻斯特瞧见过的那个人：那个前科犯，那个伪造犯，那个伪造了萨克森斯蒂德公爵签名的人！此刻却站在那里，分明神态自若，就挨在公爵身旁！这究竟是怎么回事？

===Original===
There was no explanation of what it meant to be had from the man whom Bryce and Harker and Dick Bewery secretly watched from behind the screen of cypress trees. Four of them watched in silence, or with no more than a whispered word now and then while the fifth worked. This man worked methodically, replacing each stone as he took it up and examined the soil beneath it. So far nothing had resulted, but he was by that time working at some distance from the tomb, and Bryce, who had an exceedingly accurate idea of where the spot might be, as indicated in the measurements on the scrap of paper, nudged Harker as the master-mason began to take up the last of the small flags. And suddenly there was a movement amongst the watchers, and the master-mason looked up from his job and motioned Mitchington to pass him a trowel which lay at a little distance.

“Something here!” he said, loudly enough to reach the ears of Bryce and his companions. “Not so deep down, neither, gentlemen!”

A few vigorous applications of the trowel, a few lumps of earth cast out of the cavity, and the master-mason put in his hand and drew forth a small parcel, which in the light of the lamp held close to it by Mitchington looked to be done up in coarse sacking, secured by great blotches of black sealing wax. And now it was Harker who nudged Bryce, drawing his attention to the fact that the parcel, handed by the master-mason to Mitchington was at once passed on by Mitchington to the Duke of Saxonsteade, who, it was very plain to see, appeared to be as much delighted as surprised at receiving it.

===Chinese===
布赖斯、哈克和迪克·比尤里躲在丝柏屏障后头暗暗盯着，却从那人身上得不到半点解释。他们五个里，四个默默地瞧着，至多偶尔低声交谈一句，第五个便在干活。这人干得有条不紊，每撬起一块石板、探查过底下的泥土，便把它原样放回去。至此一无所获，但他这会儿已经挖到了离墓有一段距离的地方；而布赖斯凭着纸片上那串尺寸所标示的位置，心里对那地点究竟在哪儿判断得极为精准——首席石匠开始撬最后一块小石板时，他便用胳膊肘轻轻碰了碰哈克。陡然间，围观的那几个人起了一阵骚动，首席石匠也从手底的活计上抬起头来，示意米钦顿把稍远处搁着的一把泥刀递给他。

“这儿有东西！”他说，声音大到足以传进布赖斯及其同伴的耳朵，“埋得也不算深呢，诸位！”

泥刀使劲挥了几下，从那窟窿里掏出了几团泥土，首席石匠便伸手进去，摸出一个小包裹；米钦顿把灯凑近了照着，包裹看上去是用粗麻布裹起来的，上面用大块大块的黑色封蜡封着。这回轮到哈克用胳膊肘碰布赖斯了——他提醒布赖斯注意：首席石匠把包裹递给米钦顿，米钦顿随即又把它转到了萨克森斯蒂德公爵手里；公爵接过它时，显然既惊讶，又同样地喜形于色。

===Original===
“Let us go to your office, inspector,” he said. “We’ll examine the contents there. Let us all go at once!”

The three figures behind the cypress trees remained immovable and silent until the five searchers had gone away with their lamps and tools and the sound of their retreating footsteps in Friary Lane had died out. Then Dick Bewery moved and began to slip off, and Bryce reached out a hand and took him by the shoulder.

“I say, Bewery!” he said. “Going to tell all that?”

Harker got in a word before Dick could answer.

“No matter if he does, doctor,” he remarked quietly. “Whatever it is, the whole town’ll know of it by tomorrow. They’ll not keep it back.”

===Chinese===
“到您办公室去吧，探长，”他说，“我们在那儿把里面的东西查验一番。大家这就一道去！”

丝柏树后那三道身影一动不动、一声不吭，直等到那五个搜寻者提着灯、带着工具离去，他们退去的脚步声在弗里亚里巷里渐渐消失，这才动了。迪克·比尤里挪动身子，要悄悄溜走，布赖斯却伸出手，一把按住了他的肩膀。

“我说，比尤里！”他说，“你打算把这些都说出去吗？”

迪克还没来得及答话，哈克先插了一句。

“他说不说都不要紧，医生，”他平静地说，“不管这是怎么回事，到明天全城都会知道。他们瞒不住的。”

===Original===
Bryce let Dick go, and the boy immediately darted off in the direction of the close, while the two men went towards Harker’s house. Neither spoke until they were safe in the old detective’s little parlour, then Harker, turning up his lamp, looked at Bryce and shook his head.

“It’s a good job I’ve retired!” he said, almost sadly. “I’m getting too old for my trade, doctor. Once upon a time I should have been fit to kick myself for not having twigged the meaning of this business sooner than I have done!”

“Have you twigged it?” demanded Bryce, almost scornfully. “You’re a good deal cleverer than I am if you have. For hang me if I know what it means!”

===Chinese===
布赖斯放走了迪克，那少年立刻朝教堂区的方向飞奔而去；两个男人则往哈克家走。两人一路无话，直到安稳地进了老侦探那间小小的起居室，哈克这才把灯捻亮，望着布赖斯摇了摇头。

“幸亏我已经退下来了！”他说，几乎带着几分伤感，“我干这行是太老啦，医生。搁在从前，我非得狠狠踢自己几脚不可——竟没能早些琢磨出这事的来龙去脉！”

“你琢磨出来了？”布赖斯几乎带着几分讥讽地问，“你要是琢磨出来了，可比我聪明多了。因为打死我也想不明白这是怎么回事！”

===Original===
“I do!” answered Harker. He opened a drawer in his desk and drew out a scrapbook, filled, as Bryce saw a moment later, with cuttings from newspapers, all duly arranged and indexed. The old man glanced at the index, turned to a certain page, and put his finger on an entry. “There you are!” he said. “And that’s only one﻿—there are several more. They’ll tell you in detail what I can tell you in a few words and what I ought to have remembered. It’s fifteen years since the famous robbery at Saxonsteade which has never been accounted for﻿—robbery of the Duchess’s diamonds﻿—one of the cleverest burglaries ever known, doctor. They were got one night after a grand ball there; no arrest was ever made, they were never traced. And I’ll lay all I’m worth to a penny-piece that the Duke and those men are gladding their eyes with the sight of them just now!﻿—in Mitchington’s office﻿—and that the information that they were where they’ve just been found was given to the Duke by﻿—Glassdale!”

===Chinese===
“我琢磨出来了！”哈克答道。他打开书桌的一个抽屉，取出一本剪贴簿——布赖斯随即看见，里头贴满了从报纸上剪下来的剪报，全都分门别类地整理过、编了索引。老人瞥了一眼索引，翻到某一页，用手指按住一条。“你瞧！”他说，“这还只是一条——另外还有好几条呢。那些剪报会把事情的原委详详细细告诉你，而我只用几句话便能说清楚，这本是我早该想起来的事。距萨克森斯蒂德那桩轰动一时、至今无人能解的大盗案，已经整整十五年了——丢的是公爵夫人的钻石——医生，那是迄今为止所知最巧妙的一桩入室盗窃案之一。钻石是在某天夜里一场盛大舞会之后被偷走的；始终没有抓过人，也始终没能追到下落。我敢拿我全部家当一便士一便士地押上去——公爵和那几个人此刻正在米钦顿的办公室里，对着那些钻石大饱眼福呢！——而钻石就埋在他们刚刚找到的那处地方，这条消息，是——格拉斯代尔——提供给公爵的！”

===Original===
“Glassdale! That man!” exclaimed Bryce, who was puzzling his brain over possible developments.

“That man, sir!” repeated Harker. “That’s why Glassdale was in Wrychester the day of Braden’s death. And that’s why Braden, or Brake, came to Wrychester at all. He and Glassdale, of course, had somehow come into possession of the secret, and no doubt meant to tell the Duke together, and get the reward﻿—there was £5,000 offered! And as Brake’s dead, Glassdale’s spoken, but”﻿—here the old man paused and gave his companion a shrewd look﻿—“the question still remains: How did Brake come to his end?”

===Chinese===
“格拉斯代尔！那个人！”布赖斯失声叫道，他正绞尽脑汁推想着种种可能的发展。

“就是那个人，先生！”哈克重复道，“这便是布雷登死的那天，格拉斯代尔身在赖彻斯特的缘故。这也便是布雷登——或者说布雷克——当初之所以要来赖彻斯特的缘故。他和格拉斯代尔当然是多少探得了这个秘密，无疑打算一道去告诉公爵，好领那笔赏金——当初悬赏了 £5,000 呢！布雷克既已死了，格拉斯代尔便独自开了口，不过——”老人说到这里顿了一顿，精明地瞥了同伴一眼，“那个问题依然悬而未决：布雷克究竟是怎么死的？”

## XVII — To Be Shadowed / 第十七章　遭人盯梢

===Original===
Dick Bewery burst in upon his sister and Ransford with a budget of news such as it rarely fell to the lot of romance-loving seventeen to tell. Secret and mysterious digging up of graveyards by night—discovery of sealed packets, the contents of which might only be guessed at—the whole thing observed by hidden spectators—these were things he had read of in fiction, but had never expected to have the luck to see in real life. And being gifted with some powers of imagination and of narrative, he made the most of his story to a pair of highly attentive listeners, each of whom had his, and her, own reasons for particular attention.

“More mystery!” remarked Mary when Dick’s story had come to an end. “What a pity they didn’t open the parcel!” She looked at Ransford, who was evidently in deep thought. “I suppose it will all come out?” she suggested.

“Sure to!” he answered, and turned to Dick. “You say Bryce fetched old Harker—after you and Bryce had watched these operations a bit? Did he say why he fetched him?”

“Never said anything as to his reasons,” answered Dick. “But, I rather guessed, at the end, that Bryce wanted me to keep quiet about it, only old Harker said there was no need.”

Ransford made no comment on this, and Dick, having exhausted his stock of news, presently went off to bed.

===Chinese===
迪克·比尤里一头闯进姐姐和兰斯福德的房间，带来了一肚子新闻——这般奇遇，落到一个爱读传奇的十七岁少年头上，实在罕见。夜里偷偷摸摸地掘开墓地——发现封得严严实实的包裹，里头装着什么只能凭人猜度——而这一切，还被藏在暗处的旁观者看了个一清二楚——这些事他从前只在小说里读到过，从未指望真有福气在现实里撞见。他多少有些想象和叙事的天赋，便在两位听得极专心的听众面前，把这桩故事讲得绘声绘色；而这两位听众，一男一女，各有各的缘由，要格外留神细听。

迪克讲完了，玛丽才开口：“又添了一桩谜！”她说，“可惜他们没把那个包裹打开！”她望了望兰斯福德，后者分明正陷入沉思。“我想，这一切总会水落石出的吧？”她试探着说。

“准会的！”他答道，随即转向迪克。“你是说，你和布赖斯在一旁看了一会儿之后，布赖斯去把老哈克叫来了？他说了为什么去叫他吗？”

“他一句也没提是什么缘故，”迪克答道，“不过，到末了我倒看出来了，布赖斯是想让我别把这事说出去，只是老哈克说用不着。”

兰斯福德对此未作评论；迪克把肚里的新闻倒了个干净，过了一会儿便上楼睡觉去了。

===Original===
“Master Bryce,” observed Ransford, after a period of silence, “is playing a game! What it is, I don’t know—but I’m certain of it. Well, we shall see! You’ve been much upset by all this,” he went on, after another pause, “and the knowledge that you have has distressed me beyond measure! But just have a little—a very little—more patience, and things will be cleared—I can’t tell all that’s in my mind, even to you.”

Mary, who had been sewing while Ransford, as was customary with him in an evening, read the *Times* to her, looked down at her work.

“I shouldn’t care, if only these rumours in the town—about you—could be crushed!” she said. “It’s so cruel, so vile, that such things—”

Ransford snapped his fingers.

===Chinese===
“布赖斯那位少爷，”沉默了一阵之后，兰斯福德开口道，“在玩一套把戏！什么把戏，我不知道——可这一点我拿得准。嗯，咱们走着瞧！这一阵子你被这些事搅得心神不宁，”他顿了顿，又接着说，“而知道你心神不宁，也叫我忧心得不得了！不过，再稍微——只要再稍微——耐着点儿性子，事情便会水落石出——我心里盘算的种种，就是对你，我也不能和盘托出。”

方才兰斯福德照着他每晚的习惯，把《泰晤士报》（*Times*）读给玛丽听，玛丽一直在做针线活；这时她低下头，看着自己手里的活计。

“我才不在乎呢，只要城里那些——关于你的——风言风语能被压下去！”她说，“这些话太狠毒、太下作了，叫人说这样的话——”

兰斯福德打了个响指。

===Original===
“I don’t care that about the rumours!” he answered, contemptuously. “They’ll be crushed out just as suddenly as they arose—and then, perhaps, I’ll let certain folk in Wrychester know what I think of them. And as regards the suspicion against me, I know already that the only people in the town for whose opinion I care fully accept what I said before the Coroner. As to the others, let them talk! If the thing comes to a head before its due time—”

“You make me think that you know more—much more!—than you’ve ever told me!” interrupted Mary.

“So I do!” he replied. “And you’ll see in the end why I’ve kept silence. Of course, if people who don’t know as much will interfere—”

He was interrupted there by the ringing of the front door bell, at the sound of which he and Mary looked at each other.

===Chinese===
“我压根儿不把那些风言风语放在心上！”他轻蔑地答道，“它们怎样骤然冒出来，便会怎样骤然被压下去——到那时候，也许我要让赖彻斯特某些人知道知道，我对他们是怎么个看法。至于对我的那点嫌疑，我心里清楚，城里我唯一在乎他们看法的那些人，全都信了我在验尸官面前说的那番话。至于旁人，随他们去嚼舌根吧！倘若这件事还没到时候就闹起来——”

“你这话叫我觉着，你知道的——比你所告诉我的，要多得多！”玛丽打断了他。

“的确如此！”他答道，“你终归会明白我为何一直缄口不言。当然，要是那些不知道内情的人硬要横插一杠——”

他话说到这里，便被前门门铃声打断了；听见铃声，他和玛丽对视了一眼。

===Original===
“Who can that be?” said Mary. “It’s past ten o’clock.”

Ransford offered no suggestion. He sat silently waiting, until the parlourmaid entered.

“Inspector Mitchington would be much obliged if you could give him a few minutes, sir,” she said.

Ransford got up from his chair.

“Take Inspector Mitchington into the study,” he said. “Is he alone?”

“No, sir—there’s a gentleman with him,” replied the girl.

===Chinese===
“这会是谁呢？”玛丽说，“都过十点了。”

兰斯福德没作声。他默默坐着等候，直到女佣走了进来。

“先生，米钦顿探长想请您赏几分钟，”她说。

兰斯福德从椅子上站起来。

“请米钦顿探长到书房里坐，”他说，“他一个人吗？”

“不是的，先生——还有一位先生跟他一道，”姑娘答道。

===Original===
“All right—I’ll be with them presently,” answered Ransford. “Take them both in there and light the gas. Police!” he went on, when the parlourmaid had gone. “They get hold of the first idea that strikes them, and never even look round for another. You’re not frightened?”

“Frightened—no! Uneasy—yes!” replied Mary. “What can they want, this time of night?”

“Probably to tell me something about this romantic tale of Dick’s,” answered Ransford, as he left the room. “It’ll be nothing more serious, I assure you.”

But he was not so sure of that. He was very well aware that the Wrychester police authorities had a definite suspicion of his guilt in the Braden and Collishaw matters, and he knew from experience that police suspicion is a difficult matter to dissipate. And before he opened the door of the little room which he used as a study he warned himself to be careful—and silent.

===Chinese===
“好——我马上就来，”兰斯福德答道，“请他们两位都到书房去，把煤气灯点上。警察！”女佣出去之后，他又接着说，“他们一旦抓住脑子里冒出的第一个念头，便再也不肯回头去寻第二个了。你没害怕吧？”

“害怕——倒没有！不安——是有的！”玛丽答道，“这么晚了，他们能有什么事？”

“多半是要跟我说说迪克那桩离奇故事的事，”兰斯福德一边往外走，一边答道，“我向你保证，不会是什么更严重的事。”

可他自己心里并不这么笃定。他心里清楚得很，赖彻斯特的警方已认定他跟布雷登和科利肖两桩案子脱不了干系，而且他凭经验知道，警察一旦起了疑心，要打消它可不容易。于是，在推开那间他当作书房用的小屋子的门之前，他先提醒自己——要当心，要缄口。

===Original===
The two visitors stood near the hearth—Ransford took a good look at them as he closed the door behind him. Mitchington he knew well enough; he was more interested in the other man, a stranger. A quiet-looking, very ordinary individual, who might have been half a dozen things—but Ransford instantly set him down as a detective. He turned from this man to the inspector.

“Well?” he said, a little brusquely. “What is it?”

“Sorry to intrude so late, Dr. Ransford,” answered Mitchington, “but I should be much obliged if you would give us a bit of information—badly wanted, doctor, in view of recent events,” he added, with a smile which was meant to be reassuring. “I’m sure you can—if you will.”

“Sit down,” said Ransford, pointing to chairs. He took one himself and again glanced at the stranger. “To whom am I speaking, in addition to yourself, Inspector?” he asked. “I’m not going to talk to strangers.”

“Oh, well!” said Mitchington, a little awkwardly. “Of course, doctor, we’ve had to get a bit of professional help in these unpleasant matters. This gentleman’s Detective-Sergeant Jettison, from the Yard.”

“What information do you want?” asked Ransford.

===Chinese===
两位来客站在壁炉近旁——兰斯福德随手带上门，仔细打量了他们一番。米钦顿他再熟不过；他更感兴趣的倒是另一个人，一个生面孔。这是个神色沉静、看去十分寻常的人物，身份可能是好几种行当里的任何一种——但兰斯福德一眼就断定他是探子。他收回目光，从这人转向了探长。

“嗯？”他略带生硬地说，“什么事？”

“这么晚来打扰，实在抱歉，兰斯福德医生，”米钦顿答道，“不过，要是您能给我们提供一点情况，我将不胜感激——医生，鉴于近来发生的种种事，这情况我们要得急呢，”他添了一句，脸上带着一个想叫人安心的微笑，“我敢说，您是能提供的——只要您肯。”

“请坐，”兰斯福德指了指椅子说。他自己也坐下来，又瞥了那生人一眼。“探长，除了您之外，我这是在同谁说话？”他问，“我不跟陌生人交谈。”

“哦，这个嘛！”米钦顿略带尴尬地说，“医生，您是知道的，这些不愉快的事，我们不得不请些专业上的帮手。这位先生是从苏格兰场来的杰蒂森侦探警长（Detective-Sergeant Jettison）。”

“你们要什么情况？”兰斯福德问。

===Original===
Mitchington glanced at the door and lowered his voice. “I may as well tell you, doctor,” he said confidentially, “there’s been a most extraordinary discovery made tonight, which has a bearing on the Braden case. I dare say you’ve heard of the great jewel robbery which took place at the Duke of Saxonsteade’s some years ago, which has been a mystery to this very day?”

“I have heard of it,” answered Ransford.

“Very well—tonight those jewels—the whole lot!—have been discovered in Paradise yonder, where they’d been buried, at the time of the robbery, by the thief,” continued Mitchington. “They’ve just been examined, and they’re now in the Duke’s own hands again—after all these years! And—I may as well tell you—we now know that the object of Braden’s visit to Wrychester was to tell the Duke where those jewels were hidden. Braden—and another man—had learned the secret, from the real thief, who’s dead in Australia. All that I may tell you, doctor—for it’ll be public property tomorrow.”

“Well?” said Ransford.

===Chinese===
米钦顿瞥了一眼门，压低了声音。“我不妨跟您说，医生，”他凑近了道，“今晚有了一桩极为惊人的发现，与布雷登一案有关。几年前萨克森斯蒂德公爵府上那桩失窃大案，至今仍是一桩悬案，我想您总听说过吧？”

“我听说过。”兰斯福德答道。

“那好——今晚，那些珠宝——整整一批！——在彼处的「乐园」里被发现了，正是当年案发时由那个窃贼埋在那儿的，”米钦顿接着说，“刚刚验看过，此刻已重新回到公爵本人手里了——隔了这么多年！还有——我不妨一并告诉您——我们现在知道了，布雷登此番来赖彻斯特，目的就是要把珠宝藏匿之处告诉公爵。布雷登——还有另一个人——是从真正的窃贼那里得知这个秘密的，那窃贼已经在澳大利亚死了。这些，医生，我都可以告诉您——因为到了明天，这就是满城皆知的事了。”

“然后呢？”兰斯福德说。

===Original===
Mitchington hesitated a moment, as if searching for his next words. He glanced at the detective; the detective remained immobile; he glanced at Ransford; Ransford gave him no encouragement.

“Now look here, doctor!” he exclaimed, suddenly. “Why not tell us something? We know now who Braden really was! That’s settled. Do you understand?”

“Who was he, then?” asked Ransford, quietly.

“He was one John Brake, some time manager of a branch of a London bank, who, seventeen years ago, got ten years’ penal servitude for embezzlement,” answered Mitchington, watching Ransford steadily. “That’s dead certain—we know it! The man who shared this secret with him about the Saxonsteade jewels has told us that much, today. John Brake!”

“What have you come here for?” asked Ransford.

===Chinese===
米钦顿迟疑了片刻，仿佛在斟酌下边该说什么。他瞥了那侦探一眼；侦探纹丝不动；他又瞥了兰斯福德一眼；兰斯福德却不给他半点鼓励。

“我说，医生！”他突然嚷起来，“您何不跟我们说说呢？布雷登究竟是谁，我们现在弄清楚了！这事已成定论。您明白吗？”

“那他是谁？”兰斯福德不动声色地问。

“他是约翰·布雷克，曾在伦敦一家银行的分行当过经理，十七年前因侵吞公款被判了十年苦役流放，”米钦顿答道，目光紧盯着兰斯福德，“这可是铁板钉钉——我们查实了！今天，那个同他一起知晓萨克森斯蒂德珠宝秘密的人，已经把这些都告诉了我们。约翰·布雷克！”

“你们来这儿做什么？”兰斯福德问。

===Original===
“To ask you—between ourselves—if you can tell us anything about Brake’s earlier days—antecedents—that’ll help us,” replied Mitchington. “It may be—Jettison here—a man of experience—thinks it’ll be found to be—that Brake, or Braden as we call him—was murdered because of his possession of that secret about the jewels. Our informant tells us that Braden certainly had on him, when he came to Wrychester, a sort of diagram showing the exact location of the spot where the jewels were hidden—that diagram was most assuredly not found on Braden when we examined his clothing and effects. It may be that it was wrested from him in the gallery of the clerestory that morning, and that his assailant, or assailants—for there may have been two men at the job—afterwards pitched him through that open doorway, after half-stifling him. And if that theory’s correct—and I, personally, am now quite inclined to it—it’ll help a lot if you’ll tell us what you know of Braden’s—Brake’s—antecedents. Come now, doctor!—you know very well that Braden, or Brake, did come to your surgery that morning and said to your assistant that he’d known a Dr. Ransford in times past! Why not speak?”

Ransford, instead of answering Mitchington’s evidently genuine appeal, looked at the New Scotland Yard man.

===Chinese===
“想请教您——咱们私下里说——您能不能告诉我们一些布雷克早年——他的身世来历——能帮上忙的情况，”米钦顿答道，“也许是——这位杰蒂森，是个老手——他认为最终会查明——布雷克，也就是我们所说的布雷登——是因为掌握了那桩珠宝秘密，才招来杀身之祸的。据那个提供情况的人说，布雷登来到赖彻斯特时，身上确实带着一张图，标明珠宝藏匿的确切地点——可我们查验他的衣物和遗物时，那张图却无论如何也找不着。也许那天早上，是在高侧窗层的回廊里，那张图被人从他手里夺了去；而后行凶的人——或者那帮人，因为说不定是两个人一块儿干的——在把他掐得半死之后，又把他从那扇敞开的门洞里扔了下去。倘若这个推断没错——而我个人现在也相当倾向于这种看法——那么，您要是能把您所知道的布雷登——布雷克——的身世来历告诉我们，那便帮了大忙了。来吧，医生！您心里清楚得很，那天早上布雷登，或者布雷克，确实到过您的诊室，还跟您的助手说他从前认识一位兰斯福德医生！您为何不说呢？”

兰斯福德没有接米钦顿那显然发自肺腑的恳求，而是把目光投向了那个新苏格兰场来的人。

===Original===
“Is that your theory?” he asked.

Jettison nodded his head, with a movement indicative of conviction.

“Yes, sir!” he replied. “Having regard to all the circumstances of the case, as they’ve been put before me since I came here, and with special regard to the revelations which have resulted in the discovery of these jewels, it is! Of course, today’s events have altered everything. If it hadn’t been for our informant—”

“Who is your informant?” inquired Ransford.

The two callers looked at each other—the detective nodded at the inspector.

“Oh, well!” said Mitchington. “No harm in telling you, doctor. A man named Glassdale—once a fellow-convict with Brake. It seems they left England together after their time was up, emigrated together, prospered, even went so far—both of ’em!—as to make good the money they’d appropriated, and eventually came back together—in possession of this secret. Brake came specially to Wrychester to tell the Duke—Glassdale was to join him on the very morning Brake met his death. Glassdale did come to the town that morning—and as soon as he got here, heard of Brake’s strange death. That upset him—and he went away—only to come back today, go to Saxonsteade, and tell everything to the Duke—with the result we’ve told you of.”

===Chinese===
“这是您的推断？”他问。

杰蒂森点了点头，那动作透着十分笃信。

“是的，先生！”他答道，“综合我到此地以来别人向我陈述的种种案情，尤其考虑到那些最终促成珠宝被发现的种种揭露，确是如此！当然，今天发生的种种，已经把一切都改变了。要不是那个提供情况的人——”

“那个提供情况的人是谁？”兰斯福德问。

两位来客对视了一眼——侦探朝探长点了点头。

“哦，也好！”米钦顿说，“医生，告诉您也无妨。一个名叫格拉斯代尔的人——从前与布雷克是同牢的难友。看来两人刑满之后一道离开了英国，一道移民，一道发了迹，甚至——两个人都是！——把当年侵吞的款项都如数补还了，最后又一道回来——手里攥着这个秘密。布雷克专程到赖彻斯特来告知公爵——格拉斯代尔本该在布雷克送命的那天早晨赶来与他汇合。那天早上格拉斯代尔确实到了城里——可他一到，便听说了布雷克那桩离奇的死。这一下把他吓着了——他随即离去——却不料今天又转了回来，跑到萨克森斯蒂德，把一切对公爵和盘托出——结果便是我俩方才跟您说的那些。”

===Original===
“Which result,” remarked Ransford, steadily regarding Mitchington, “has apparently altered all your ideas about—me!”

Mitchington laughed a little awkwardly.

“Oh, well, come, now, doctor!” he said. “Why, yes—frankly, I’m inclined to Jettison’s theory—in fact, I’m certain that’s the truth.”

“And your theory,” inquired Ransford, turning to the detective, “is—put it in a few words.”

“My theory—and I’ll lay anything it’s the correct one!—is this,” replied Jettison. “Brake came to Wrychester with his secret. That secret wasn’t confined to him and Glassdale—either he let it out to somebody, or it was known to somebody. I understand from Inspector Mitchington here that on the evening of his arrival Brake was away from the Mitre Hotel for two hours. During that time, he was somewhere—with whom? Probably with somebody who got the secret out of him, or to whom he communicated it. For, think!—according to Glassdale, who, we are quite sure, has told the exact truth about everything, Brake had on him a scrap of paper, on which were instructions, in Latin, for finding the exact spot whereat the missing Saxonsteade jewels had been hidden, years before, by the actual thief—who, I may tell you, sir, never had the opportunity of returning to repossess himself of them. Now, after Brake’s death, the police examined his clothes and effects—they never found that scrap of paper! And I work things out this way. Brake was followed into that gallery—a lonely, quiet place—by the man or men who had got possession of the secret; he was, I’m told, a slightly-built, not over-strong man—he was seized and robbed of that paper and flung to his death. And all that fits in with the second mystery of Collishaw—who probably knew, if not everything, then something, of the exact circumstances of Brake’s death, and let his knowledge get to the ears of—Brake’s assailant!—who cleverly got rid of him. That’s my notion,” concluded the detective. “And—I shall be surprised if it isn’t a correct one!”

===Chinese===
“而这结果，”兰斯福德目不转睛地望着米钦顿说，“显然把你们先前对——我！——的看法全都改变了！”

米钦顿略带尴尬地笑了笑。

“嗳，好了，好了，医生！”他说，“这个嘛，是的——坦白讲，我倾向于杰蒂森那个推断——其实，我确信那便是真相。”

“而您的推断，”兰斯福德转向那侦探问，“是——用几句话说出来。”

“我的推断——我敢拿任何东西打赌，它便是正确的那个！——是这样的，”杰蒂森答道，“布雷克带着他的秘密来到赖彻斯特。这秘密并非只有他和格拉斯代尔两人知道——不是他向什么人漏了底，便是早有什么人知晓了它。据这位米钦顿探长告诉我，布雷登到达的那天晚上，离开米特旅馆足足有两个钟头。在那段时间里，他去了某处——跟谁？很可能是同一个从他嘴里套出秘密的人，或者一个他主动把秘密告诉了的人。因为，您想想！——据格拉斯代尔所说——而我们对格拉斯代尔所讲的一切，都确信不疑——布雷克身上带着一张纸片，上面用拉丁文写着几条指示，说明那些下落不明的萨克森斯蒂德珠宝当年被真正的窃贼藏匿的确切地点——顺便告诉您，先生，那窃贼后来始终没有机会再回来取回它们。布雷克死后，警方查验过他的衣物和遗物——那张纸片却始终没有找到！我是这样推想的：布雷克被人尾随进了那道回廊——一个偏僻、清静的地方——尾随他的，便是掌握了那个秘密的那个人或那一伙人；据说他是个身量单薄、不算壮健的人——于是他被一把抓住，抢去了那张纸片，被扔下去送了命。而这一切，又与科利肖那第二桩谜案吻合——科利肖即便不是知道全部，至少也知道一些布雷克之死的确切情形，而他的话不知怎的传到了——布雷克那个行凶者的耳朵里！——那行凶者便巧妙地除掉了他。这便是我的看法，”侦探收尾道，“而且——倘若它不是正确的那个，我倒要奇怪了！”

===Original===
“And, as I’ve said, doctor,” chimed in Mitchington, “can’t you give us a bit of information, now? You see the line we’re on? Now, as it’s evident you once knew Braden, or Brake—”

“I have never said so!” interrupted Ransford sharply.

“Well—we infer it, from the undoubted fact that he called here,” remarked Mitchington. “And if—”

===Chinese===
“那么，正如我方才所说，医生，”米钦顿接话道，“您现在不能给我们提供一点情况吗？您瞧我们走的是哪条线索了吧？那么，既然您当年分明认识布雷登，或者布雷克——”

“我可从未这样说过！”兰斯福德厉声打断他。

“这个嘛——我们从他那次登门这一不容置疑的事实里推断出来的，”米钦顿说，“要是——”

===Original===
“Wait!” said Ransford. He had been listening with absorbed attention to Jettison’s theory, and he now rose from his chair and began to pace the room, hands in pockets, as if in deep thought. Suddenly he paused and looked at Mitchington. “This needs some reflection,” he said. “Are you pressed for time?”

“Not in the least,” answered Mitchington, readily. “Our time’s yours, sir. Take as long as you like.”

Ransford touched a bell and summoning the parlourmaid told her to fetch whisky, soda, and cigars. He pressed these things on the two men, lighted a cigar himself, and for a long time continued to walk up and down his end of the room, smoking and evidently in very deep thought. The visitors left him alone, watching him curiously now and then—until, when quite ten minutes had gone by, he suddenly drew a chair close to them and sat down again.

===Chinese===
“等一下！”兰斯福德说。他方才全神贯注地听着杰蒂森那番推断，这时便从椅子上站起身，双手插在口袋里，在屋里踱起步来，似在沉思。猛然间他停下脚步，望着米钦顿。“这件事得好好琢磨琢磨，”他说，“你们赶时间吗？”

“一点也不，”米钦顿爽快地答道，“我们的时间尽可由您支配，先生。您爱琢磨多久就琢磨多久。”

兰斯福德按了按铃，把女佣叫来，吩咐她去取威士忌、苏打水和雪茄。他殷勤劝两位来客享用，自己也点上一支雪茄，很长一段时间里，只管在他那一头的屋里来回踱步，抽着烟，分明在极深地思忖着什么。两位来客不去打扰他，只是时不时好奇地瞧上他一眼——直等到足足过了十分钟，他才突然把一把椅子拉近他们，重新坐了下来。

===Original===
“Now, listen to me!” he said. “If I give my confidence to you, as police officials, will you give me your word that you won’t make use of my information until I give you leave—or until you have consulted me further? I shall rely on your word, mind!”

“I say yes to that, doctor,” answered Mitchington.

“The same here, sir,” said the detective.

“Very well,” continued Ransford. “Then—this is between ourselves, until such time as I say something more about it. First of all, I am not going to tell you anything whatever about Braden’s antecedents—at present! Secondly—I am not sure that your theory, Mr. Jettison, is entirely correct, though I think it is by way of coming very near to the right one—which is sure to be worked out before long. But—on the understanding of secrecy for the present I can tell you something which I should not have been able to tell you but for the events of tonight, which have made me put together certain facts. Now attention! To begin with, I know where Braden was for at any rate some time on the evening of the day on which he came to Wrychester. He was with the old man whom we all know as Simpson Harker.”

===Chinese===
“现在，听我说！”他道，“我若是以警察官的身份，把我的底细向你们和盘托出，你们能不能向我保证：在我许可之前——或者在你们进一步与我商议之前——绝不利用我提供的这些情况？我可全指望你们一句话了，记住！”

“我答应，医生，”米钦顿答道。

“我也一样，先生，”侦探说。

“很好，”兰斯福德接着说，“那么——此事仅限你我之间，直到我另行说起为止。第一，布雷登的身世来历，我现在——眼下——什么也不会告诉你们！第二——杰蒂森先生，您的推断是否完全正确，我没有把握，不过我想它已相当接近真相——而真相，不久定能水落石出。但是——在眼下保密的前提下，我可以告诉你们一件事；若不是今夜发生了这些事，叫我重新把若干事实串联起来，这件事我原是说不了的。现在留神！头一件，布雷登来到赖彻斯特的那天傍晚，至少有一段工夫他在哪儿，我是知道的。他同那个我们大伙儿都熟知的老人——辛普森·哈克——待在一起。”

===Original===
Mitchington whistled; the detective, who knew nothing of Simpson Harker, glanced at him as if for information. But Mitchington nodded at Ransford, and Ransford went on.

“I know this for this reason,” he continued. “You know where Harker lives. I was in attendance for nearly two hours that evening on a patient in a house opposite—I spent a good deal of time in looking out of the window. I saw Harker take a man into his house: I saw the man leave the house nearly an hour later: I recognized that man next day as the man who met his death at the Cathedral. So much for that.”

“Good!” muttered Mitchington. “Good! Explains a lot.”

===Chinese===
米钦顿倒吸一口气，吹了声口哨；那侦探对辛普森·哈克其人一无所知，便朝他望了一眼，像是要讨个底细。但米钦顿只朝兰斯福德点了点头，兰斯福德便接着往下说。

“我知道这点，理由如下，”他接着说，“哈克住在哪儿，你们是知道的。那天傍晚，我在他家对面一户人家的一位病人床前守了将近两个钟头——我有好大一段工夫都站在窗前朝外张望。我看见哈克把一个男人领进了自家屋里：将近一个钟头之后，我又看见那人从屋里出来：到了第二天，我认出那人正是那个在大教堂送了命的人。这件事，就说到这里。”

“好！”米钦顿喃喃道，“好！这一下许多事都说得通了。”

===Original===
“But,” continued Ransford, “what I have to tell you now is of a much more serious—and confidential—nature. Now, do you know—but, of course, you don’t!—that your proceedings tonight were watched?”

“Watched!” exclaimed Mitchington. “Who watched us?”

“Harker, for one,” answered Ransford. “And—for another—my late assistant, Mr. Pemberton Bryce.”

Mitchington’s jaw dropped.

“God bless my soul!” he said. “You don’t mean it, doctor! Why, how did you—”

===Chinese===
“不过，”兰斯福德接着说，“我现在要告诉你们的这件事，性质可要严重得多——也机密得多。我问你们——当然，你们是无从知道的！——今晚你们那一番举动，可有人在一旁盯着，你们知道吗？”

“有人盯着！”米钦顿失声叫道，“谁盯着我们了？”

“哈克，是一个，”兰斯福德答道，“还有——另一个——我从前的助手，彭伯顿·布赖斯先生。”

米钦顿惊得下巴都掉了下来。

“老天爷！”他说，“您不是说着玩的吧，医生！嗳，您是怎么——”

===Original===
“Wait a minute,” interrupted Ransford. He left the room, and the two callers looked at each other.

“This chap knows more than you think,” observed Jettison in a whisper. “More than he’s telling now!”

“Let’s get all we can, then,” said Mitchington, who was obviously much surprised by Ransford’s last information. “Get it while he’s in the mood.”

“Let him take his own time,” advised Jettison. “But—you mark me!—he knows a lot! This is only an instalment.”

===Chinese===
“等一下，”兰斯福德打断他。他走出屋去，两位来客便面面相觑。

“这家伙知道的比你料想的多，”杰蒂森悄声道，“比他此刻说的还要多！”

“那咱们就尽量多套些出来，”米钦顿说，兰斯福德方才那番话显然把他惊得不轻，“趁他肯说的时候多套些。”

“让他按自己的步子来，”杰蒂森劝道，“不过——你给我记着！——他肚里有货！这不过是头一笔。”

===Original===
Ransford came back—with Dick Bewery, clad in a loud patterned and gaily coloured suit of pyjamas.

“Now, Dick,” said Ransford. “Tell Inspector Mitchington precisely what happened this evening, within your own knowledge.”

Dick was nothing loth to tell his story for the second time—especially to a couple of professional listeners. And he told it in full detail, from the moment of his sudden encounter with Bryce to that in which he parted with Bryce and Harker. Ransford, watching the official faces, saw what it was in the story that caught the official attention and excited the official mind.

===Chinese===
兰斯福德回来了——身后带着迪克·比尤里，少年身上套着一身花色扎眼、鲜艳明快的睡衣。

“来，迪克，”兰斯福德说，“把你今晚亲眼所见、亲耳所闻的事，原原本本告诉米钦顿探长。”

迪克巴不得把这故事再讲上第二遍——尤其是讲给两位吃公家饭的内行人听。他把事情从头到尾讲得详详细细，从他怎样猛地撞见布赖斯那一刻起，直到他与布赖斯、哈克分手那一刻为止。兰斯福德留神察看那两张官方面的脸，看出了故事里究竟是哪一处攥住了他们的注意、撩动了他们的心思。

===Original===
“Dr. Bryce went off at once to fetch Harker, did he?” asked Mitchington, when Dick had made a end.

“At once,” answered Dick. “And was jolly quick back with him!”

“And Harker said it didn’t matter about your telling as it would be public news soon enough?” continued Mitchington.

“Just that,” said Dick.

===Chinese===
“布赖斯医生马上就去叫哈克了，是吗？”迪克讲完之后，米钦顿问。

“马上就去，”迪克答道，“而且带着他飞快地转了回来！”

“哈克还说，你说出去也不要紧，因为这事很快就会传得尽人皆知？”米钦顿又问。

“正是这样，”迪克说。

===Original===
Mitchington looked at Ransford, and Ransford nodded to his ward.

“All right, Dick,” he said. “That’ll do.”

The boy went off again, and Mitchington shook his head.

“Queer!” he said. “Now what have those two been up to?—something, that’s certain. Can you tell us more, doctor?”

===Chinese===
米钦顿望了望兰斯福德，兰斯福德便朝他的被监护人点了点头。

“好了，迪克，”他说，“你去吧。”

少年又出去了，米钦顿摇了摇头。

“蹊跷！”他说，“那两个人到底在搞什么名堂？——准是在搞什么，这没错。您还能再多告诉我们一些吗，医生？”

===Original===
“Under the same conditions—yes,” answered Ransford, taking his seat again. “The fact is, affairs have got to a stage where I consider it my duty to tell you more. Some of what I shall tell you is hearsay—but it’s hearsay that you can easily verify for yourselves when the right moment comes. Mr. Campany, the librarian, lately remarked to me that my old assistant, Mr. Bryce, seemed to be taking an extraordinary interest in archaeological matters since he left me—he was now, said Campany, always examining documents about the old tombs and monuments of the Cathedral and its precincts.”

“Ah—just so!” exclaimed Mitchington. “To be sure!—I’m beginning to see!”

“And,” continued Ransford, “Campany further remarked, as a matter for humorous comment, that Bryce was also spending much time looking round our old tombs. Now you made this discovery near an old tomb, I understand?”

“Close by one—yes,” assented the inspector.

===Chinese===
“在同样的条件下——可以，”兰斯福德答道，重新坐了下来，“事实是，事情已经到了这个地步，我认为有责任再多告诉你们一些。我下面要说的，有些是道听途说——但那都是到了合适的时机，你们自己轻而易举便能查证的。图书馆员坎帕尼先生最近对我说起，我从前的助手布赖斯先生，离开我之后，似乎对考古一道生出了异乎寻常的兴趣——坎帕尼说，他这会儿成天翻检的，都是些关于大教堂及其四围那些古墓古碑的文献。”

“啊——正是如此！”米钦顿叫起来，“可不是嘛！——我有点儿开窍了！”

“还有，”兰斯福德接着说，“坎帕尼又带着几分打趣的口吻说，布赖斯还花了不少工夫，在我们那些古墓跟前转悠来转悠去。我且问你，你们这一桩发现，是在一座古墓附近，对吧？”

“就在一座古墓跟前——是的，”探长点头承认。

===Original===
“Then let me draw your attention to one or two strange facts—which are undoubted facts,” continued Ransford. “Bryce was left alone with the dead body of Braden for some minutes, while Varner went to fetch the police. That’s one.”

“That’s true,” muttered Mitchington. “He was—several minutes!”

“Bryce it was who discovered Collishaw—in Paradise,” said Ransford. “That’s fact two. And fact three—Bryce evidently had a motive in fetching Harker tonight—to overlook your operations. What was his motive? And taking things altogether; what are, or have been, these secret affairs which Bryce and Harker have evidently been engaged in?”

===Chinese===
“那么，容我请你们注意一两桩蹊跷的事实——而且是确凿无疑的事实，”兰斯福德接着说，“瓦纳去叫警察的那阵子，布赖斯独自和布雷登的尸体待了几分钟。这是其一。”

“这倒不假，”米钦顿喃喃道，“他确实——待了好几分钟！”

“在「乐园」里发现科利肖的，正是布赖斯，”兰斯福德说，“这是其二。其三——今夜布赖斯去叫哈克，分明是别有用心——好监视你们的举动。他的用意何在？再把种种事归到一处看：布赖斯和哈克分明一直在暗中进行的，究竟是怎样一些勾当，或曾经是些什么勾当？”

===Original===
Jettison suddenly rose, buttoning his light overcoat. The action seemed to indicate a newly-formed idea, a definite conclusion. He turned sharply to Mitchington.

“There’s one thing certain, inspector,” he said. “You’ll keep an eye on those two from this out! From—just now!”

“I shall!” assented Mitchington. “I’ll have both of ’em shadowed wherever they go or are, day or night. Harker, now, has always been a bit of a mystery, but Bryce—hang me if I don’t believe he’s been having me! Double game!—but, never mind. There’s no more, doctor?”

“Not yet,” replied Ransford. “And I don’t know the real meaning or value of what I have told you. But—in two days from now, I can tell you more. In the meantime—remember your promise!”

He let his visitors out then, and went back to Mary.

“You’ll not have to wait long for things to clear,” he said. “The mystery’s nearly over!”

===Chinese===
杰蒂森陡然站起身来，扣上他那件薄外套的扣子。这动作似乎意味着一个新念头已然成形，一个明确的结论已然下定。他蓦地转向米钦顿。

“有一件事是肯定的，探长，”他说，“从今往后，你可得盯紧那两个人！从——眼下这刻起！”

“我一定！”米钦顿应道，“我要派人盯他们的梢，不论他们上哪儿、待在哪儿，白天黑夜都盯。哈克嘛，向来有点儿叫人摸不透，可布赖斯——打死我也得信，他一直在耍我！两面三刀！——不过，且不去管它。没有别的了吧，医生？”

“暂时没有，”兰斯福德答道，“我所告诉你们的这些，真正的含义和分量几何，我也说不准。不过——再过两天，我能告诉你们更多。这期间——记着你们的承诺！”

他随即把两位来客送了出去，回到玛丽身边。

“你要等着事情廓清，日子不会太久了，”他说，“这桩谜案，快收场了！”

## XVIII — Surprise / 第十八章　惊奇

===Original===
Mitchington and the man from New Scotland Yard walked away in silence from Ransford’s house and kept the silence up until they were in the middle of the Close and accordingly in solitude. Then Mitchington turned to his companion.

“What d’ye think of that?” he asked, with a half laugh. “Different complexion it puts on things, eh?”

“I think just what I said before﻿—in there,” replied the detective. “That man knows more than he’s told, even now!”

“Why hasn’t he spoken sooner, then?” demanded Mitchington. “He’s had two good chances﻿—at the inquests.”

“From what I saw of him, just now,” said Jettison, “I should say he’s the sort of man who can keep his own counsel till he considers the right time has come for speaking. Not the sort of man who’ll care twopence whatever’s said about him, you understand? I should say he’s known a good lot all along, and is just keeping it back till he can put a finishing touch to it. Two days, didn’t he say? Aye, well, a lot can happen in two days!”

===Chinese===
米钦顿和新苏格兰场（New Scotland Yard）来的那人一言不发地走离了兰斯福德的宅子，一路沉默，直到走进教堂区中央、四下无人的地方。米钦顿这才转向他的同伴。

“你觉得怎么样？”他带着半声笑问道，“这下事情可就另是一番面目啦，是不是？”

“我的想法跟方才在里头说的一样——”那侦探答道，“这个人，直到现在，知道的也比他亲口说出口的多！”

“那他怎么不早些开口？”米钦顿追问，“他有过两次好机会——在验尸聆讯上。”

“就凭我方才对他的观察，”杰蒂森说，“我得说，他是那种能把心思藏在肚里、直到他认为该开口的时候才开口的人。绝不是那种别人议论他什么、他都要在乎半分的人，你懂吗？依我看，他一向知道得不少，只是按着不说，等到能给这事添上最后那一笔的时候。两天，他不是说吗？哈，好嘛，两天里头能出多少事啊！”

===Original===
“But about your theory?” questioned Mitchington. “What do you think of it now﻿—in relation to what we’ve just heard?”

“I’ll tell you what I can see,” answered Jettison. “I can see how one bit of this puzzle fits into another﻿—in view of what Ransford has just told us. Of course, one’s got to do a good deal of supposing﻿—it’s unavoidable in these cases. Now supposing Braden let this man Harker into the secret of the hidden jewels that night, and supposing that Harker and Bryce are in collusion﻿—as they evidently are, from what that boy told us﻿—and supposing they between them, together or separately, had to do with Braden’s death, and supposing that man Collishaw saw some thing that would incriminate one or both﻿—eh?”

“Well?” asked Mitchington.

“Bryce is a medical man,” observed Jettison. “It would be an easy thing for a medical man to get rid of Collishaw as he undoubtedly was got rid of. Do you see my point?”

“Aye﻿—and I can see that Bryce is a clever hand at throwing dust in anybody’s eyes!” muttered Mitchington. “I’ve had some dealings with him over this affair and I’m beginning to think﻿—only now!﻿—that he’s been having me for the mug! He’s evidently a deep ’un﻿—and so’s the other man.”

===Chinese===
“那你那套推断呢？”米钦顿问道，“对照咱们方才听见的——眼下你觉得它怎么样了？”

“我告诉你我看得出的，”杰蒂森答道，“就凭兰斯福德方才跟我们说的，我看得出这桩谜案的一块怎样扣进了另一块里。当然，这当中得做不少假设——办这种案子是免不了的。现在假设，那天夜里布雷登把藏珠宝的秘密透露给了这个叫哈克的人；再假设哈克和布赖斯暗中勾结——从那孩子告诉咱们的话来看，他们显然是勾结着的——又假设他们两个人，或合力或分头，与布雷登的死脱不了干系；再假设那个叫科利肖的人撞见了什么、足以坐实其中一人或两人的罪——怎么样？”

“怎么？”米钦顿问。

“布赖斯是学医的人，”杰蒂森说道，“对一个学医的人来说，像科利肖那样被除掉，是件轻而易举的事——而科利肖无疑正是那样被除掉的。你明白我的意思吗？”

“是啊——我还看得出来，布赖斯这家伙蒙起人来可是一把好手！”米钦顿喃喃道，“这案子我同他打过几回交道，我这会儿才——直到这会儿才——觉出他是一直拿我当冤大头耍呢！他显然是个城府深的角色——另外那人也是。”

===Original===
“I wanted to ask you that,” said Jettison. “Now, exactly who are these two?﻿—tell me about them﻿—both.”

“Not so much to tell,” answered Mitchington. “Harker’s a quiet old chap who lives in a little house over there﻿—just off that far corner of this Close. Said to be a retired tradesman, from London. Came here a few years ago, to settle down. Inoffensive, pleasant old chap. Potters about the town﻿—puts in his time as such old chaps do﻿—bit of reading at the libraries﻿—bit of gossip here and there﻿—you know the sort. Last man in the world I should have thought would have been mixed up in an affair of this sort!”

“And therefore all the more likely to be!” said Jettison. “Well﻿—the other?”

===Chinese===
“我正想问你这事，”杰蒂森说，“那么，这俩人究竟是谁？——跟我讲讲——两个人都讲。”

“倒没什么可讲的，”米钦顿答道，“哈克是个安安静静的老头儿，住在那边一座小房子里——就在这教堂区那一头角落外边。据说是个退了休的买卖人，从伦敦来的。几年前搬到这儿来安顿下来。是个不招惹是非、和和气气的老头儿。成天在城里东游西逛——像这类老头儿那样打发日子——在图书馆里读读书——到各处扯扯闲话——你晓得那种人。天底下我最想不到会卷进这种案子的人，就数他了！”

“正因为如此，才更有可能！”杰蒂森说，“那么——另外一个呢？”

===Original===
“Bryce was until the very day of Braden’s appearance, Ransford’s assistant,” continued Mitchington. “Been with Ransford about two years. Clever chap, undoubtedly, but certainly deep and, in a way, reserved, though he can talk plenty if he’s so minded and it’s to his own advantage. He left Ransford suddenly﻿—that very morning. I don’t know why. Since then he’s remained in the town. I’ve heard that he’s pretty keen on Ransford’s ward﻿—sister of that lad we saw tonight. I don’t know myself, if it’s true﻿—but I’ve wondered if that had anything to do with his leaving Ransford so suddenly.”

“Very likely,” said Jettison. They had crossed the Close by that time and come to a gas-lamp which stood at the entrance, and the detective pulled out his watch and glanced at it. “Ten past eleven,” he said. “You say you know this Bryce pretty well? Now, would it be too late﻿—if he’s up still﻿—to take a look at him! If you and he are on good terms, you could make an excuse. After what I’ve heard, I’d like to get at close quarters with this gentleman.”

“Easy enough,” assented Mitchington. “I’ve been there as late as this﻿—he’s one of the sort that never goes to bed before midnight. Come on!﻿—it’s close by. But﻿—not a word of where we’ve been. I’ll say I’ve dropped in to give him a bit of news. We’ll tell him about the jewel business﻿—and see how he takes it. And while we’re there﻿—size him up!”

===Chinese===
“直到布雷登出现的那一天为止，布赖斯一直是兰斯福德的助手，”米钦顿接着说，“跟兰斯福德有两年了。无疑是聪明人，但城府也深，某种意义上还不爱说话，不过只要他愿意、且对他自己有好处，他也能滔滔不绝。他是那天早上突然离开兰斯福德的。我不知道缘由。此后他一直留在城里。我听说他对兰斯福德的被监护人——就是咱们今晚见的那少年的姐姐——挺上心。真假我也不知道——但我曾琢磨过，他那么突然地离开兰斯福德，跟这事有没有干系。”

“很有可能，”杰蒂森说。他们这时已经穿过教堂区，走到入口处一盏煤气灯跟前，那侦探掏出怀表看了一眼。“十一点过十分，”他说，“你说你跟这布赖斯挺熟的？那么——要是他还没睡——这会儿登门，会不会太晚了点？去瞧瞧他！要是你跟他处得不错，你能找个借口。听了方才那一席话，我倒想凑到近处端详端详这位先生。”

“容易得很，”米钦顿附和道，“比这更晚的时辰我都去过他家——他是那种不到半夜不上床的人。走吧！——就在跟前。不过——咱们去过哪儿，半个字也不许漏。我就说我是顺道来给他透个消息的。咱们把珠宝那档子事告诉他——瞧他怎么个反应。趁咱们在那儿——好好掂量掂量他！”

===Original===
Mitchington was right in his description of Bryce’s habits﻿—Bryce rarely went to bed before one o’clock in the morning. He liked to sit up, reading. His favourite mental food was found in the lives of statesmen and diplomatists, most of them of the sort famous for trickery and chicanery﻿—he not only made a close study of the ways of these gentry but wrote down notes and abstracts of passages which particularly appealed to him. His lamp was burning when Mitchington and Jettison came in view of his windows﻿—but that night Bryce was doing no thinking about statecraft: his mind was fixed on his own affairs. He had lighted his fire on going home and for an hour had sat with his legs stretched out on the fender, carefully weighing things up. The event of the night had convinced him that he was at a critical phase of his present adventure, and it behoved him, as a good general, to review his forces.

The forestalling of his plans about the hiding-place in Paradise had upset Bryce’s schemes﻿—he had figured on being able to turn that secret, whatever it was, to his own advantage. It struck him now, as he meditated, that he had never known exactly what he expected to get out of that secret﻿—but he had hoped that it would have been something which would make a few more considerable and tightly-strung meshes in the net which he was endeavouring to weave around Ransford. Now he was faced by the fact that it was not going to yield anything in the way of help﻿—it was a secret no longer, and it had yielded nothing beyond the mere knowledge that John Braden, who was in reality John Brake, had carried the secret to Wrychester﻿—to reveal it in the proper quarter. That helped Bryce in no way﻿—so far as he could see. And therefore it was necessary to restate his case to himself; to take stock; to see where he stood﻿—and more than all, to put plainly before his own mind exactly what he wanted.

And just before Mitchington and the detective came up the path to his door, Bryce had put his notions into clear phraseology. His aim was definite﻿—he wanted to get Ransford completely into his power, through suspicion of Ransford’s guilt in the affairs of Braden and Collishaw. He wanted, at the same time, to have the means of exonerating him﻿—whether by fact or by craft﻿—so that, as an ultimate method of success for his own projects he would be able to go to Mary Bewery and say “Ransford’s very life is at my mercy: if I keep silence, he’s lost: if I speak, he’s saved: it’s now for you to say whether I’m to speak or hold my tongue﻿—and you’re the price I want for my speaking to save him!” It was in accordance with his views of human nature that Mary Bewery would accede to his terms: he had not known her and Ransford for nothing, and he was aware that she had a profound gratitude for her guardian, which might even be akin to a yet unawakened warmer feeling. The probability was that she would willingly sacrifice herself to save Ransford﻿—and Bryce cared little by what means he won her, fair or foul, so long as he was successful. So now, he said to himself, he must make a still more definite move against Ransford. He must strengthen and deepen the suspicions which the police already had: he must give them chapter and verse and supply them with information, and get Ransford into the tightest of corners, solely that, in order to win Mary Bewery, he might have the credit of pulling him out again. That, he felt certain, he could do﻿—if he could make a net in which to enclose Ransford he could also invent a two-edged sword which would cut every mesh of that net into fragments. That would be﻿—child’s play﻿—mere statecraft﻿—elementary diplomacy. But first﻿—to get Ransford fairly bottled up﻿—that was the thing! He determined to lose no more time﻿—and he was thinking of visiting Mitchington immediately after breakfast next morning when Mitchington knocked at his door.

===Chinese===
米钦顿对布赖斯起居习惯的描述一点不错——布赖斯极少在凌晨一点以前上床。他爱熬夜看书。他最对胃口的精神食粮，是政治家与外交官们的传记，其中大半是以权谋诡诈闻名的那一类——他不但仔细琢磨这些人物的手段，还把那些格外打动他的段落抄成笔记和摘录。米钦顿和杰蒂森望见他的窗口时，他的灯还亮着——可那一夜，布赖斯根本没在琢磨什么治国之术：他的心神全放在自己的事情上。他一回家就生了火，伸开两腿搁在炉挡上，坐了足足一个钟头，仔细地权衡着种种利害。今夜这一场变故让他确信，他眼下的这番冒险正到了一个紧要关头，他身为一名合格的统帅，理当检阅一番自己的兵力。
