他在「乐园」藏宝处一役被抢了先，这一来全套算盘便全被打乱了——他原盘算着，无论那秘密究竟是什么，总能拿来为己所用。此刻他一边琢磨，一边发觉自己其实从未想清楚到底指望从那秘密里得到什么——可他原本盼着，那会是某种能让他在兰斯福德周围慢慢织就的罗网上、再多上几道结实绷紧的网眼的东西。眼下摆在面前的事实却是：这事再不能给他任何助力了——那已经不成其为秘密，而且除了让他晓得约翰·布雷登——也就是真名叫约翰·布雷克的那人——曾把这秘密带到赖彻斯特、要在适当之处把它揭开来之外，什么也没留下。这事帮不了布赖斯——至少他想不出怎么帮。因此，他必须把自己的案情向自己重新陈述一遍；清点一番；看看自己身处何地——更要紧的，是清清楚楚地把自己到底要什么，摆到自己的心头。

就在米钦顿和那侦探沿着小径走到他家门口之前，布赖斯已经把自己的想法化作了清楚的措辞。他的目标十分明确——他要借布雷登和科利肖两桩案子，把兰斯福德涉嫌有罪的疑心攥在手里，使兰斯福德彻底落入他的掌心。与此同时，他还要握住替兰斯福德洗脱罪名的门径——无论是凭借事实还是凭借手腕——这样一来，作为他自己那盘棋最终取胜的手段，他便能走到玛丽·比尤里面前说：“兰斯福德的身家性命，捏在我手心里：我若不开口，他便完了；我一开口，他便得救；现在该由你来定，我是开口还是闭嘴——而我要的、替我开口救他的代价，就是你！”依他对人性的看法，玛丽·比尤里是会答应他的条件的：他和她、和兰斯福德打交道不是白打的，他深知她对那位监护人怀着深深的感激，这份感激甚至可能近乎一种尚未觉醒的、更炽热的情愫。十之八九，她会甘愿牺牲自己来救兰斯福德——而布赖斯全不在乎用什么法子赢取她，光明也好，卑鄙也罢，只要能成事便行。所以这会儿，他暗自盘算，他必须对兰斯福德再走一步更为明确的棋。他必须把警方已有的疑心加深加重：他得给他们确凿详尽的凭据，源源不断地递上情报，把兰斯福德逼到最窄的死胡同里去，唯一的目的，便是为了赢取玛丽·比尤里，好让自己担起把他从那胡同里搭救出来的功劳。这事，他笃定自己办得到——他既能织一张把兰斯福德兜进去的网，也就能造出一把双刃剑，把那网上的每一根网眼都斩成碎片。那不过是——小孩子的把戏——区区权术——入门级的外交手腕罢了。可头一桩——得先把兰斯福德结结实实地困进瓮里——这才是要紧的！他决心不再耽搁——正盘算着明早一吃过早饭便去拜访米钦顿，米钦顿便来敲他的门了。

===Original===
Bryce was rarely taken back, and on seeing Mitchington and a companion, he forthwith invited them into his parlour, put out his whisky and cigars, and pressed both on them as if their late call were a matter of usual occurrence. And when he had helped both to a drink, he took one himself, and tumbler in hand, dropped into his easy chair again.

“We saw your light, doctor﻿—so I took the liberty of dropping into tell you a bit of news,” observed the inspector. “But I haven’t introduced my friend﻿—this is Detective-Sergeant Jettison, of the Yard﻿—we’ve got him down about this business﻿—must have help, you know.”

Bryce gave the detective a half-sharp, half-careless look and nodded.

“Mr. Jettison will have abundant opportunities for the exercise of his talents!” he observed in his best cynical manner. “I dare say he’s found that out already.”

===Chinese===
布赖斯很少猝不及防，一见到米钦顿和同伴，他当即请他们进了起居室，端出威士忌和雪茄，一个劲儿地劝他们享用，仿佛这样深夜造访本是寻常之事。等给两位都斟上了酒，自己也来了一杯，他便手执平底杯，重新跌坐回自己的安乐椅里。

“我们瞧见您的灯还亮着，医生——所以我冒昧进来给您透个消息，”探长说道，“不过我还没介绍我的朋友——这位是苏格兰场的杰蒂森侦探警长——为了这档子事，我们把他从伦敦请了下来——您知道，总得有个帮手。”

布赖斯朝那侦探投去一眼，半是锐利、半是漫不经心，点了点头。

“杰蒂森先生将有大把的机会来施展他的才干！”他用他最地道的那副冷嘲热讽的腔调说道，“我敢说，这一点他自己已经发觉了。”

===Original===
“Not an easy affair, sir, to be sure,” assented Jettison. “Complicated!”

“Highly so!” agreed Bryce. He yawned, and glanced at the inspector. “What’s your news, Mitchington?” he asked, almost indifferently.

“Oh, well!” answered Mitchington. “As the *Herald*’s published tomorrow you’ll see it in there, doctor﻿—I’ve supplied an account for this week’s issue; just a short one﻿—but I thought you’d like to know. You’ve heard of the famous jewel robbery at the Duke’s, some years ago? Yes?﻿—well, we’ve found all the whole bundle tonight﻿—buried in Paradise! And how do you think the secret came out?”

“No good at guessing,” said Bryce.

“It came out,” continued Mitchington, “through a man who, with Braden﻿—Braden, mark you!﻿—got in possession of it﻿—it’s a long story﻿—and, with Braden, was going to reveal it to the Duke that very day Braden was killed. This man waited until this very morning and then told his Grace﻿—his Grace came with him to us this afternoon, and tonight we made a search and found﻿—everything! Buried﻿—there in Paradise! Dug ’em up, doctor!”

===Chinese===
“确实不是一桩轻松的案子，先生，这是实在的，”杰蒂森附和道，“盘根错节哪！”

“错综复杂极了！”布赖斯附和道。他打了个哈欠，瞟了探长一眼。“你带来什么消息，米钦顿？”他几乎漫不经心地问。

“嗳，是这样！”米钦顿答道，“《先驱报》（*Herald*）明天要出的，您明儿就能在那上头瞧见，医生——我给这一周的报纸写了篇稿子；不长——可我想您还是乐意知道的。几年前公爵府上那桩轰动一时的珠宝盗案，您听说过吧？听说过？——好哇，今儿夜里我们把那一整包东西全找着了——就埋在「乐园」里！您猜这秘密是怎么露出来的？”

“我可不擅猜谜，”布赖斯说。

“是这样露出来的，”米钦顿接着说，“有个人，跟布雷登——您听着，是布雷登！——一道得了这个秘密——说来话长——本来和布雷登打算，就在布雷登被害的那天，去把这事儿告诉公爵。这人一直等到今儿早上才禀明了公爵大人——公爵大人今天下午陪他到了我们这儿，今夜我们就搜了一回，结果——一应俱全！全埋着——就在「乐园」里头！把它们挖出来啦，医生！”

===Original===
Bryce showed no great interest. He took a leisurely sip at his liquor and set down the glass and pulled out his cigarette case. The two men, watching him narrowly, saw that his fingers were steady as rocks as he struck the match.

“Yes,” he said as he threw the match away. “I saw you busy.”

In spite of himself Mitchington could not repress a start nor a glance at Jettison. But Jettison was as imperturbable as Bryce himself, and Mitchington raised a forced laugh.

“You did!” he said, incredulously. “And we thought we had it all to ourselves! How did you come to know, doctor?”

“Young Bewery told me what was going on,” replied Bryce, “so I took a look at you. And I fetched old Harker to take a look, too. We all watched you﻿—the boy, Harker, and I﻿—out of sheer curiosity, of course. We saw you get up the parcel. But, naturally, I didn’t know what was in it﻿—till now.”

===Chinese===
布赖斯没显出多大兴致。他不慌不忙地抿了一口酒，放下杯子，掏出烟盒。那两人紧紧盯着他，只见他划火柴时，手指稳如磐石。

“是啊，”他一面把火柴扔掉一面说，“我瞧见你们忙活来着。”

米钦顿不由自主地猛一激灵，忍不住朝杰蒂森瞟了一眼。可杰蒂森和布赖斯本人一样镇定自若，米钦顿只好挤出一声干笑。

“您瞧见了！”他不相信地说，“我们还当这事只有我们几个知道呢！您怎么晓得的，医生？”

“小比尤里把你们的动静告诉了我，”布赖斯答道，“所以我去瞧了瞧你们。我还把老哈克也叫去瞧了一瞧。我们三个——那孩子、哈克、还有我——都看着你们呢——当然，纯粹是好奇。我们瞧见你们把那包东西挖出来了。不过，那包里头是什么，我自然是不晓得的——直到这会儿。”

===Original===
Mitchington, thoroughly taken aback by this candid statement, was at a loss for words, and again he glanced at Jettison. But Jettison gave no help, and Mitchington fell back on himself.

“So you fetched old Harker?” he said. “What﻿—what for, doctor? If one may ask, you know.”

Bryce made a careless gesture with his cigarette.

“Oh﻿—old Harker’s deeply interested in what’s going on,” he answered. “And as young Bewery drew my attention to your proceedings, why, I thought I’d draw Harker’s. And Harker was﻿—interested.”

Mitchington hesitated before saying more. But eventually he risked a leading question.

===Chinese===
米钦顿被这番坦白惊得完全措手不及，一时竟不知说什么好，又朝杰蒂森瞟了一眼。可杰蒂森并不替他解围，米钦顿只好靠自己应付。

“这么说，您是把老哈克也叫去了？”他说，“那——那是图什么，医生？要是可以问的话，您知道。”

布赖斯夹着烟，漫不经心地一摆手。

“嗳——老哈克对眼下这些事可上心得很呢，”他答道，“既然是小比尤里把你们的举动引到了我眼里，那么，我想我不妨也把它引到哈克眼里去。哈克嘛——果然上心了。”

米钦顿在往下说之前迟疑了一下。可他到底还是冒险问了一句诱导性的话。

===Original===
“Any special reason why he should be, doctor?” he asked.

Bryce put his thumbs in the armholes of his waistcoat and looked half-lazily at his questioner.

“Do you know who old Harker really is?” he inquired.

“No!” answered Mitchington. “I know nothing about him﻿—except that he’s said to be a retired tradesman, from London, who settled down here some time ago.”

===Chinese===
“他上心，总有什么特别的缘故吧，医生？”他问。

布赖斯把两个大拇指插进马甲的袖洞，半带慵懒地望着提问的人。

“你可知道，老哈克究竟是什么人？”他反问道。

“不知道！”米钦顿答道，“我对他一无所知——只听说他是个退了休的买卖人，从伦敦来的，前些时候在这儿安顿下来。”

===Original===
Bryce suddenly turned on Jettison.

“Do you?” he asked.

“I, sir!” exclaimed Jettison. “I don’t know this gentleman﻿—at all!”

Bryce laughed﻿—with his usual touch of cynical sneering.

“I’ll tell you﻿—now﻿—who old Harker is, Mitchington,” he said. “You may as well know. I thought Mr. Jettison might recognize the name. Harker is no retired London tradesman﻿—he’s a retired member of your profession, Mr. Jettison. He was in his day one of the smartest men in the service of your department. Only he’s transposed his name﻿—ask them at the Yard if they remember Harker Simpson? That seems to startle you, Mitchington! Well, as you’re here, perhaps I’d better startle you a bit more.”

===Chinese===
布赖斯忽地转向杰蒂森。

“你呢？”他问。

“我吗，先生！”杰蒂森叫道，“这位先生——我压根儿不认得！”

布赖斯笑了起来——带着他惯常那一点冷嘲讥诮的味道。

“我这就告诉你们——这会儿——老哈克是什么人，米钦顿，”他说，“你们也该知道知道。我本以为杰蒂森先生或许会认得这个名字。哈克才不是什么退了休的伦敦买卖人——他是贵同行里退下来的人，杰蒂森先生。当年他是你们那一行里最精明能干的人物之一。只不过他把名字颠倒了一下——你去问苏格兰场那些人，看他们记不记得哈克·辛普森（Harker Simpson）？瞧这样子，这话叫你吃了一惊，米钦顿！也罢，既然你们都来了，我不妨再叫你们多吃一惊。”

## XIX — The Subtlety of the Devil / 第十九章　魔鬼的狡黠

===Original===
There was a sudden determination and alertness in Bryce’s last words which contrasted strongly, and even strangely, with the almost cynical indifference that had characterized him since his visitors came in, and the two men recognized it and glanced questioningly at each other. There was an alteration, too, in his manner; instead of lounging lazily in his chair, as if he had no other thought than of personal ease, he was now sitting erect, looking sharply from one man to the other; his whole attitude, bearing, speech seemed to indicate that he had suddenly made up his mind to adopt some definite course of action.

“I’ll tell you more!” he repeated. “And, since you’re here—now!”

Mitchington, who felt a curious uneasiness, gave Jettison another glance. And this time it was Jettison who spoke.

“I should say,” he remarked quietly, “knowing what I’ve gathered of the matter, that we ought to be glad of any information Dr. Bryce can give us.”

“Oh, to be sure!” assented Mitchington. “You know more, then, doctor?”

===Chinese===
布赖斯最后这几句话里，忽然带上了一种决断与警醒，与他自打两位来客进门以来那副近乎玩世不恭的冷淡作派形成了强烈的、甚至可以说古怪的对照；两个来访者都察觉到了这一点，交换了一个探询的眼神。他的神情也变了；先前他还懒洋洋地瘫在椅子里，仿佛除了自身的安逸再无别的念想，此刻却坐得笔直，目光锐利地在这两人之间来回扫视；他整个人的姿态、做派、谈吐，似乎都在表明：他已猛地下定决心，要采取某种明确的行动步骤。

“我还要再告诉你们一些！”他重复道，“既然你们在这儿——眼下就在！”

米钦顿心头莫名生出一阵不安，又朝杰蒂森瞥了一眼。这一回，开口的是杰蒂森。

“我倒要说，”他不紧不慢地说道，“就我对这桩案子所了解到的情况来看，布赖斯医生能提供给我们的任何线索，我们都该乐于听取。”

“哦，那是当然！”米钦顿附和道，“那么，医生，你知道的更多？”

===Original===
Bryce motioned his visitors to draw their chairs nearer to his, and when he spoke it was in the low, concentrated tones of a man who means business—and confidential business.

“Now look here, Mitchington,” he said, “and you, too, Mr. Jettison, as you’re on this job—I’m going to talk straight to both of you. And to begin with, I’ll make a bold assertion—I know more of this Wrychester Paradise mystery—involving the deaths of both Braden and Collishaw, than any man living—because, though you don’t know it, Mitchington, I’ve gone right into it. And I’ll tell you in confidence why I went into it—I want to marry Dr. Ransford’s ward, Miss Bewery!”

Bryce accompanied this candid admission with a look which seemed to say: Here we are, three men of the world, who know what things are—we understand each other! And while Jettison merely nodded comprehendingly, Mitchington put his thoughts into words.

“To be sure, doctor, to be sure!” he said. “And accordingly—what’s their affair, is yours! Of course!”

===Chinese===
布赖斯示意两位来客把椅子挪近一些；待他开口时，那低沉而专注的语调，分明是一个要谈正事——而且是机密正事——的人。

“我说，米钦顿，”他道，“还有你，杰蒂森先生，既然你们俩都插手了这桩差事——我要跟你们两位打开天窗说亮话。首先，我要下一句大胆的断言——关于这桩赖彻斯特「乐园」之谜，也就是牵涉到布雷登和科利肖两人之死的那桩案子，我比世上任何活人都知道得多——因为，米钦顿，你虽不知情，我可是把这事彻头彻尾地查过了一遍。至于我为什么要查它，我可以私下告诉你们——我想娶兰斯福德医生的被监护人比尤里小姐！”

布赖斯说出这番坦率的自白时，配上了那样一种神情，仿佛在说：咱们这儿坐着三个见过世面的人，都懂得人情世故——彼此心照不宣！杰蒂森只是心领神会地点了点头，米钦顿却把心里话说了出来。

“那是当然，医生，当然！”他说，“所以说——他们的事，也就是你的事！理所当然！”

===Original===
“Something like that,” assented Bryce. “Naturally no man wishes to marry unless he knows as much as he can get to know about the woman he wants, her family, her antecedents—and all that. Now, pretty nearly everybody in Wrychester who knows them, knows that there’s a mystery about Dr. Ransford and his two wards—it’s been talked of, no end, amongst the old dowagers and gossips of the Close, particularly—you know what they are! Miss Bewery herself, and her brother, young Dick, in a lesser degree, know there’s a mystery. And if there’s one man in the world who knows the secret, it’s Ransford. And, up to now, Ransford won’t tell—he won’t even tell Miss Bewery. I know that she’s asked him—he keeps up an obstinate silence. And so—I determined to find things out for myself.”

“Aye—and when did you start on that little game, now, doctor?” asked Mitchington. “Was it before, or since, this affair developed?”

“In a really serious way—since,” replied Bryce. “What happened on the day of Braden’s death made me go thoroughly into the whole matter. Now, what did happen? I’ll tell you frankly, now, Mitchington, that when we talked once before about this affair, I didn’t tell you all I might have told. I’d my reasons for reticence. But now I’ll give you full particulars of what happened that morning within my knowledge—pay attention, both of you, and you’ll see how one thing fits into another. That morning, about half-past nine, Ransford left his surgery and went across the Close. Not long after he’d gone, this man Braden came to the door, and asked me if Dr. Ransford was in? I said he wasn’t—he’d just gone out, and I showed the man in which direction. He said he’d once known a Dr. Ransford, and went away. A little later, I followed. Near the entrance of Paradise, I saw Ransford leaving the west porch of the Cathedral. He was undeniably in a state of agitation—pale, nervous. He didn’t see me. I went on and met Varner, who told me of the accident. I went with him to the foot of St. Wrytha’s Stair and found the man who had recently called at the surgery. He died just as I reached him. I sent for you. When you came, I went back to the surgery—I found Ransford there in a state of most unusual agitation—he looked like a man who has had a terrible shock. So much for these events. Put them together.”

Bryce paused awhile, as if marshalling his facts.

===Chinese===
“差不多就是这个意思，”布赖斯附和道，“一个男人，自然非得把自己想娶的那个女人——她的家世、出身、种种情形——尽可能地打听清楚了，才肯娶她。如今，赖彻斯特凡是认识他们的人，几乎都知道兰斯福德医生和他那两个被监护人身上有桩蹊跷事——这事在教堂区那些老命妇和长舌妇中间，早就被嚼烂了舌根——你是晓得她们那帮人的！比尤里小姐本人，还有她弟弟小迪克，程度上轻些，也知道有这么一桩蹊跷。倘若这世上只有一个人知道这其中的秘密，那就是兰斯福德。可是，直到如今，兰斯福德就是不肯说——他甚至连比尤里小姐都不肯告诉。我知道她已经问过他——他却一味固执地保持沉默。于是——我决心自己把事情查个水落石出。”

“哦——那你是什么时候开始玩这一手的呢，医生？”米钦顿问，“是在这桩命案发生之前，还是之后？”

“真正认真地查——是在那之后，”布赖斯答道，“布雷登死的那天所发生的事，促使我把整桩事情彻查了一遍。那么，那天究竟发生了什么？米钦顿，我如今坦白告诉你，上回咱们谈起这桩案子时，我并没有把本可以说的都告诉你。我当时有所保留，自有我的道理。可现在，我要把那天早晨据我所知发生的一切，原原本本地讲给你们听——你俩仔细听着，便会明白一桩桩事是如何环环相扣的。那天早晨，约莫九点半钟，兰斯福德离开诊室，穿过教堂区去了。他走后不久，布雷登这个人来到门口，问我兰斯福德医生在不在。我说不在——他刚出去，并指给他医生走的方向。他说他从前认识一位兰斯福德医生，便走开了。过了一会儿，我跟了出去。在「乐园」入口附近，我看见兰斯福德正从大教堂的西门廊里走出来。他无可争辩地处于一种激动不安的状态——脸色发白，神情紧张。他没有看见我。我继续往前走，遇见了瓦纳，他告诉了我那桩意外。我随他来到圣赖莎阶梯脚下，找到了那个刚才还到诊室来过的男人。我赶到他身边时，他刚好咽了气。我派人去叫你。你来之后，我回到诊室——发现兰斯福德也在那里，激动得异乎寻常——他那副模样，活像一个刚受了可怕打击的人。这些便是当时种种事件的全貌。你把它们串起来看吧。”

布赖斯停了一阵子，像是在梳理自己的头绪。

===Original===
“Now, after that,” he continued presently, “I began to investigate matters myself—for my own satisfaction. And very soon I found out certain things—which I’ll summarize, briefly, because some of my facts are doubtless known to you already. First of all—the man who came here as John Braden was, in reality, one John Brake. He was at one time manager of a branch of a well-known London banking company. He appropriated money from them under apparently mysterious circumstances of which I, as yet, know nothing; he was prosecuted, convicted, and sentenced to ten years’ penal servitude. And those two wards of Ransford’s, Mary and Richard Bewery, as they are called, are, in reality, Mary and Richard Brake—his children.”

“You’ve established that as a fact?” asked Jettison, who was listening with close attention. “It’s not a surmise on your part?”

Bryce hesitated before replying to this question. After all, he reflected, it was a surmise. He could not positively prove his assertion.

===Chinese===
“在那之后，”他过了一会儿又接着说，“我便开始自己着手调查——为的是满足我自己。而且很快我就查清了一些事情——我将简明扼要地概括一下，因为我所掌握的事实中，有些无疑你们已经知道了。首先——那个以约翰·布雷登之名来到此地的人，实际上是一个叫约翰·布雷克的人。他曾是伦敦一家知名银行公司某分行的经理。他盗用了那家银行的钱，情形乍看上去颇为离奇，至于其中详情，我至今仍一无所知；他遭到起诉，被定罪，判处十年苦役。而兰斯福德的两个被监护人——所谓玛丽·比尤里和理查德·比尤里，实际上就是玛丽·布雷克和理查德·布雷克——是他的孩子。”

“你把这查证成既定事实了？”正在凝神细听的杰蒂森问道，“这并非你的揣测？”

布赖斯回答这个问题之前犹豫了一下。说到底，他暗想，这终究是个揣测。他无法确凿地证实自己的断言。

===Original===
“Well,” he answered after a moment’s thought, “I’ll qualify that by saying that from the evidence I have, and from what I know, I believe it to be an indisputable fact. What I do know of fact, hard, positive fact, is this:—John Brake married a Mary Bewery at the parish church of Braden Medworth, near Barthorpe, in Leicestershire: I’ve seen the entry in the register with my own eyes. His best man, who signed the register as a witness, was Mark Ransford. Brake and Ransford, as young men, had been in the habit of going to Braden Medworth to fish; Mary Bewery was governess at the vicarage there. It was always supposed she would marry Ransford; instead, she married Brake, who, of course, took her off to London. Of their married life, I know nothing. But within a few years, Brake was in trouble, for the reason I have told you. He was arrested—and Harker was the man who arrested him.”

“Dear me!” exclaimed Mitchington. “Now, if I’d only known—”

“You’ll know a lot before I’m through,” said Bryce. “Now, Harker, of course, can tell a lot—yet it’s unsatisfying. Brake could make no defence—but his counsel threw out strange hints and suggestions—all to the effect that Brake had been cruelly and wickedly deceived—in fact, as it were, trapped into doing what he did. And—by a man whom he’d trusted as a close friend. So much came to Harker’s ears—but no more, and on that particular point I’ve no light. Go on from that to Brake’s private affairs. At the time of his arrest he had a wife and two very young children. Either just before, or at, or immediately after his arrest they completely disappeared—and Brake himself utterly refused to say one single word about them. Harker asked if he could do anything—Brake’s answer was that no one was to concern himself. He preserved an obstinate silence on that point. The clergyman in whose family Mrs. Brake had been governess saw Brake, after his conviction—Brake would say nothing to him. Of Mrs. Brake, nothing more is known—to me at any rate. What was known at the time is this—Brake communicated to all who came in contact with him, just then, the idea of a man who has been cruelly wronged and deceived, who takes refuge in sullen silence, and who is already planning and cherishing—revenge!”

===Chinese===
“那么，”他想了想答道，“我要把这话修正一下：根据我所掌握的证据，根据我所了解的情形，我相信这是一桩无可争辩的事实。而我确确实实知道的、铁一般的、确凿的事实是：——约翰·布雷克在莱斯特郡巴索普附近的布雷登梅德沃斯教区教堂，娶了一位玛丽·比尤里：我是亲眼在登记簿上看到那条记录的。他的男傧相，也就是在登记簿上签字作证的人，正是马克·兰斯福德。布雷克和兰斯福德年轻时，惯常去布雷登梅德沃斯钓鱼；玛丽·比尤里当时是那座牧师宅邸的家庭女教师。大家一向以为她会嫁给兰斯福德；结果她却嫁给了布雷克，布雷克自然就把她带到了伦敦。至于他们婚后的生活，我一无所知。可没过几年，布雷克便惹上了麻烦，原因我方才已经说过。他被捕了——而逮捕他的人，正是哈克。”

“我的天！”米钦顿失声叫道，“当初我要是知道——”

“等我把话说完，你知道的会多得多，”布赖斯说，“哈克当然能讲出不少东西——可叫人不满足。布雷克无法为自己辩护——可他的律师却抛出了些古怪的暗示和影射——全都是那个意思：布雷克遭到了残忍而恶毒的欺骗——事实上，简直可以说是被人设了套，才做出那等事来。而且——是被一个他当作挚友信任的人。这些话传到了哈克耳朵里——但也仅止于此，至于那关键的一点，我并无任何线索。由此再往下，看看布雷克的私事。他被捕时，已有妻室和两个很小的孩子。就在他被捕之前、当时、或紧接着之后，妻儿便彻底失踪了——而布雷克本人，对这事死活不肯吐露半个字。哈克问他能不能帮上什么忙——布雷克的回答是，谁也不必为此操心。他在这一点上固执地保持沉默。布雷克太太当过家庭女教师的那户人家里的牧师，在布雷克定罪之后见过他——布雷克对他什么也不肯说。至于布雷克太太，再无任何消息——至少我不知道。当时为人所知的是这样——布雷克在当时一切与他接触的人眼中，都留下了这样一个印象：他是个蒙受了残酷冤屈与欺骗的人，躲进阴沉的沉默里寻求庇护，而且已经在暗中筹谋、酝酿着——报复！”

===Original===
“Aye, aye!” muttered Mitchington. “Revenge?—just so!”

“Brake, then,” continued Bryce, “goes off to his term of penal servitude, and so disappears—until he reappears here in Wrychester. Leave him for a moment, and go back. And—it’s a going back, no doubt, to supposition and to theory—but there’s reason in what I shall advance. We know—beyond doubt—that Brake had been tricked and deceived, in some money matter, by some man—some mysterious man—whom he referred to as having been his closest friend. We know, too, that there was extraordinary mystery in the disappearance of his wife and children. Now, from all that has been found out, who was Brake’s closest friend? Ransford! And of Ransford, at that time, there’s no trace. He, too, disappeared—that’s a fact which I’ve established. Years later, he reappears—here at Wrychester, where he’s bought a practice. Eventually he has two young people, who are represented as his wards, come to live with him. Their name is Bewery. The name of the young woman whom John Brake married was Bewery. What’s the inference? That their mother’s dead—that they’re known under her maiden name: that they, without a shadow of doubt, are John Brake’s children. And that leads up to my theory—which I’ll now tell you in confidence—if you wish for it.”

“It’s what I particularly wish for,” observed Jettison quietly. “The very thing!”

===Chinese===
“哎，哎！”米钦顿喃喃道，“报复？——正是如此！”

“那么，布雷克，”布赖斯接着说，“便去服他的苦役刑期，就此销声匿迹——直到他又在此地赖彻斯特现身。先把他搁下，咱们回过头去看看。这——无疑是一种回溯，回到了揣测和推断上——可我下面要讲的，自有其道理。我们确知——毫无疑问——布雷克曾在某桩钱财事务上，被某个人——某个神秘的人——所设的圈套欺骗了；而此人，据他所说，曾是他最亲密的朋友。我们还知道，他的妻儿失踪一事，其中有着非同寻常的蹊跷。那么，从已查明的一切来看，布雷克最亲密的朋友是谁？是兰斯福德！而那个时期的兰斯福德，却杳无踪迹。他也失踪了——这是我已经查证属实的事实。多年之后，他才重新露面——就在此地赖彻斯特，在这儿盘下了一家诊所。最终，他把两个年轻人接来同住，对外说是他的被监护人。他们姓比尤里。而约翰·布雷克当年娶的那个年轻女子，便姓比尤里。那么，由此可作何推断？说明他们的母亲已经不在了——说明他们用的是母亲的娘家姓：说明他们毫无疑问、正是约翰·布雷克的孩子。这就引到了我那套推断上——我现在便私下讲给你们听——如果你们想听的话。”

“这正是我格外想听的，”杰蒂森平静地说，“求之不得！”

===Original===
“Then, it’s this,” said Bryce. “Ransford was the close friend who tricked and deceived Brake. He probably tricked him in some money affair, and deceived him in his domestic affairs. I take it that Ransford ran away with Brake’s wife, and that Brake, sooner than air all his grievance to the world, took it silently and began to concoct his ideas of revenge. I put the whole thing this way. Ransford ran away with Mrs. Brake and the two children—mere infants—and disappeared. Brake, when he came out of prison, went abroad—possibly with the idea of tracking them. Meanwhile, as is quite evident, he engaged in business and did well. He came back to England as John Braden, and, for the reason of which you’re aware, he paid a visit to Wrychester, utterly unaware that anyone known to him lived here. Now, try to reconstruct what happened. He looks round the Close that morning. He sees the name of Dr. Mark Ransford on the brass plate of a surgery door. He goes to the surgery, asks a question, makes a remark, goes away. What is the probable sequence of events? He meets Ransford near the Cathedral—where Ransford certainly was. They recognize each other—most likely they turn aside, go up to that gallery as a quiet place, to talk—there is an altercation—blows—somehow or other, probably from accident, Braden is thrown through that open doorway, to his death. And—Collishaw saw what happened!”

Bryce was watching his listeners, turning alternately from one to the other. But it needed little attention on his part to see that theirs was already closely strained; each man was eagerly taking in all that he said and suggested. And he went on emphasizing every point as he made it.

===Chinese===
“那么，是这样的，”布赖斯说，“兰斯福德便是那个欺骗、坑害了布雷克的挚友。他很可能在某桩钱财事务上坑了布雷克，又在布雷克的家事上骗了他。我的看法是：兰斯福德拐走了布雷克的妻子，而布雷克宁可把满腹冤屈咽在肚里，也不愿闹得满城风雨，便默默忍受下来，开始盘算他的复仇大计。我把整件事这样来还原。兰斯福德带着布雷克太太和那两个孩子——那时还都是襁褓婴儿——私奔了，从此销声匿迹。布雷克出狱之后，便去了国外——或许正是存着追踪他们的念头。与此同时——这一点十分明显——他做了生意，而且做得颇为得意。他以约翰·布雷登之名回到英国，又因为你们都清楚的那个缘故，来到了赖彻斯特，全然不知此地住着任何他相识的人。现在，试着还原一下当时的情形。那天早晨，他在教堂区四下打量。他看见一扇诊室门的铜牌上，写着马克·兰斯福德医生的名字。他走进诊室，问了一个问题，说了一句话，转身离去。接下来可能发生了什么？他在大教堂附近遇见了兰斯福德——兰斯福德当时确实在那儿。两人彼此认了出来——多半是闪到一旁，登上那处回廊层，图个清净地方说话——随即起了争执——动了手——不知怎的，也许是出于意外，布雷登被从那扇敞开的门口甩了出去，摔成了致命伤。而且——科利肖目睹了事情经过！”

布赖斯注视着他的两位听众，目光不时从这一个移到那一个身上。但他几乎不必费心去看，便知道他们的注意力已经紧绷到了极点；两个人都在急切地吞咽着他所说的、所暗示的每一句话。他便继续一条一条地，把每一个要点都加重了语气。

===Original===
“Collishaw saw what happened?” he repeated. “That, of course, is theory—supposition. But now we pass from theory back to actual fact. I’ll tell you something now, Mitchington, which you’ve never heard of, I’m certain. I made it in my way, after Collishaw’s death, to get some information, secretly, from his widow, who’s a fairly shrewd, intelligent woman for her class. Now, the widow, in looking over her husband’s effects, in a certain drawer in which he kept various personal matters, came across the deposit book of a Friendly Society of which Collishaw had been a member for some years. It appears that he, Collishaw, was something of a saving man, and every year he managed to put by a bit of money out of his wages, and twice or thrice in the year he took these savings—never very much; merely a pound or two—to this Friendly Society, which, it seems, takes deposits in that way from its members. Now, in this book is an entry—I saw it—which shows that only two days before his death, Collishaw paid fifty pounds—fifty pounds, mark you!—into the Friendly Society. Where should Collishaw get fifty pounds, all of a sudden? He was a mason’s labourer, earning at the very outside twenty-six or eight shillings a week. According to his wife, there was no one to leave him a legacy. She never heard of his receipt of this money from any source. But—there’s the fact! What explains it? My theory—that the rumour that Collishaw, with a pint too much ale in him, had hinted that he could say something about Braden’s death if he chose, had reached Braden’s assailant; that he had made it his business to see Collishaw and had paid him that fifty pounds as hush-money—and, later, had decided to rid himself of Collishaw altogether, as he undoubtedly did, by poison.”

Once more Bryce paused—and once more the two listeners showed their attention by complete silence.

“Now we come to the question—how was Collishaw poisoned?” continued Bryce. “For poisoned he was, without doubt. Here we go back to theory and supposition once more. I haven’t the least doubt that the hydrocyanic acid which caused his death was taken by him in a pill—a pill that was in that box which they found on him, Mitchington, and showed me. But that particular pill, though precisely similar in appearance, could not be made up of the same ingredients which were in the other pills. It was probably a thickly coated pill which contained the poison;—in solution of course. The coating would melt almost as soon as the man had swallowed it—and death would result instantaneously. Collishaw, you may say, was condemned to death when he put that box of pills in his waistcoat pocket. It was mere chance, mere luck, as to when the exact moment of death came to him. There had been six pills in that box—there were five left. So Collishaw picked out the poisoned pill—first! It might have been delayed till the sixth dose, you see—but he was doomed.”

===Chinese===
“科利肖目睹了事情经过？”他重复道，“这当然只是推断——是揣测。可现在，我们要从推断回到确凿的事实上来。米钦顿，我现在要告诉你一桩事，我敢说你从没听说过。科利肖死后，我设法悄悄地从他遗孀那里弄到了一些消息——他那位遗孀，就她那个阶层而言，算是个相当精明、相当有脑子的女人。这位遗孀在清理亡夫遗物时，在他存放各种私人杂件的一个抽屉里，翻出了一本友谊会（Friendly Society）的存款簿——科利肖加入这家友谊会已有好些年了。看来科利肖这人倒颇有些储蓄的习惯，每年总能从工钱里攒下一点钱，一年里总要拿上两三回——数目都不大，不过一两英镑——存进这家友谊会；这家友谊会，似乎便是以这种办法接收会员存款的。可就在这本存款簿上，有一条记录——我亲眼见过——上面显示，就在科利肖死前两天，他往友谊会里存了五十英镑——五十英镑，注意！——科利肖从哪儿一下子能弄来五十英镑？他不过是个石匠帮工，每周工钱撑死也不过二十六到二十八先令。据他妻子说，并无任何亲属会留遗产给他。这笔钱的来路，她从任何渠道都未曾听说。可是——事实就摆在那儿！作何解释？我的推断是——科利肖多灌了几品脱黄汤之后，曾口出风声，说他只要愿意，就能道出一些关于布雷登之死的内情；这话传到了袭击布雷登的那人耳中；那人便专程去找了科利肖，把那五十英镑当作封口费付给了他——再后来，又决定索性把科利肖这个祸根除掉，正如他毫无疑问所做的那样，用的手段是下毒。”

布赖斯又停了下来——两位听众再一次以彻底的沉默，表示他们正全神贯注。

“现在我们来到这个问题——科利肖是怎么中的毒？”布赖斯接着说，“因为他是中了毒，这一点毫无疑问。这一下我们又要回到推断和揣测上来了。我丝毫不怀疑，致他死命的那种氢氰酸，是他随一颗药丸服下的——那颗药丸，就装在米钦顿从他身上搜出、拿给我看的那个药盒里。但那一颗药丸，尽管外表与其余几颗一模一样，其成分却绝不可能与其余几颗相同。那很可能是一颗裹着厚厚糖衣的药丸，里头藏着毒药——自然是以溶液的形态。那层糖衣，几乎在人咽下药丸的同时便会化开——随即当场毙命。可以说，科利肖把那盒药丸揣进马甲口袋的那一刻，便已被判了死刑。至于死神究竟在哪一刻降临，不过是碰巧，不过是运气罢了。那盒药丸原本有六颗——剩下五颗。也就是说，科利肖偏偏头一颗便挑中了那颗毒药丸！你瞧，这本可以拖到第六次服药才发作的——可他注定难逃一死。”

===Original===
Mitchington showed a desire to speak, and Bryce paused.

“What about what Ransford said before the Coroner?” asked Mitchington. “He demanded certain information about the postmortem, you know, which, he said, ought to have shown that there was nothing poisonous in those pills.”

“Pooh!” exclaimed Bryce contemptuously. “Mere bluff! Of such a pill as that I’ve described there’d be no trace but the sugar coating—and the poison. I tell you, I haven’t the least doubt that that was how the poison was administered. It was easy. And—who is there that would know how easily it could be administered but—a medical man?”

===Chinese===
米钦顿露出想说话的样子，布赖斯便停了下来。

“兰斯福德在验尸官面前说的那番话，又怎么讲？”米钦顿问，“你知道的，他要求公开某些关于尸检的情况，他说，那些情况本该表明那些药丸里并无毒性。”

“呸！”布赖斯轻蔑地叫道，“不过是虚张声势罢了！像我方才描述的那种药丸，除了糖衣和毒药之外，根本不会留下任何痕迹。我跟你们说，那毒药就是这么下进去的，我丝毫也不怀疑。这事轻而易举。而——除了一个懂医的人，还有谁会知道这手段下起来竟如此容易呢？”

===Original===
Mitchington and Jettison exchanged glances. Then Jettison leaned nearer to Bryce.

“So your theory is that Ransford got rid of both Braden and Collishaw—murdered both of them, in fact?” he suggested. “Do I understand that’s what it really comes to—in plain words?”

“Not quite,” replied Bryce. “I don’t say that Ransford meant to kill Braden—my notion is that they met, had an altercation, probably a struggle, and that Braden lost his life in it. But as regards Collishaw—”

“Don’t forget!” interrupted Mitchington. “Varner swore that he saw Braden flung through that doorway! Flung out! He saw a hand.”

“For everything that Varner could prove to the contrary,” answered Bryce, “the hand might have been stretched out to pull Braden back. No—I think there may have been accident in that affair. But, as regards Collishaw—murder, without doubt—deliberate!”

He lighted another cigarette, with the air of a man who had spoken his mind, and Mitchington, realizing that he had said all he had to say, got up from his seat.

===Chinese===
米钦顿和杰蒂森交换了一个眼神。随后杰蒂森朝布赖斯凑近了些。

“这么说，你的推断是——兰斯福德把布雷登和科利肖两个人都除了掉——事实上，就是谋杀了他们两个？”他试探着说，“我没理解错吧——说白了就是这样？”

“不完全是，”布赖斯答道，“我并没说兰斯福德是有意要杀布雷登——我的看法是：他们相遇，起了争执，多半动了手，布雷登是在冲突中丢了性命。但至于科利肖——”

“别忘了！”米钦顿打断他，“瓦纳发过誓，说他亲眼看见布雷登被人从那道门里甩了出去！是甩出去的！他看见了那只手。”

“瓦纳纵然能证明什么，也证明不了相反的情形，”布赖斯答道，“那只手，也完全可能是伸出去想把布雷登拉回来。不——我以为那一桩里也许有偶然的成分。但至于科利肖——那是谋杀，毫无疑问——是蓄意的！”

他又点燃一支烟，摆出一副已把心里话全盘倒出来的神情；米钦顿看出他再无别的话要讲，便从座位上站起身来。

===Original===
“Well—it’s all very interesting and very clever, doctor,” he said, glancing at Jettison. “And we shall keep it all in mind. Of course, you’ve talked all this over with Harker? I should like to know what he has to say. Now that you’ve told us who he is, I suppose we can talk to him?”

“You’ll have to wait a few days, then,” said Bryce. “He’s gone to town—by the last train tonight—on this business. I’ve sent him. I had some information today about Ransford’s whereabouts during the time of disappearance, and I’ve commissioned Harker to examine into it. When I hear what he’s found out, I’ll let you know.”

“You’re taking some trouble,” remarked Mitchington.

“I’ve told you the reason,” answered Bryce.

===Chinese===
“嗯——这些都十分有趣，也十分高明，医生，”他一边说，一边瞥了杰蒂森一眼，“我们会把这些都记在心上。这些，你当然都已经和哈克商量过了吧？我很想知道他怎么说。既然你已把他的身份告诉了我们，我想，我们总可以跟他谈谈了？”

“那么，你们得等上几天了，”布赖斯说，“他进城去了——搭的是今夜最后一班火车——为的便是这桩事。是我派他去的。今天我得到一条消息，是关于兰斯福德失踪那段时间的行踪的，我便委托哈克去查证一番。等我知道他查出了什么，再通知你们。”

“你可真是费了不少心思，”米钦顿说。

“我已经把缘故告诉过你们了，”布赖斯答道。

===Original===
Mitchington hesitated a little; then, with a motion of his head towards the door, beckoned Jettison to follow him.

“All right,” he said. “There’s plenty for us to see into, I’m thinking!”

Bryce laughed and pointed to a shelf of books near the fireplace.

“Do you know what Napoleon Bonaparte once gave as sound advice to police?” he asked. “No! Then I’ll tell you. ‘The art of the police,’ he said, ‘is not to see that which it is useless for it to see.’ Good counsel, Mitchington!”

===Chinese===
米钦顿稍一迟疑；随后朝门那边一摆头，示意杰蒂森跟他走。

“好吧，”他说，“我想，我们要查的东西可多着呢！”

布赖斯笑了起来，指了指壁炉旁边的一个书架。

“你们可知道，拿破仑·波拿巴曾给警察留下过一句金玉良言？”他问，“不知道吧！那我来告诉你们。他说：‘警察的艺术，在于不去看那些它看了也无用的事物。’这可是句良言啊，米钦顿！”

===Original===
The two men went away through the midnight streets, and kept silence until they were near the door of Jettison’s hotel. Then Mitchington spoke.

“Well!” he said. “We’ve had a couple of tales, anyhow! What do you think of things, now?”

Jettison threw back his head with a dry laugh.

“Never been better puzzled in all my time!” he said. “Never! But—if that young doctor’s playing a game—then, by the Lord Harry, inspector, it’s a damned deep ’un! And my advice is—watch the lot!”

===Chinese===
两人穿过午夜的街巷离去，一路无言，直走到快到杰蒂森下榻的旅馆门口，米钦顿才开了口。

“哎呀！”他说，“不管怎样，咱们算是听到了两套说辞！现在你是怎么想的？”

杰蒂森仰起头，干笑了一声。

“我干这行这么多年，从没比现在更摸不着头脑！”他说，“从来没有！不过——要是那位年轻医生是在下一盘棋的话——那么，老天爷作证，探长，这盘棋下得可真够深的！我的忠告是——把这帮人都盯紧了！”

## XX — Jettison Takes a Hand / 第二十章　杰蒂森插手

===Original===
By breakfast time next morning the man from New Scotland Yard had accomplished a series of meditations on the confidences made to him and Mitchington the night before and had determined on at least one course of action. But before entering upon it he had one or two important letters to write, the composition of which required much thought and trouble, and by the time he had finished them, and deposited them by his own hand in the General Post Office, it was drawing near to noon﻿—the great bell of the Cathedral, indeed, was proclaiming noontide to Wrychester as Jettison turned into the police-station and sought Mitchington in his office.

“I was just coming round to see if you’d overslept yourself,” said Mitchington good-humouredly. “We were up pretty late last night, or, rather, this morning.”

“I’ve had letters to write,” said Jettison. He sat down and picked up a newspaper and cast a casual glance over it. “Got anything fresh?”

“Well, this much,” answered Mitchington. “The two gentlemen who told us so much last night are both out of town. I made an excuse to call on them both early this morning﻿—just on nine o’clock. Dr. Ransford went up to London by the eight-fifteen.

“Dr. Bryce, says his landlady, went out on his bicycle at half-past eight﻿—where, she didn’t know, but, she fancied, into the country. However, I ascertained that Ransford is expected back this evening, and Bryce gave orders for his usual dinner to be ready at seven o’clock, and so﻿—”

===Chinese===
到了第二天早晨用早餐的时候，那位来自新苏格兰场（New Scotland Yard）的人，已经就昨夜对他和米钦顿吐露的那些秘密反复思量了一番，并至少定下了一步棋。但在付诸实施之前，他还有一两封要紧的信要写，措辞起来颇费思量与周折；待他写完，又亲自送往邮政总局投递了，时辰已近正午——事实上，当杰蒂森拐进警察局、到办公室里找米钦顿的时候，大教堂那口大钟正敲响正午，向全赖彻斯特报时。

「我正要过去瞧瞧你是不是睡过头了呢，」米钦顿和颜悦色地说，「咱们昨夜熬得可不早——或者说，今儿一大早才睡。」

「我有几封信要写，」杰蒂森说。他坐下来，拿起一份报纸，随手翻了翻。「有什么新消息没有？」

「嗯，倒有这么些，」米钦顿答道，「昨晚跟咱们讲了那么多话的两位先生，都出城去了。今天一大早我找了个由头去拜访他俩——刚好九点。兰斯福德医生搭八点一刻的车上伦敦去了。

「布赖斯医生呢，据他的房东太太说，八点半就骑自行车出去了——去哪儿她不清楚，不过她猜是下乡去了。话虽如此，我已打听明白，兰斯福德今夜就要回来；而布赖斯也吩咐过，照常在七点把他的晚饭预备好，所以——」

===Original===
Jettison flung away the newspaper and pulled out his pipe.

“Oh, I don’t think they’ll run away﻿—either of ’em,” he remarked indifferently. “They’re both too cocksure of their own ways of looking at things.”

“You looked at ’em any more?” asked Mitchington.

“Done a bit of reflecting﻿—yes,” replied the detective. “Complicated affair, my lad! More in it than one would think at first sight. I’m certain of this quite apart from whatever mystery there is about the Braden affair and the Collishaw murder, there’s a lot of scheming and contriving been going on﻿—and is going on!﻿—somewhere, by somebody. Underhand work, you understand? However, my particular job is the Collishaw business﻿—and there’s a bit of information I’d like to get hold of at once. Where’s the office of that Friendly Society we heard about last night?”

“That’ll be the Wrychester Second Friendly,” answered Mitchington. “There are two such societies in the town﻿—the first’s patronized by small tradesmen and the like; the second by workingmen. The second does take deposits from its members. The office is in Fladgate﻿—secretary’s name outside﻿—Mr. Stebbing. What are you after?”

“Tell you later,” said Jettison. “Just an idea.”

===Chinese===
杰蒂森把报纸一扔，掏出烟斗来。

「嗳，我看他俩谁也不会跑——」他满不在乎地说，「两人都太自以为是，笃信自己看事情的那一套了。」

「你又琢磨过他俩了？」米钦顿问。

「琢磨过一点——是的，」侦探答道，「这桩案子错综复杂啊，老弟！里头的名堂，比乍一看要多得多。这一点我敢肯定——撇开布雷登那桩事和科利肖被杀的案子里头的种种谜团不谈——还有些人在什么地方、由什么人，一直在暗中策划盘算——而且至今仍在策划盘算！——明白吗，是见不得人的勾当。不过，我手头管的是科利肖这桩案子——有一桩消息，我想立马弄到手。昨晚听说的那个互助会（Friendly Society），办公地点在哪儿？」

「那该是赖彻斯特第二互助会，」米钦顿答道，「城里这样的会社有两家——头一家做的是小商人那一类的生意；第二家则是做工的人去的。第二家确实收会员的存款。办公地点在弗拉德盖特——门外挂着秘书的姓名牌——斯特宾先生。你打听这个做什么？」

「回头再告诉你，」杰蒂森说，「不过是心里起了个念头。」

===Original===
He went leisurely out and across the market square and into the narrow, old-world street called Fladgate, along which he strolled as if doing no more than looking about him until he came to an ancient shop which had been converted into an office, and had a wire blind over the lower half of its front window, wherein was woven in conspicuous gilt letters Wrychester Second Friendly Society﻿—George Stebbing, Secretary. Nothing betokened romance or mystery in that essentially humble place, but it was in Jettison’s mind that when he crossed its threshold he was on his way to discovering something that would possibly clear up the problem on which he was engaged.

The staff of the Second Friendly was inconsiderable in numbers﻿—an outer office harboured a small boy and a tall young man; an inner one accommodated Mr. Stebbing, also a young man, sandy-haired and freckled, who, having inspected Detective-Sergeant Jettison’s professional card, gave him the best chair in the room and stared at him with a mingling of awe and curiosity which plainly showed that he had never entertained a detective before. And as if to show his visitor that he realized the seriousness of the occasion, he nodded meaningly at his door.

“All safe, here, sir!” he whispered. “Well fitting doors in these old houses﻿—knew how to make ’em in those days. No chance of being overheard here﻿—what can I do for you, sir?”

===Chinese===
他不慌不忙地走出来，穿过集市广场，拐进一条名叫弗拉德盖特的狭窄古街。他沿街信步而行，仿佛不过四处张望一番，直走到一间由老铺面改成的办公室前——那临街窗户的下半截装着一幅金属丝帘子，上面用醒目的镀金大字织着：赖彻斯特第二互助会——秘书乔治·斯特宾（George Stebbing）。这地方本质上朴素寒素，丝毫看不出什么浪漫或神秘；然而杰蒂森心里却觉得，待他跨过那道门槛，便有望发现一些线索，也许能把他手头这桩案子理出个头绪来。

第二互助会的人手不多——外间办公室里有一个小厮和一个高个子年轻人；里间则坐着斯特宾先生，同样是个年轻人，沙色头发，满脸雀斑。他仔细验过杰蒂森侦探警长的公务证件之后，把屋里最好的一把椅子让给客人，然后既敬畏又好奇地盯着他瞧——显然他从没接待过侦探。为了向客人表明自己懂得眼下这场面的分量，他还朝自己的房门意味深长地点了点头。

「这里头保险得很，先生！」他压低了嗓子说，「这些老宅子的门做工严实——那年月的人懂得怎么造门。在这儿绝无被人偷听之虞——先生有何见教？」

===Original===
“Thank you﻿—much obliged to you,” said Jettison. “No objection to my pipe, I suppose? Just so. Ah!﻿—well, between you and me, Mr. Stebbing, I’m down here in connection with that Collishaw case﻿—you know.”

“I know, sir﻿—poor fellow!” said the secretary. “Cruel thing, sir, if the man was put an end to. One of our members, was Collishaw, sir.”

“So I understand,” remarked Jettison. “That’s what I’ve come about. Bit of information, on the quiet, eh? Strictly between our two selves﻿—for the present.”

Stebbing nodded and winked, as if he had been doing business with detectives all his life. “To be sure, sir, to be sure!” he responded with alacrity. “Just between you and me and the door post!﻿—all right. Anything I can do, Mr. Jettison, shall be done. But it’s more in the way of what I can tell, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
「多谢——麻烦你了，」杰蒂森说，「我抽烟斗不碍事吧？这就好。嗳！——这么说吧，斯特宾先生，天知地知你知我知，我此番下来，是为着科利肖那桩案子——你是知道的。」

「我知道，先生——可怜的人！」秘书说，「要是这人是被人害死的，那可太狠毒了，先生。科利肖，是我们会的会员呢，先生。」

「我也听说了，」杰蒂森道，「我正是为这个来的。想打听一点消息，悄悄的，嗯？眼下只限你我两人之间，严守秘密。」

斯特宾点头眨眼，一副这辈子都在跟侦探打交道的神气。「那是自然，先生，那是自然！」他连忙应道，「就你知我知，再加一根门柱子！——成。杰蒂森先生，凡是我能效劳的，一定办到。不过，想必更主要的，是我能告诉您些什么吧？」

===Original===
“Something of that sort,” replied Jettison in his slow, easygoing fashion. “I want to know a thing or two. Yours is a workingman’s society, I think? Aye﻿—and I understand you’ve a system whereby such a man can put his bits of savings by in your hands?”

“A capital system, too!” answered the secretary, seizing on a pamphlet and pushing it into his visitor’s hand. “I don’t believe there’s better in England! If you read that﻿—”

“I’ll take a look at it some time,” said Jettison, putting the pamphlet in his pocket. “Well, now, I also understand that Collishaw was in the habit of bringing you a bit of saved money now and then﻿—a sort of saving fellow, wasn’t he?” Stebbing nodded assent and reached for a ledger which lay on the farther side of his desk.

“Collishaw,” he answered, “had been a member of our society ever since it started﻿—fourteen years ago. And he’d been putting in savings for some eight or nine years. Not much, you’ll understand. Say, as an average, two to three pounds every half-year﻿—never more. But, just before his death, or murder, or whatever you like to call it, he came in here one day with fifty pounds! Fairly astounded me, sir! Fifty pounds﻿—all in a lump!”

===Chinese===
「差不多是这么回事，」杰蒂森以他那慢条斯理、不疾不徐的口吻答道，「我想知道一两件事。你们这是个工人互助会，是吧？嗯——我还听说，你们这儿有一套办法，好让这样的人把零星攒下的积蓄存到你们手里？」

「而且是顶呱呱的一套办法！」秘书答道，一把抓起一本小册子塞到客人手里，「全英国再找不出更好的了！您瞧瞧这个——」

「回头我有空再瞧，」杰蒂森说着，把小册子揣进口袋，「那么，我又听说，科利肖素来有时不时带点积蓄上你这儿来的习惯——他这人算是挺俭省的吧？」斯特宾点头称是，伸手去够桌案另一头的一本账册。

「科利肖，」他答道，「我会一开办他就入会了——那是十四年前的事。他往里头存钱，也有八九个年头了。数目不多，这您要明白。平均下来，每半年两三镑——从没多过。可是，就在他死——或者说被谋害，或随您怎么叫法——之前没多久，有一天他上这儿来，带来了五十镑！可把我吓了一跳，先生！五十镑——整整齐齐一笔！」

===Original===
“It’s about that fifty pounds I want to know something,” said Jettison. “He didn’t tell you how he’d come by it? Wasn’t a legacy, for instance?”

“He didn’t say anything but that he’d had a bit of luck,” answered Stebbing. “I asked no questions. Legacy, now?﻿—no, he didn’t mention that. Here it is,” he continued, turning over the pages of the ledger. “There! £50. You see the date﻿—that ’ud be two days before his death.”

Jettison glanced at the ledger and resumed his seat.

“Now, then, Mr. Stebbing, I want you to tell me something very definite,” he said. “It’s not so long since this happened, so you’ll not have to tax your memory to any great extent. In what form did Collishaw pay that fifty pounds to you?”

===Chinese===
「我正是想打听这五十镑的事，」杰蒂森说，「他没跟你说这笔钱是怎么到手的？比方说，是不是一笔遗产？」

「他什么也没说，只说是走了点运，」斯特宾答道，「我也没多问。遗产吗？——不，他没提这个。在这儿呢，」他一边翻着账册，一边接着说，「瞧！五十镑。您看这个日子——那是他死前两天。」

杰蒂森瞧了一眼账册，重新坐下。

「那么，斯特宾先生，我要你给我一个十分确切的答复，」他说，「这事发生至今为时不长，你不必太费神去回忆。科利肖交那五十镑给你，是用什么形式交的？」

===Original===
“That’s easy answered, sir,” said the secretary. “It was in gold. Fifty sovereigns﻿—he had ’em in a bit of a bag.” Jettison reflected on this information for a moment or two. Then he rose.

“Much obliged to you, Mr. Stebbing,” he said. “That’s something worth knowing. Now there’s something else you can tell me as long as I’m here﻿—though, to be sure, I could save you the trouble by using my own eyes. How many banks are there in this little city of yours?”

“Three,” answered Stebbing promptly. “Old Bank, in Monday Market; Popham & Hargreaves, in the Square; Wrychester Bank, in Spurriergate. That’s the lot.”

“Much obliged,” said Jettison. “And﻿—for the present﻿—not a word of what we’ve talked about. You’ll be hearing more﻿—later.”

===Chinese===
「这个好答，先生，」秘书说，「是金币。五十枚沙弗林（sovereign）——他装在一个小布袋里提来的。」杰蒂森把这条消息琢磨了一两下，随即站起身来。

「多谢你了，斯特宾先生，」他说，「这一条很值得知道。既然来了，还有一桩事你也可以告诉我——虽说，老实讲，我自己瞧瞧也省得麻烦你。你们这座小城里有几家银行？」

「三家，」斯特宾脱口而出，「老银行（Old Bank），在星期一集市（Monday Market）；波帕姆与哈格里夫斯（Popham & Hargreaves），在广场上；赖彻斯特银行，在斯珀里尔门（Spurriergate）。就这三家。」

「多谢，」杰蒂森说，「还有——眼下暂且——咱们谈的这些，一个字也别往外说。往后——你还会听到消息的。」

===Original===
He went away, memorizing the names of the three banking establishments﻿—ten minutes later he was in the private parlour of the first, in serious conversation with its manager. Here it was necessary to be more secret, and to insist on more secrecy than with the secretary of the Second Friendly, and to produce all his credentials and give all his reasons. But Jettison drew that covert blank, and the next, too, and it was not until he had been closeted for some time with the authorities of the third bank that he got the information he wanted. And when he had got it, he impressed secrecy and silence on his informants in a fashion which showed them that however easygoing his manner might be, he knew his business as thoroughly as they knew theirs.

It was by that time past one o’clock, and Jettison turned into the small hotel at which he had lodged himself. He thought much and gravely while he ate his dinner; he thought still more while he smoked his after-dinner pipe. And his face was still heavy with thought when, at three o’clock, he walked into Mitchington’s office and finding the inspector alone shut the door and drew a chair to Mitchington’s desk.

“Now then,” he said. “I’ve had a rare morning’s work, and made a discovery, and you and me, my lad, have got to have about as serious a bit of talk as we’ve had since I came here.”

===Chinese===
他离去时，心里记下了那三家银行的名字——十分钟后，他已经坐在第一家的私人会客室里，同经理郑重其事地交谈起来。在这里，保密的必要更甚于在第二互助会那位秘书面前，既要更加隐秘，也要更加坚持对方守口如瓶，还得亮出全部证件、道出全部来由。然而杰蒂森这番秘密查访，在头一家就扑了空，第二家也是空手而归；直到他在第三家银行的当家人那里密谈了相当一阵子，才弄到了他要的消息。弄到手之后，他又以一套办法叮嘱提供消息的人严格保密、三缄其口——那套办法让他们明白：别看他举止随和，干这一行他跟他们干自己那一行一样在行。

这时已过了一点钟，杰蒂森回到他下榻的那家小旅馆。吃饭时他心事重重地思虑着；饭后抽烟斗时，他想得更多。三点钟，他走进米钦顿的办公室，见探长独自一人在，便关上门，拉过一把椅子坐到米钦顿的桌前——这时他脸上仍是一副心事沉重的神色。

「好了，」他说，「我这一上午干得可真不少，有了一个发现。咱们俩，老弟，得好好谈一谈，这番谈话的分量，怕是打我到这儿以来头一遭。」

===Original===
Mitchington pushed his papers aside and showed his keen attention.

“You remember what that young fellow told us last night about that man Collishaw paying in fifty pounds to the Second Friendly two days before his death,” said Jettison. “Well, I thought over that business a lot, early this morning, and I fancied I saw how I could find something out about it. So I have﻿—on the strict quiet. That’s why I went to the Friendly Society. The fact was﻿—I wanted to know in what form Collishaw handed in that fifty pounds. I got to know. Gold!”

Mitchington, whose work hitherto had not led him into the mysteries of detective enterprise, nodded delightedly.

“Good!” he said. “Rare idea! I should never have thought of it! And﻿—what do you make out of that, now?”

===Chinese===
米钦顿把文件推到一边，凝神细听。

「你还记得昨夜那个年轻人跟咱们说的，科利肖死前两天往第二互助会存了五十镑那事，」杰蒂森说，「嗯，今天一清早我把这事翻来覆去想了好久，觉得自己瞧出了个门道，兴许能查出点什么来。我也确实查到了——极其隐秘地查到的。这就是我上互助会去的原因。事情是——我想知道科利肖交那五十镑，是用什么形式交的。我弄清楚了。金币！」

米钦顿——他以往的工作还从未涉足侦探行当这一类的奥秘——高兴得直点头。

「好！」他说，「好主意！我可想不到这一着！那么——你从中琢磨出什么来了？」

===Original===
“Nothing,” replied Jettison. “But﻿—a good deal out of what I’ve learned since that bit of a discovery. Now, put it to yourself﻿—whoever it was that paid Collishaw that fifty pounds in gold did it with a motive. More than one motive, to be exact﻿—but we’ll stick to one, to begin with. The motive for paying in gold was﻿—avoidance of discovery. A cheque can be readily traced. So can banknotes. But gold is not easily traced. Therefore the man who paid Collishaw fifty pounds took care to provide himself with gold. Now then﻿—how many men are there in a small place like this who are likely to carry fifty pounds in gold in their pockets, or to have it at hand?”

“Not many,” agreed Mitchington.

“Just so﻿—and therefore I’ve been doing a bit of secret inquiry amongst the bankers, as to who supplied himself with gold about that date,” continued Jettison. “I’d to convince ’em of the absolute necessity of information, too, before I got any! But I got some﻿—at the third attempt. On the day previous to that on which Collishaw handed that fifty pounds to Stebbing, a certain Wrychester man drew fifty pounds in gold at his bank. Who do you think he was?”

===Chinese===
「没什么，」杰蒂森答道，「不过——自打那一丁点发现之后我又查到的，倒是大有文章。你且这么想——无论是什么人付了科利肖那五十镑金币，都是带着动机的。确切地说，动机不止一个——可咱们先抓一个说。付金币的动机是——免得被人查出。支票很容易追查。钞票也一样。可金币不容易追。所以，付科利肖五十镑的那个人，特意备足了金币。那么——在这么个小地方，口袋里揣着、或是手头备着五十镑金币的人，能有几个？」

「不多，」米钦顿赞同道。

「正是——所以我在那几家银行里暗地里查了一遭，查的是谁在前后那段日子里兑过金币，」杰蒂森接着说，「还得先让他们相信，提供消息是绝对必要的事，这才问出一点来！我到底问到了——问到了第三家。在科利肖把那五十镑交给斯特宾的前一天，赖彻斯特有一个男子，在他开户的银行里兑了五十镑金币。你猜这人是谁？」

===Original===
“Who﻿—who?” demanded Mitchington.

Jettison leaned half-across the desk.

“Bryce!” he said in a whisper. “Bryce!”

Mitchington sat up in his chair and opened his mouth in sheer astonishment.

===Chinese===
「谁——是谁？」米钦顿追问。

杰蒂森把身子探过半张桌子。

「布赖斯！」他压低嗓子说，「布赖斯！」

米钦顿在椅子上猛地坐直了，惊得张大了嘴。

===Original===
“Good heavens!” he muttered after a moment’s silence. “You don’t mean it?”

“Fact!” answered Jettison. “Plain, incontestable fact, my lad. Dr. Bryce keeps an account at the Wrychester bank. On the day I’m speaking of he cashed a cheque to self for fifty pounds and took it all in gold.”

The two men looked at each other as if each were asking his companion a question.

“Well?” said Mitchington at last. “You’re a cut above me, Jettison. What do you make of it?”

===Chinese===
「老天爷！」他沉默了一瞬，喃喃道，「你不是当真的吧？」

「事实！」杰蒂森答道，「明明白白、铁证如山的事实，老弟。布赖斯医生在赖彻斯特银行开了户。就在我说那天，他兑现了一张抬头写『本人』的五十镑支票，全部兑成了金币。」

两人面面相觑，仿佛都在拿同一个问题问对方。

「那么？」米钦顿终于开口，「你比我高明，杰蒂森。你怎么看这事？」

===Original===
“I said last night that the young man was playing a deep game,” replied Jettison. “But﻿—what game? What’s he building up? For mark you, Mitchington, if﻿—I say if, mind!﻿—if that fifty pounds which he drew in gold is the identical fifty paid to Collishaw, Bryce didn’t pay it as hush-money!”

“Think not?” said Mitchington, evidently surprised. “Now, that was my first impression. If it wasn’t hush-money﻿—”

“It wasn’t hush-money, for this reason,” interrupted Jettison. “We know that whatever else he knew, Bryce didn’t know of the accident to Braden until Varner fetched him to Braden. That’s established﻿—on what you’ve put before me. Therefore, whatever Collishaw saw, before or at the time that accident happened, it wasn’t Bryce who was mixed up in it. Therefore, why should Bryce pay Collishaw hush-money?”

===Chinese===
「我昨夜就说了，那年轻人是在下一盘很深的棋，」杰蒂森答道，「可——是什么棋？他在图什么？你要知道，米钦顿，如果——我说的是如果，注意！——如果他兑出来的那五十镑金币，正是付给科利肖的那五十镑，那么布赖斯这笔钱就不是封口费！」

「你这么认为？」米钦顿说，显然很诧异，「这倒怪了，我起初正是这么想的。要不是封口费——」

「这不是封口费，理由在此，」杰蒂森打断他，「我们知道，布赖斯不论还知道别的什么，在瓦纳跑去叫他来治布雷登之前，他并不知道布雷登出了事。这一点是确凿的——就凭你给我看的那些。所以，无论科利肖在那场事故发生之前或当时看见了什么，牵涉在里头的人都绝不是布赖斯。那么，布赖斯何必付科利肖封口费呢？」

===Original===
Mitchington, who had evidently been thinking, suddenly pulled out a drawer in his desk and took some papers from it which he began to turn over.

“Wait a minute,” he said. “I’ve an abstract here﻿—of what the foreman at the Cathedral mason’s yard told me of what he knew as to where Collishaw was working that morning when the accident happened﻿—I made a note of it when I questioned him after Collishaw’s death. Here you are:

‘Foreman says that on morning of Braden’s accident, Collishaw was at work in the north gallery of the clerestory, clearing away some timber which the carpenters had left there. Collishaw was certainly thus engaged from nine o’clock until past eleven that morning. *Mem.* Have investigated this myself. From the exact spot where C. was clearing the timber, there is an uninterrupted view of the gallery on the south side of the nave, and of the arched doorway at the head of St. Wrytha’s Stair.’ ”

“Well,” observed Jettison, “that proves what I’m saying. It wasn’t hush-money. For whoever it was that Collishaw saw lay hands on Braden, it wasn’t Bryce﻿—Bryce, we know, was at that time coming across the Close or crossing that path through the part you call Paradise: Varner’s evidence proves that. So﻿—if the fifty pounds wasn’t paid for hush-money, what was it paid for?”

===Chinese===
米钦顿显然一直在思索，这时猛地拉开书桌的一个抽屉，从中取出几份文件，翻找起来。

「且慢，」他说，「我这儿有一份摘录——是大教堂石匠场的工头告诉我的，关于事故发生那天早上科利肖在哪儿干活，他所知道的情况——科利肖死后我盘问他时记下的。你瞧：

『工头称：布雷登出事那天早晨，科利肖在高侧窗层的北侧回廊干活，清理木匠们留下的木料。那天上午九点到十一点过后，科利肖确实一直在干这活。*备考*。此节本人已亲往查证。从科利肖清理木料的确切位置望去，可一览无余地望见中殿南侧的回廊，以及圣赖莎阶梯（St. Wrytha's Stair）顶端的拱门。』」

「嗯，」杰蒂森道，「这便证明了我说的话。这不是封口费。因为无论科利肖看见对布雷登下手的究竟是谁，那人都不是布赖斯——我们知道，当时布赖斯正穿过教堂区，或是穿过你们叫做「乐园」的那片园子里的那条小径：瓦纳的证词可以证明。所以——要是这五十镑不是封口费，那是为什么付的？」

===Original===
“Do you suggest anything?” asked Mitchington.

“I’ve thought of two or three things,” answered the detective. “One’s this﻿—was the fifty pounds paid for information? If so, and Bryce has that information, why doesn’t he show his hand more plainly? If he bribed Collishaw with fifty pounds: to tell him who Braden’s assailant was, he now knows!﻿—so why doesn’t he let it out, and have done with it?”

“Part of his game﻿—if that theory’s right,” murmured Mitchington.

“It mayn’t be right,” said Jettison. “But it’s one. And there’s another﻿—supposing he paid Collishaw that money on behalf of somebody else? I’ve thought this business out right and left, topside and bottom-side, and hang me if I don’t feel certain there is somebody else! What did Ransford tell us about Bryce and this old Harker﻿—think of that! And yet, according to Bryce, Harker is one of our old Yard men!﻿—and therefore ought to be above suspicion.”

===Chinese===
「你有什么看法？」米钦顿问。

「我想过两三种可能，」侦探答道，「其一——这五十镑是买消息的吗？如果是，而布赖斯又已拿到那消息，那他为什么不更明白地摊牌？要是他用五十镑贿买科利肖，让他说出对布雷登下手的人是谁，那么他现在已经知道了！——那他为什么不干脆抖出来，一了百了？」

「那是他棋局里的一着——要是这推测成立的话，」米钦顿喃喃道。

「这推测未必成立，」杰蒂森说，「但终归是一种。还有一种——倘若他是替别人付科利肖这笔钱呢？我把这事前后左右、上上下下都想遍了，可我敢拿脑袋打赌，这里头一定还有一个人！兰斯福德跟咱们讲的，关于布赖斯和那个老哈克的事——你想想看！可是照布赖斯的说法，哈克是我们苏格兰场的老人！——那就不该受到怀疑了。」

===Original===
Mitchington suddenly started as if an idea had occurred to him.

“I say, you know!” he exclaimed. “We’ve only Bryce’s word for it that Harker is an ex-detective. I never heard that he was﻿—if he is, he’s kept it strangely quiet. You’d have thought that he’d have let us know, here, of his previous calling﻿—I never heard of a policeman of any rank who didn’t like to have a bit of talk with his own sort about professional matters.”

“Nor me,” assented Jettison. “And as you say, we’ve only Bryce’s word. And, the more I think of it, the more I’m convinced there’s somebody﻿—some man of whom you don’t seem to have the least idea﻿—who’s in this. And it may be that Bryce is in with him. However﻿—here’s one thing I’m going to do at once. Bryce gave us that information about the fifty pounds. Now I’m going to tell Bryce straight out that I’ve gone into that matter in my own fashion﻿—a fashion he evidently never thought of﻿—and ask him to explain why he drew a similar amount in gold. Come on round to his rooms.”

===Chinese===
米钦顿猛地一激灵，像是忽然想到了什么。

「哎，我说！」他叫起来，「哈克以前是侦探，这话只是布赖斯一面之词啊。我可从没听说他是——他要是的话，也把这事儿瞒得紧得很呢。你们原该以为，他上我们这儿来，会把自己从前干的那一行告诉我们才对——我还从没见过哪一个警察，不论官阶大小，不喜欢跟同行聊聊本行的事的。」

「我也没见过，」杰蒂森附和道，「而且如你所说，只是布赖斯一面之词。这事我想得越多，就越相信还有一个人——一个你似乎毫无概念的人——卷在里头。而布赖斯可能跟他是同伙。不过——有一件事我马上要办。布赖斯把那五十镑的消息告诉了我们。如今我要直截了当地告诉布赖斯，我已经按我自己的法子去查了那件事——一种他显然没想到的法子——然后叫他说清楚，他干吗也兑了同等数目的金币。走，到他寓所去。」

===Original===
But Bryce was not to be found at his rooms﻿—had not been back to his rooms, said his landlady, since he had ridden away early in the morning: all she knew was that he had ordered his dinner to be ready at his usual time that evening. With that the two men had to be content, and they went back to the police-station still discussing the situation. And they were still discussing it an hour later when a telegram was handed to Mitchington, who tore it open, glanced over its contents and passed it to his companion who read it aloud.

“Meet me with Jettison Wrychester Station on arrival of five-twenty express from London mystery cleared up guilty men known﻿—**Ransford**.”

Jettison handed the telegram back.

“A man of his word!” he said. “He mentioned two days﻿—he’s done it in one! And now, my lad﻿—do you notice?﻿—he says men, not man! It’s as I said﻿—there’s been more than one of ’em in this affair. Now then﻿—who are they?”

===Chinese===
可布赖斯并不在寓所——据他的房东太太说，他清早骑车走后就没回来过：她所知的全部，只是他吩咐过当晚照常把晚饭预备好。两位只得作罢，一边折回警察局，一边还在商量着局势。一个钟头之后，他们仍在讨论，这时有人递给米钦顿一封电报，他撕开一看，便递给同伴，同伴把电报念出声来：

「偕杰蒂森赖彻斯特车站接我 五时二十伦敦快车抵 谜底已破 凶手们已知——**兰斯福德**。」

杰蒂森把电报递了回去。

「他说到做到！」他说，「他原说两天——一天就办成了！而且现在，老弟——你注意到了吗？——他说的是『凶手们』，不是『凶手』！果然不出我所料——这桩案子里不止一个人。那么——他们是谁呢？」

## XXI — The Saxonsteade Arms / 第二十一章　萨克森斯蒂德纹章

===Original===
Bryce had ridden away on his bicycle from Wrychester that morning intent on a new piece of diplomacy. He had sat up thinking for some time after the two police officials had left him at midnight, and it had occurred to him that there was a man from whom information could be had of whose services he had as yet made no use but who must be somewhere in the neighbourhood﻿—the man Glassdale. Glassdale had been in Wrychester the previous evening; he could scarcely be far away now; there was certainly one person who would know where he could be found, and that person was the Duke of Saxonsteade. Bryce knew the Duke to be an extremely approachable man, a talkative, even a garrulous man, given to holding converse with anybody about anything, and he speedily made up his mind to ride over to Saxonsteade, invent a plausible excuse for his call, and get some news out of his Grace. Even if Glassdale had left the neighbourhood, there might be fragments of evidence to pick up from the Duke, for Glassdale, he knew, had given his former employer the information about the stolen jewels and would, no doubt, have added more about his acquaintance with Braden. And before Bryce came to his dreamed-of masterstroke in that matter, there were one or two things he wanted to clear up, to complete his double net, and he had an idea that an hour’s chat with Glassdale would yield all that he desired.

The active brain that had stood Bryce in good stead while he spun his meshes and devised his schemes was more active than ever that early summer morning. It was a ten-mile ride through woods and valleys to Saxonsteade, and there were sights and beauties of nature on either side of him which any other man would have lingered to admire and most men would have been influenced by. But Bryce had no eyes for the clouds over the copper-crowned hills or the mystic shadows in the deep valleys or the new buds in the hedgerows, and no thought for the rustic folk whose cottages he passed here and there in a sparsely populated country. All his thoughts were fixed on his schemes, almost as mechanically as his eyes followed the white road in front of his wheel. Ever since he had set out on his campaign he had regularly taken stock of his position; he was forever reckoning it up. And now, in his opinion, everything looked very promising. He had﻿—so far as he was aware﻿—created a definite atmosphere of suspicion around and against Ransford﻿—it needed only a little more suggestion, perhaps a little more evidence to bring about Ransford’s arrest. And the only question which at all troubled Bryce was﻿—should he let matters go to that length before putting his ultimatum before Mary Bewery, or should he show her his hand first? For Bryce had so worked matters that a word from him to the police would damn Ransford or save him﻿—and now it all depended, so far as Bryce himself was concerned, on Mary Bewery as to which word should be said. Elaborate as the toils were which he had laid out for Ransford to the police, he could sweep them up and tear them away with a sentence of added knowledge﻿—if Mary Bewery made it worth his while. But first﻿—before coming to the critical point﻿—there was yet certain information which he desired to get, and he felt sure of getting it if he could find Glassdale. For Glassdale, according to all accounts, had known Braden intimately of late years, and was most likely in possession of facts about him﻿—and Bryce had full confidence in himself as an interviewer of other men and a supreme belief that he could wheedle a secret out of anybody with whom he could procure an hour’s quiet conversation.

===Chinese===
那天早晨，布赖斯骑着自行车离开了赖彻斯特，一门心思要做一桩新的笼络功夫。两位警务人员午夜离去之后，他又独自坐着盘算了好一阵，忽然想起一个人来——一个能问出消息、而他至今尚未借重过的人：格拉斯代尔。这人也必在这一带什么地方。昨天傍晚格拉斯代尔还在赖彻斯特；此刻他走也走不了多远；而知道他下落的人，至少有一个是稳拿的——那就是萨克森斯蒂德公爵。布赖斯深知这位公爵平易近人得很，是个健谈、乃至有些喋喋不休的人，最爱跟任何人就任何事攀谈一番；于是他很快就拿定主意：骑车赶到萨克森斯蒂德去，编一个说得过去的由头登门造访，从公爵大人嘴里掏出些消息来。纵然格拉斯代尔已经离开了这地方，公爵那里也许还能拾到些零星的证据，因为他知道，格拉斯代尔已把失窃珠宝的事告诉了他从前的东家公爵，多半还会再多讲些他与布雷登交往的情形。而在布赖斯梦想着的那一记神来之笔出手之前，还有一两桩事他想弄个明白，好把他那张双重罗网织得严丝合缝；他心想，与格拉斯代尔聊上一个钟头，便能如愿以偿。

那个初夏的清晨，布赖斯那副素来帮他结网设局、应变自如的机敏头脑，比往常更加活跃。骑到萨克森斯蒂德要穿过林莽山谷，足有十英里的路程，一路上两旁的景色与造化之美，换了旁人定要驻足流连，换了大多数人也会为之动容。可布赖斯却无暇去看那铜色山巅上的云霞，无暇去看那深谷里幽秘的阴影，也无暇去看那篱笆上新发的嫩芽；在这人烟稀少的地方，他偶尔经过些乡民的小屋，也无心去想那些质朴的人。他满脑子都是自己的谋划，那般专注，几乎像他的眼睛机械地盯着车轮前那条白色大路一样。自从他着手这场布局以来，他总是定期盘点自己的处境；他无时无刻不在心里盘算掂量。而眼下，依他看来，一切都很是乐观。他——据他所知——已在兰斯福德周身织就了一层明确无误的疑云；只需再多加一点暗示，或许再多一点证据，便可促成兰斯福德的落网。唯一让布赖斯略感为难的只是这样一个问题：他该让事态走到那一步、再把最后通牒摆到玛丽·比尤里面前呢，还是先把底牌亮给她看？因为布赖斯已经把事情做得如此圆熟：他对警方的一句话，便足以置兰斯福德于死地，或者救他于水火——而如今，就布赖斯自己而言，究竟说哪一句话，全看玛丽·比尤里了。他向警方布下的那张用来围困兰斯福德的罗网固然精密，却也能凭添上一句新知便将其一举收拢、一撕而尽——只要玛丽·比尤里肯让他觉得值得一试。但首先——在走到那紧要关头之前——还有一些消息他非弄到手不可，而他确信，只要能找到格拉斯代尔，便能弄到手。因为据种种说法，格拉斯代尔近年来与布雷登过从甚密，多半掌握着关于此人的实情——而布赖斯对自己盘问别人的本领满怀信心，深信但凡能同谁弄到一个钟头清静谈话的机会，他便能套出任何秘密。

===Original===
As luck would have it, Bryce had no need to make a call upon the approachable and friendly Duke. Outside the little village at Saxonsteade, on the edge of the deep woods which fringed the ducal park, stood an old wayside inn, a relic of the coaching days, which bore on its sign the ducal arms. Into its old stone hall marched Bryce to refresh himself after his ride, and as he stood at the bow-windowed bar, he glanced into the garden beyond and there saw, comfortably smoking his pipe and reading the newspaper, the very man he was looking for.

Bryce had no spice of bashfulness, no want of confidence anywhere in his nature; he determined to attack Glassdale there and then. But he took a good look at his man before going out into the garden to him. A plain and ordinary sort of fellow, he thought; rather over middle age, with a tinge of grey in his hair and moustache; prosperous looking and well-dressed, and at that moment of the appearance of what he was probably taken for by the inn people﻿—a tourist. Whether he was the sort who would be communicative or not, Bryce could not tell from outward signs, but he was going to try, and he presently found his card-case, took out a card, and strolling down the garden to the shady spot in which Glassdale sat, assumed his politest and suavest manner and presented himself.

===Chinese===
也是合该凑巧，布赖斯竟无须登门去见那位平易近人、和蔼可亲的公爵。在萨克森斯蒂德那个小村子外头，紧贴着公爵园林边上那片幽深林地，有一家古老的路边客栈，是驿马车时代留下的旧物，招牌上绘着公爵家的纹章。布赖斯走进那古旧的石砌厅堂，打算歇歇脚、润润喉；他站在弓形窗的柜台前，朝那边的花园瞟了一眼，便瞧见了此行要找的那个人——那人正舒舒服服地抽着烟斗、看着报纸。

布赖斯天性里没有半分怯场，哪儿都不缺自信；他当场就拿定主意，要在这儿、当下便向格拉斯代尔发起进攻。但在走进花园走向那人之前，他先把对方细细打量了一番。一个其貌不扬、普普通通的家伙，他心想；年纪已过中年，头发和胡髭里都掺了些许灰白；看去颇为殷实，衣着也得体；此刻的模样，正同客栈里的人多半把他错认成的那种身份相符——一个游客。此人是不是那种肯把话和盘托出的性子，布赖斯从外表上判断不出来，但他打算试一试，便摸出名片盒，抽出一张名片，踱下花园，走到格拉斯代尔所坐的那处荫凉地方，摆出他那最为客气、最为圆熟的姿态，自报了家门。

===Original===
“Allow me, sir,” he said, carefully abstaining from any mention of names. “May I have the pleasure of a few minutes’ conversation with you?”

Glassdale cast a swift glance of surprise, not unmingled with suspicion, at the intruder﻿—the sort of glance that a man used to watchfulness would throw at anybody, thought Bryce. But his face cleared as he read the card, though it was still doubtful as he lifted it again.

“You’ve the advantage of me, sir,” he said. “Dr. Bryce, I see. But﻿—”

Bryce smiled and dropped into a garden chair at Glassdale’s side.

“You needn’t be afraid of talking to me,” he answered. “I’m well known in Wrychester. The Duke,” he went on, nodding his head in the direction of the great house which lay behind the woods at the foot of the garden, “knows me well enough﻿—in fact, I was on my way to see his Grace now, to ask him if he could tell me where you could be found. The fact is, I’m aware of what happened last night﻿—the jewel affair, you know﻿—Mitchington told me﻿—and of your friendship with Braden, and I want to ask you a question or two about Braden.”

Glassdale, who had looked somewhat mystified at the beginning of this address, seemed to understand matters better by the end of it.

===Chinese===
“冒昧了，先生，”他说，刻意避而不提任何姓名，“可否赏光，与您交谈几分钟？”

格拉斯代尔飞快地朝这位不速之客瞥了一眼，那目光里带着惊讶，却也掺着几分戒备——布赖斯心想，这正是惯于提防的人投向任何人的那种目光。不过，他看了名片之后，脸色便舒展开来，只是再抬起头时，神情里仍带着犹疑。

“您是占了我上风了，先生，”他说，“我瞧见了，布赖斯医生。可是——”

布赖斯微微一笑，在格拉斯代尔身旁的一张花园椅子上坐了下来。

“您尽可放心跟我说话，”他答道，“我在赖彻斯特是颇有人缘的。公爵，”他朝花园尽头那片林木背后的大宅方向点了点头，接着说，“也跟我相熟得很——其实，我这会儿正要去拜访公爵大人，问他知不知道能在哪儿找到您。实情是，昨夜发生的事我都知道——那桩珠宝案，您是知道的——是米钦顿告诉我的——也知道您与布雷登的交情，我正想就布雷登的事向您请教一两句。”

格拉斯代尔听这番话时，起初面露几分迷惘，听到末了，似乎把事情弄明白些了。

===Original===
“Oh, well, of course, doctor,” he said, “if that’s it﻿—but, of course﻿—a word first!﻿—these folk here at the inn don’t know who I am or that I’ve any connection with the Duke on that affair. I’m Mr. Gordon here﻿—just staying for a bit.”

“That’s all right,” answered Bryce with a smile of understanding. “All this is between ourselves. I saw you with the Duke and the rest of them last night, and I recognized you just now. And all I want is a bit of talk about Braden. You knew him pretty well of late years?”

“Knew him for a good many years,” replied Glassdale. He looked narrowly at his visitor. “I suppose you know his story﻿—and mine?” he asked. “Bygone affairs, eh?”

“Yes, yes!” answered Bryce reassuringly. “No need to go into that﻿—that’s all done with.”

“Aye﻿—well, we both put things right,” said Glassdale. “Made restitution﻿—both of us, you understand. So that is done with? And you know, then, of course, who Braden really was?”

===Chinese===
“哦，那当然，医生，”他说，“要只是为这个——不过，当然——先说一句！——这客栈里的人既不知我是谁，也不知我在那桩事上跟公爵有什么干系。我在这一带用的是戈登先生（Mr. Gordon）的名号——不过是暂住几天。”

“这没问题，”布赖斯心照不宣地笑了笑，答道，“这些都只在你我之间。昨夜我瞧见您跟公爵他们一处在一块儿，方才也认出了您。我所求的，不过是就布雷登聊上几句。近年来您跟他挺熟吧？”

“认识他好些年了，”格拉斯代尔回答。他细细地打量着来客。“我想，他的底细——还有我的底细——您都知道了吧？”他问，“都是些陈年旧事了，嗯？”

“对，对！”布赖斯宽慰地答道，“那个就不必再提了——都过去了。”

“是了——唉，我们俩都把事情了结清了，”格拉斯代尔说，“把亏空都补上了——我们俩都是，您明白。那么这事也算过去了？这么说来，布雷登究竟是谁，您当然也是知道的了？”

===Original===
“John Brake, ex bank-manager,” answered Bryce promptly. “I know all about it. I’ve been deeply interested and concerned in his death. And I’ll tell you why. I want to marry his daughter.”

Glassdale turned and stared at his companion.

“His daughter!” he exclaimed. “Brake’s daughter! God bless my soul! I never knew he had a daughter!”

It was Bryce’s turn to stare now. He looked at Glassdale incredulously.

“Do you mean to tell me that you knew Brake all those years and that he never mentioned his children?” he exclaimed.

“Never a word of ’em!” replied Glassdale. “Never knew he had any!”

===Chinese===
“约翰·布雷克，前银行经理，”布赖斯脱口而出，“这些我全知道。对他的死，我一直深为关切，也卷入其中。我告诉您缘由吧——我想娶他的女儿。”

格拉斯代尔转过身来，盯着自己的同伴。

“他的女儿！”他失声叫道，“布雷克的女儿！老天爷！我可从不知道他还有个女儿！”

这回轮到布赖斯发愣了。他难以置信地看着格拉斯代尔。

“您的意思是说，您认识了布雷克那么些年，他竟从未提起过他的孩子？”他嚷道。

“从没提过半个字！”格拉斯代尔答道，“压根儿不知道他有孩子！”

===Original===
“Did he never speak of his past?” asked Bryce.

“Not in that respect,” answered Glassdale. “I’d no idea that he was﻿—or had been﻿—a married man. He certainly never mentioned wife nor children to me, sir, and yet I knew Brake about as intimately as two men can know each other for some years before we came back to England.”

Bryce fell into one of his fits of musing. What could be the meaning of this extraordinary silence on Brake’s part? Was there still some hidden secret, some other mystery at which he had not yet guessed?

“Odd!” he remarked at last after a long pause during which Glassdale had watched him curiously. “But, did he ever speak to you of an old friend of his named Ransford﻿—a doctor?”

“Never!” said Glassdale. “Never mentioned such a man!”

===Chinese===
“他从未跟您谈起过从前的事吗？”布赖斯问。

“在那一层上从没有过，”格拉斯代尔回答，“我一点也不知道他——是、或者曾经是——一个成了家的人。先生，他确实从没跟我提过妻室儿女，可是在我们回到英国之前的那几年里，我跟布雷克的交情，已经深厚到两个男人之间所能深厚的一切地步了。”

布赖斯又陷入了那种出神的冥想。布雷克这般异乎寻常的缄默，究竟用意何在？莫非其中还藏着什么秘密，还有什么他尚未猜透的谜？

“怪了！”他终于开口道——这一阵长久的沉默里，格拉斯代尔一直好奇地打量着他。“可是——他可曾跟您提起过一个名叫兰斯福德的旧友——一位医生？”

“从没有过！”格拉斯代尔说，“从没提过这么一个人！”

===Original===
Bryce reflected again, and suddenly determined to be explicit.

“John Brake, the bank manager,” he said, “was married at a place called Braden Medworth, in Leicestershire, to a girl named Mary Bewery. He had two children, who would be, respectively, about four and one years of age when his﻿—we’ll call it misfortune﻿—happened. That’s a fact!”

“First I ever heard of it, then,” said Glassdale. “And that’s a fact, too!”

“He’d also a very close friend named Ransford﻿—Mark Ransford,” continued Bryce. “This Ransford was best man at Brake’s wedding.”

“Never heard him speak of Ransford, nor of any wedding!” affirmed Glassdale. “All news to me, doctor.”

“This Ransford is now in practice in Wrychester,” said Bryce. “And he has two young people living with him as his wards﻿—a girl of twenty, a boy of seventeen﻿—who are, without doubt, John Brake’s children. It is the daughter that I want to marry.”

===Chinese===
布赖斯又沉思起来，忽然决定把话挑明。

“那个银行经理约翰·布雷克，”他说，“在莱斯特郡一个叫布雷登梅德沃斯的地方，娶了一个名叫玛丽·比尤里的姑娘为妻。他有两个孩子，在他那桩——我们姑且称之为变故——发生的时候，分别大约是四岁和一岁。这是实情！”

“那我还是头一回听说呢，”格拉斯代尔说，“这也是实情！”

“他还有一个极为要好的朋友，名叫兰斯福德——马克·兰斯福德，”布赖斯接着说，“这位兰斯福德是布雷克婚礼上的伴郎。”

“从没听他提起过兰斯福德，也没提过什么婚礼！”格拉斯代尔肯定地说，“对我来说都是新闻哪，医生。”

“这位兰斯福德如今在赖彻斯特行医，”布赖斯说，“他身边住着两个年轻人，算作他的被监护人——一个二十岁的姑娘，一个十七岁的少年——毫无疑问，正是约翰·布雷克的孩子。我要娶的，便是那个女儿。”

===Original===
Glassdale shook his head as if in sheer perplexity.

“Well, all I can say is, you surprise me!” he remarked. “I’d no idea of any such thing.”

“Do you think Brake came to Wrychester because of that?” asked Bryce.

“How can I answer that, sir, when I tell you that I never heard him breathe one word of any children?” exclaimed Glassdale. “No! I know his reason for coming to Wrychester. It was wholly and solely﻿—as far as I know﻿—to tell the Duke here about that jewel business, the secret of which had been entrusted to Brake and me by a man on his deathbed in Australia. Brake came to Wrychester by himself﻿—I was to join him next morning: we were then to go to see the Duke together. When I got to Wrychester, I heard of Brake’s accident, and being upset by it, I went away again and waited some days until yesterday, when I made up my mind to tell the Duke myself, as I did, with very fortunate results. No, that’s the only reason I know of why Brake came this way. I tell you I knew nothing at all of his family affairs! He was a very close man, Brake, and apart from his business matters, he’d only one idea in his head, and that was lodged there pretty firmly, I assure you!”

“What was it?” asked Bryce.

===Chinese===
格拉斯代尔摇了摇头，仿佛纯属困惑。

“唉，我只能这么说——您叫我吃惊！”他说，“这种事我一点都不知道。”

“您觉得，布雷克到赖彻斯特来，是为这个缘故吗？”布赖斯问。

“先生，我既然告诉您，他从没对孩子的事漏过半个字，您让我怎么回答呢？”格拉斯代尔嚷道，“不！他来赖彻斯特的缘由，我是知道的。据我所知，完完全全、别无他由——就是为了来告诉此地的公爵那桩珠宝案，而那桩秘密，是澳大利亚有一个临终之人托付给布雷克和我的。布雷克独自先到了赖彻斯特——我本该第二天一早赶来与他会合：然后我们一道去拜见公爵。等我赶到赖彻斯特，却听说了布雷克那桩意外，我心里一乱，便又走了，在外头候了好些天，直到昨天，才拿定主意亲自去告诉公爵——我也果然这么做了，结果颇为幸运。不，布雷克走这一趟，我所知道的就是这一个缘由。我告诉您，他的家事我一无所知！布雷克是个嘴很严的人，除了他的正经事务，他脑子里只有一个念头，而且那个念头扎得相当牢，我可以向您保证！”

“什么念头？”布赖斯问。

===Original===
“He wanted to find a certain man﻿—or, rather, two men﻿—who’d cruelly deceived and wronged him, but one of ’em in particular,” answered Glassdale. “The particular one he believed to be in Australia, until near the end, when he got an idea that he’d left for England; as for the other, he didn’t bother much about him. But the man that he did want!﻿—ah, he wanted him badly!”

“Who was that man?” asked Bryce.

“A man of the name of Falkiner Wraye,” answered Glassdale promptly. “A man he’d known in London. This Wraye, together with his partner, a man called Flood, tricked Brake into lending ’em several thousands pounds﻿—bank’s money, of course﻿—for a couple of days﻿—no more﻿—and then clean disappeared, leaving him to pay the piper! He was a fool, no doubt, but he’d been mixed up with them; he’d done it before, and they’d always kept their promises, and he did it once too often. He let ’em have some thousands; they disappeared, and the bank inspector happened to call at Brake’s bank and ask for his balances. And﻿—there he was. And﻿—that’s why he’d Falkiner Wraye on his mind﻿—as his one big idea. T’other man was a lesser consideration, Wraye was the chief offender.”

“I wish you’d tell me all you know about Brake,” said Bryce after a pause during which he had done some thinking. “Between ourselves, of course.”

===Chinese===
“他要找一个特定的人——或者不如说，两个人——这两个人曾狠心地欺骗他、坑害他，而其中一个尤其让他恨之入骨，”格拉斯代尔回答，“那一个他认定是在澳大利亚，直到将近最后，他才听说那人已经动身去了英国；至于另一个，他倒不大在意。可他要找的那个人哪！——唉，他想找那人想得发狂！”

“那人是谁？”布赖斯问。

“一个名叫福尔基纳·雷的人，”格拉斯代尔脱口而出，“是他在伦敦认识的旧识。这个雷，连同他的合伙人——一个叫弗拉德（Flood）的人——哄骗布雷克借给他们好几千镑——当然是银行的钱——说是只借一两天——不多借——随后便消失得无影无踪，把他扔下独自背锅！他无疑是个傻瓜，可他早已跟那两人搅在了一起；从前也这么干过，而他们每次都信守了承诺，他便多干了那么一回。他放出去好几千镑；那两人消失了，偏偏银行稽查员又赶巧到了布雷克的分行，要查他的账目余额。于是——事情就败露了。正因如此——福尔基纳·雷才成了他心头挥之不去的那一个念头，成了他唯一的大事。另一个人倒在其次，雷才是首恶。”

“我倒希望您把所知道的关于布雷克的事，统统告诉我，”布赖斯沉吟了一阵——这一阵里他已转了几转念头——才说道，“当然，这事只在咱俩之间。”

===Original===
“Oh﻿—I don’t know that there’s so much secrecy!” replied Glassdale almost indifferently. “Of course, I knew him first when we were both inmates of﻿—you understand where; no need for particulars. But after we left that place, I never saw him again until we met in Australia a few years ago. We were both in the same trade﻿—speculating in wool. We got pretty thick and used to see each other a great deal, and of course, grew confidential. He told me in time about his affair, and how he’d traced this Wraye to the United States, and then, I think, to New Zealand, and afterwards to Australia, and as I was knocking about the country a great deal buying up wool, he asked me to help him, and gave me a description of Wraye, of whom, he said, he’d certainly heard something when he first landed at Sydney, but had never been able to trace afterwards. But it was no good﻿—I never either saw or heard of Wraye﻿—and Brake came to the conclusion he’d left Australia. And I know he hoped to get news of him, somehow, when we returned to England.”

“That description, now?﻿—what was it?” asked Bryce.

“Oh!” said Glassdale. “I can’t remember it all, now﻿—big man, clean shaven, nothing very particular except one thing. Wraye, according to Brake, had a bad scar on his left jaw and had lost the middle finger of his left hand﻿—all from a gun accident. He﻿—what’s the matter, sir?”

===Chinese===
“哦——我倒不觉得有那么多需要保密的！”格拉斯代尔近乎无所谓地答道，“我头一回认识他，是我们俩都待在——您明白那地方；不必细说。可出了那地方之后，我一直没再见过他，直到几年前我们在澳大利亚重逢。我们干的是同一行——做羊毛投机生意。我们交情变得挺厚，常常碰头，自然也就无话不谈了。他后来把他的那桩事告诉了我，说他如何一路追查这个雷，先是追到了美国，然后我记得是新西兰，之后又追到澳大利亚；而我那时为了收购羊毛，在全国四处奔波，他便求我帮忙，把雷的模样形容给我听——他说，他刚到悉尼（Sydney）那阵子，确实听到过一些关于雷的风声，可后来就再也查不出半点下落了。但这都是白费工夫——我既没见过雷，也没听到他的任何消息——布雷克便断定他已经离开了澳大利亚。我知道，我们回到英国之后，他总盼着能从某处打听到那人的消息。”

“那模样，怎样？——他长什么样？”布赖斯问。

“哦！”格拉斯代尔说，“如今我可记不全了——是个大块头，胡子刮得光光的，旁的也没什么特别，除了一处。据布雷克说，雷的左下颌有一道厉害的疤，左手的中指也没了——都是一次走火事故落下的。他——您怎么啦，先生？”

===Original===
Bryce had suddenly let his pipe fall from his lips. He took some time in picking it up. When he raised himself again his face was calm if a little flushed from stooping.

“Bit my pipe on a bad tooth!” he muttered. “I must have that tooth seen to. So you never heard or saw anything of this man?”

“Never!” answered Glassdale. “But I’ve wondered since this Wrychester affair if Brake accidentally came across one or other of those men, and if his death arose out of it. Now, look here, doctor! I read the accounts of the inquest on Brake﻿—I’d have gone to it if I’d dared, but just then I hadn’t made up my mind about seeing the Duke; I didn’t know what to do, so I kept away, and there’s a thing has struck me that I don’t believe the police have ever taken the slightest, notice of.”

“What’s that?” demanded Bryce.

“Why, this!” answered Glassdale. “That man who called himself Dellingham﻿—who came with Brake to the Mitre Hotel at Wrychester﻿—who is he? Where did Brake meet him? Where did he go? Seems to me the police have been strangely negligent about that! According to the accounts I’ve read, everybody just accepted this Dellingham’s first statement, took his word, and let him﻿—vanish! No one, as far as I know, ever verified his account of himself. A stranger!”

===Chinese===
布赖斯猛地让烟斗从唇边滑落了下去。他捡起烟斗，颇费了一点工夫。等他直起身来时，脸色很平静，只是因弯腰而微微发红。

“一口咬在烟斗上，磕着坏牙了！”他嘟囔道，“我得去把这颗牙瞧瞧。这么说，这个人的事，您既没听过也没见过半点？”

“从没有过！”格拉斯代尔答道，“可自从出了赖彻斯特这桩事，我倒一直在想：布雷克会不会是偶然撞见了那两人中的一个，而他的死正是由此而起的？嗳，您听着，医生！我读了布雷克那场验尸聆讯的报道——我本想去旁听的，要是我敢的话，可那会儿我还没拿定主意要不要去见公爵；我不知如何是好，便躲开了。可有一件事我越想越觉得蹊跷——我敢说，警方对那件事从没留意过半分。”

“什么事？”布赖斯追问。

“喏，就是这件事！”格拉斯代尔答道，“那个自称德灵厄姆的人——跟布雷克一道到赖彻斯特米特旅馆落脚的那个人——他如今在哪儿？布雷克在哪儿遇见他的？他又去了哪儿？依我看，警方在这上头疏忽得离奇！据我读到的报道来看，大家伙儿都毫不追问地照单全收了这个德灵厄姆头一回的自陈，信了他的话，便任他——消失了！据我所知，没有一个人核实过他自报的身份。一个生人哪！”

===Original===
Bryce, who was already in one of his deep moods of reflection, got up from his chair as if to go.

“Yes,” he said. “There maybe something in your suggestion. They certainly did take his word without inquiry. It’s true﻿—he mightn’t be what he said he was.”

“Aye, and from what I read, they never followed his movements that morning!” observed Glassdale. “Queer business altogether! Isn’t there some reward offered, doctor? I heard of some placards or something, but I’ve never seen them; of course, I’ve only been here since yesterday morning.”

Bryce silently drew some papers from his pocket. From them he extracted the two handbills which Mitchington had given him and handed them over.

“Well, I must go,” he said. “I shall no doubt see you again in Wrychester, over this affair. For the present, all this is between ourselves, of course?”

===Chinese===
布赖斯本已陷入深深的沉思，此时从椅子上站起身来，像是要走。

“不错，”他说，“您这提醒也许有些道理。他们确实是不加查问就信了他的话。这话不假——他未必就是他自己所说的那个人。”

“可不是嘛，而且据我所读的，那天早上他的行踪，他们也压根儿没去追查过！”格拉斯代尔说道，“整桩事古怪透顶！医生，不是悬了什么赏吗？我听人说起过什么招贴之类的，可我从来没见过；当然啦，我从前天早上才到这儿。”

布赖斯默不作声地从口袋里抽出几张纸。他从中拣出米钦顿给他的那两张招贴，递了过去。

“好，我该走了，”他说，“这事我们多半还会在赖彻斯特再见。眼下嘛，这一切自然只在咱俩之间？”

===Original===
“Oh, of course, doctor!” answered Glassdale. “Quite so!” Bryce went off and got his bicycle and rode away in the direction of Wrychester. Had he remained in that garden he would have seen Glassdale, after reading both the handbills, go into the house and have heard him ask the landlady at the bar to get him a trap and a good horse in it as soon as possible; he, too, now wanted to go to Wrychester and at once. But Bryce was riding down the road, muttering certain words to himself over and over again.

“The left jaw﻿—and the left hand!” he repeated. “Left hand﻿—left jaw! Unmistakable!”

===Chinese===
“哦，那是当然，医生！”格拉斯代尔答道，“那是当然！”布赖斯走开去，取了自行车，朝着赖彻斯特的方向骑走了。倘若他留在那花园里，便能看见格拉斯代尔读完那两张招贴之后，转身进了屋，还能听见他吩咐柜台那位老板娘，尽快给他备一辆轻便马车，车里套一匹好马——他这会儿也急着要赶去赖彻斯特，而且刻不容缓。可布赖斯这会儿正沿着大路骑行，一遍又一遍地喃喃自语着某几个字。

“左下颌——还有左手！”他反复念叨，“左手——左下颌！错不了！”

## XXII — Other People’s Notions / 第二十二章　旁人的看法

===Original===
The great towers of Wrychester Cathedral had come within Bryce’s view before he had made up his mind as to the next step in this last stage of his campaign. He had ridden away from the Saxonsteade Arms feeling that he had got to do something at once, but he was not quite clear in his mind as to what that something exactly was. But now, as he topped a rise in the road, and saw Wrychester lying in its hollow beneath him, the summer sun shining on its red roofs and grey walls, he suddenly came to a decision, and instead of riding straight ahead into the old city he turned off at a byroad, made a line across the northern outskirts, and headed for the golf-links. He was almost certain to find Mary Bewery there at that hour, and he wanted to see her at once. The time for his great stroke had come.

But Mary Bewery was not there—had not been there that morning said the caddy-master. There were only a few players out. In one of them, coming towards the clubhouse, Bryce recognized Sackville Bonham. And at sight of Sackville, Bryce had an inspiration. Mary Bewery would not come up to the links now before afternoon; he, Bryce, would lunch there and then go towards Wrychester to meet her by the path across the fields on which he had waylaid her after his visit to Leicestershire. And meanwhile he would inveigle Sackville Bonham into conversation. Sackville fell readily into Bryce’s trap. He was the sort of youth who loves to talk, especially in a hinting and mysterious fashion. And when Bryce, after treating him to an appetizer in the bar of the clubhouse, had suggested that they should lunch together and got him into a quiet corner of the dining-room, he launched forth at once on the pertinent matter of the day.

===Chinese===
布赖斯眼前映入赖彻斯特大教堂那两座巍峨塔楼之时，他对自己这场战役最后一程的下一步棋尚未拿定主意。他骑车离开萨克森斯蒂德纹章旅馆（the Saxonsteade Arms）时，只觉得非得立刻有所行动不可，可这行动究竟该是什么，他心里还不十分了然。然而此刻，当他骑上路边一道高坡，望见赖彻斯特卧在脚下那片凹地之中，夏日阳光照耀着它的红瓦灰墙，他蓦地拿定了主意——他不直骑行入这座古城，而是从一条岔路转了出去，斜穿过北郊，朝高尔夫球场奔去。这个钟点他十拿九稳能在那儿找到玛丽·比尤里，而他急于立刻见到她。他放手一搏的时机到了。

可是玛丽·比尤里并不在那里——球童主管说，她那天早上根本没来。场上只有寥寥几位球手。其中一位正朝俱乐部会所走来，布赖斯认出是萨克维尔·博纳姆。一见萨克维尔，布赖斯灵机一动。玛丽·比尤里午后之前是不会到球场来的了；他布赖斯不妨在这里用午餐，然后再朝赖彻斯特方向走去，沿着那条穿过田野的小路去迎她——上回他去了一趟莱斯特郡回来，便是在那条路上拦过她。趁着这工夫，他正好把萨克维尔·博纳姆套进话里去。萨克维尔毫不费力便落入了布赖斯的圈套。他正是那种爱说话的年轻人，尤其爱用一种影影绰绰、故作神秘的口吻说话。布赖斯在会所酒吧请他喝了一杯开胃酒，又提议两人共进午餐，把他带进餐厅一个清静的角落，他便马上滔滔不绝地谈起当日最要紧的那桩事来。

===Original===
“Heard all about this discovery of those missing Saxonsteade diamonds?” he asked as he and Bryce picked up their knives and forks. “Queer business that, isn’t it? Of course, it’s got to do with those murders!”

“Think so?” asked Bryce.

“Can anybody think anything else?” said Sackville in his best dogmatic manner. “Why, the thing’s plain. From what’s been let out—not much, certainly, but enough—it’s quite evident.”

“What’s your theory?” inquired Bryce.

“My stepfather—knowing old bird he is, too!—sums the whole thing up to a nicety,” answered Sackville. “That old chap, Braden, you know, is in possession of that secret. He comes to Wrychester about it. But somebody else knows. That somebody gets rid of Braden. Why? So that the secret’ll be known then only to one—the murderer! See! And why? Why?”

“Well, why?” repeated Bryce. “Don’t see, so far.”

===Chinese===
“萨克森斯蒂德那批失踪钻石被找出来的事，听说了吧？”他和布赖斯一道拿起刀叉时问道，“这事可真蹊跷，是不是？当然了，这跟那几桩命案脱不了干系！”

“你这么认为？”布赖斯问。

“旁人还能怎么想呢？”萨克维尔拿出他那副最武断的腔调说道，“嗐，这明摆着嘛。就外头透露出来的——虽说不多，可也够了——事情再清楚不过了。”

“你有什么看法？”布赖斯探问道。

“我继父——他可是只精明的老狐狸！——把整件事概括得一针见血，”萨克维尔回答，“你知道，布雷登那老头，是掌握了那个秘密的。他就是为了这事才到赖彻斯特来的。可另外还有一个人也知道。那个人便除掉了布雷登。为什么？这样一来，那个秘密就只剩下一个人知道了——凶手本人！明白了吧！至于为什么？为什么？”

“嗯，为什么？”布赖斯重复道，“目前为止我还没看出来。”

===Original===
“You must be dense, then,” said Sackville with the lofty superiority of youth. “Because of the reward, of course! Don’t you know that there’s been a standing offer—never withdrawn!—of five thousand pounds for news of those jewels?”

“No, I didn’t,” answered Bryce.

“Fact, sir—pure fact,” continued Sackville. “Now, five thousand, divided in two, is two thousand five hundred each. But five thousand, undivided, is—what?”

“Five thousand—apparently,” said Bryce.

“Just so! And,” remarked Sackville knowingly, “a man’ll do a lot for five thousand.”

===Chinese===
“那你可真是迟钝了，”萨克维尔带着年轻人那种高高在上的优越感说道，“当然是为了那笔赏金啦！你难道不知道，一直有人悬赏——至今从未撤销过！——五千英镑，征求那批珠宝的下落？”

“这我倒不知道，”布赖斯答道。

“事实如此，先生——千真万确的事实，”萨克维尔接着说，“你想想，五千英镑，两个人分，每人两千五百。可要是不分，五千英镑整一份——那是多少？”
